# 7c-1 Implicit Geometry Core Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Add a second geometry world beside OCCT's exact B-rep: implicit solids (signed distance functions) that combine, shell and offset, and watertight manifold triangle meshes made from them, previewed progressively in the viewport and exported to STL. The module is portable: the CPU engine is complete, and the GPU sits behind a protocol.

**Architecture:** A new module `CreatorImplicit` holds the immutable `ImplicitSolid` tree and `TriangleMesh`, a CPU evaluator that is a complete engine on every platform, a generator of Metal source (cached by the tree's shape; numbers go in buffers) and a GPU engine protocol `ImplicitGPUEngine` whose only implementation, `MetalImplicitEngine`, is the one file that imports Metal and is compiled only under `#if canImport(Metal)`, a sparse octree pruned by lipschitz constants, a marching-cubes mesher with global edge keys, and baked distance blocks with a BVH-and-pseudonormal sign. `CreatorGraph` gains the `implicit` and `mesh` socket types and holds the values as protocols (`ImplicitPayload`, `MeshPayload`), because `CreatorImplicit` depends on `CreatorGraph` for `Field`. `CreatorNodes` gets eleven nodes in two new library categories, `CreatorViewport` shows a mesh as a display-only solid, and `CreatorApp` refines the mesh pass by pass, shows the badge, and exports in a sheet.

**Tech Stack:** Swift 6.4 (strict concurrency), Swift Testing, SwiftPM, Metal (runtime-compiled Metal Shading Language, no `.metal` files), MetalUI, OpenCascade through the existing kernel. No third-party libraries.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-implicit-lattices-design.md` (§2 values, §3 nodes, §4 engine, §5 UI and export, §6 performance, §7 testing, §8 build split: the 7c-1 row), under the parent spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-metalcreator-vertical-slice-design.md` with all its Errata, beside `2026-10-10-patterns-trees-design.md` (7a) and `2026-10-12-fields-design.md` (7b). Repo: worktree `MetalCreator-7c-core`, branch `7c-core`, off master `1d60b26` (3,847 tests). Out of scope for this track: lattices, mappings, Drain Holes, Decimate, checks, Mesh to Solid, 3MF, Cut view (7c-2, 7c-3 and 7c-4), meshing on the GPU (Errata D13: the lane 7c-1g, see U5) and the streamed export (Errata D12: the streaming writer is 7c-3).

## Errata applied

The spec's errata of 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-14 (D11 to D13, user decisions) are binding. What each means for this plan:

| Erratum | What the plan does |
|---|---|
| **D11** portability: the CPU evaluator is a complete engine; GPU evaluation sits behind a protocol, compiled only where Metal exists; nothing else imports Metal, simd, CoreGraphics or another Apple-only framework | `ImplicitGPUEngine` (`GPU/ImplicitGPUEngine.swift`: prepare, compile, evaluate a batch, cancel) is the protocol; `MetalImplicitEngine` is its only implementation, in the one file that imports Metal, wrapped in `#if canImport(Metal)`. `GPUBatchEvaluator` adapts any engine to the mesher's `ImplicitBatchEvaluator`; `ImplicitGPUEngines.platformDefault` is the machine's engine or `nil` (the only `canImport` outside the engine); `ImplicitEvaluators` has no `import os` and names no GPU API. `ImplicitPortabilityTests` (Task 8) reads every source of the module and fails on an Apple-only import, on `MTL…` outside the Metal engine, and on a `Metal*.swift` file not wholly under `#if canImport(Metal)`. The agreement suite is under the same guard and skipped without a device. The CPU path is tested on every platform through `StubGPUEngine`, an engine that is the CPU underneath. Not done: building on Linux or Windows (no such toolchain here); the portability test is what stands in for it. |
| **D12** export streams to the file (user, on the old U5) | The 4 GB / 60 s target of i-4 stays. **The streaming writer belongs to 7c-3 (D12 says so), so 7c-1 keeps the in-memory export of Task 15 and i-4 is expected to miss until 7c-3**; the plan records it as a miss and says why in `performance.md`. The "approximate (preview resolution)" labelling of the checks and Decimate is 7c-3's. |
| **D13** meshing on the GPU too (user, on the old U6): protocol `ImplicitGPUEngine` (evaluate bricks, mesh bricks), `MetalImplicitEngine` first, CPU mesher stays the reference, GPU and CPU meshes agree in topology class and within 1e-4 mm after the snap, i-1 and i-2 measured on the GPU path, a lane "7c-1g GPU meshing" after the Metal evaluator task; SDL3 later (track xp-c) | The protocol has its D13 name from the start so nothing is renamed later (D11's first names, `ImplicitGPUEvaluator` and `MetalImplicitEvaluator`, are the ones D13 supersedes). It declares evaluation only; **the mesh-bricks requirement, the Metal marching-cubes kernel, the CPU-against-GPU mesh agreement test and the GPU measurement of i-1 and i-2 are the lane 7c-1g, a separate plan** (user decision U5 below: say if it should be added to this plan instead). Until 7c-1g, meshing runs on the CPU over GPU-evaluated bricks. |

## Spec corrections this plan makes

The spec is binding; where code could not follow it as written, this plan says how it differs, once, here. Module and type names are the spec's as the errata leave them (checked against the spec at `bf5aad7`), so 7c-1g, 7c-2 and 7c-3 can build on them.

1. **Layering.** The spec puts `ImplicitSolid` in `CreatorImplicit`, which depends on `CreatorGraph` for `Field`, and also makes `Scalar.implicit(ImplicitSolid)` and `Field.implicit(ImplicitSolid)` (both in `CreatorGraph`) hold it. That is a cycle. The plan breaks it at the value: `CreatorGraph` declares `protocol ImplicitPayload` (summary, bytes, bounds, lipschitz, `signedDistance(at:)`, equality), `struct ImplicitReference` (an `ImplicitPayload` with value equality, so `Field` stays `Hashable`) and `protocol MeshPayload`; `Scalar.implicit(ImplicitReference)`, `Scalar.mesh(any MeshPayload)` and `Field.implicit(ImplicitReference)` hold them; `ImplicitSolid` and `TriangleMesh` conform; `ImplicitReference.solid` reads the solid back. Nothing else about the module boundary changes.
2. **`converted(to: .mesh)`.** An implicit wired into a mesh socket must be meshed "at default Mesh settings", but meshing needs the run's quality, progress and cancellation, none of which `Scalar.converted(to:)` has. It passes an implicit through unchanged (`SocketType.mesh.accepts(.implicit)` is true, so `connectionProblem` allows the wire), and the node that takes the mesh socket meshes it (`MeshInput`, used by Output).
3. **Names in this codebase.** The spec's `EvaluationContext` is `EvalContext`; `connectionProblem` is `Graph.connectionProblem` over `SocketType.accepts`.
4. **Pictures** go to the shaders in a float buffer, not an `r32Float` texture: the same sampling code on every tree, no argument buffers.
5. **Shared maths moved down.** `SeededRandom`, `FieldNoise` and `ProfileDistance` (with a new `FieldMath` for remap, clamp, minimum, maximum and smooth minimum) move from `CreatorNodes` into `CreatorGeometry` as public API, because the evaluator needs them and `CreatorImplicit` sits below `CreatorNodes`. Their tests stay where they are.
6. **Baked blocks.** The stored band is one block, not four (row i-3's 500 ms), and `DistanceBlocks.lipschitz` is measured from the samples instead of assumed 1. The spacing is `sqrt(16 · tolerance)` (0.4 mm at the default 0.01 mm), which assumes no fillet tighter than 2 mm; Shell and Offset warn when they reach past the band.
7. **Isosurface** is clipped to its region (`max((field - level) / maxSlope, distance to the region box)`), which is what makes its `bounds` true for a field that is negative everywhere. Its region is the field's support when it has one (an implicit read as a field) else the `bounds` input.
8. **Marching cubes table** is generated by a written rule at start-up instead of typed in (`MarchingCubesTable`), so the choices that make the mesh manifold are in the doc comment and under test.
9. **Output and Place.** Output gains a second optional list input `mesh` and a second output; its `solid` input becomes optional. Place gains an optional `implicit` input and `implicits` output; its `tool` becomes optional.
10. **Mesh node's "preview resolution (Auto, or mm)"** is a number where 0 means Auto.
11. **Row i-1 to i-4's gyroid** does not exist until 7c-2, so `ImplicitBenchFixtures` builds a sheet of the same kind from a noise field; the rows' ids and targets are the spec's.
12. **The GPU seams.** The spec's `ImplicitEvaluator.cpu` is `CPUBatchEvaluator`, an `ImplicitBatchEvaluator` (evaluate a batch of points of one solid, the seam the octree and mesher call). `ImplicitGPUEngine` is the device-level protocol of D11 and D13; `GPUBatchEvaluator` binds an engine to one compiled solid. `ImplicitCompiler`, `CompiledImplicit` and `ImplicitShaderLibrary` (in `Metal/`) generate Metal source as text and import nothing, so they build everywhere; an SDL3 engine will need that source translated, which is xp-c's.
13. **Compile errors are logged.** The spec logs a compile error and shows "Running on the CPU". The module cannot log portably (`os` is Apple-only), so `ImplicitEvaluators.Selection` carries the reason in `diagnostic` and the badge shows the warning; nothing logs the diagnostic yet. `ImplicitCompileError.metal` is `.engine`.

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements include these.

- Swift 6 strict concurrency (`swift-tools-version: 6.4`, `swiftLanguageModes: [.v6]`). Data-race errors are compile errors. No `@unchecked Sendable` or `nonisolated(unsafe)` to silence a diagnostic; shared mutable state is an actor, `Mutex` or `@MainActor`.
- Tests use Swift Testing (`import Testing`, `@Test`, `#expect`), never XCTest. Models are `@MainActor @Observable`; behaviour lives in models and views are thin; one type per file (extensions of a type with a small helper type beside them are fine); no force unwraps, no `try!`; no `DispatchQueue`; `Task.sleep(for:)`, never `Task.sleep(nanoseconds:)`; `FormatStyle`, never `String(format:)` or a `Formatter` subclass.
- `swiftlint lint --strict` reports zero violations (lines up to 140 columns, trailing commas in multi-line collection literals). Prefer fixing the code to disabling a rule, and give every disable a reason.
- Numbers in node and kernel messages are written with `Locale.messages` (`Double.millimetreText(locale:)`, `.formatted(...locale(.messages))`).
- **A full `swift test` passes only if** the exit code is 0, no line says "recorded an issue" or "failed after" (Swift Testing uses glyphs, not ✘), and **11 "Test run with" lines appear before Task 2 and 12 from Task 2 on**: Task 2 adds the test target `CreatorImplicitTests`, which prints its own line.
- Tests always use the CPU path: `ImplicitEngineChoice.cpu` is what a context made by hand has. Code that depends on a GPU engine is tested against `StubGPUEngine` on every platform. The GPU agreement suite and the tests that need a Metal device are inside `#if canImport(Metal)` and skipped without a device (`@Suite(.enabled(if: MetalImplicitEngine.shared != nil, ...))`).
- **`CreatorImplicit` is portable (Errata D11).** Nothing in it imports Metal, simd, CoreGraphics, `os` or another Apple-only framework, except `MetalImplicitEngine.swift`, wrapped whole in `#if canImport(Metal)`; no other file names an `MTL…` type. `ImplicitPortabilityTests` enforces it; run it with the module's tests after any change to the GPU files.
- Module names and types from spec §2 to §4 and its errata exactly: `CreatorImplicit`, `ImplicitSolid`, `TriangleMesh`, `ImplicitCompiler`, `DistanceBlocks`, `ImplicitBakeCache`, `ImplicitBench`, `ImplicitGPUEngine` (D13), `MetalImplicitEngine` (D13). `GraphFile.currentFormatVersion` is 8 at the end; files of formats 1 to 7 open unchanged.
- MetalUI gaps are logged in `docs/metalui-gaps.md` (entry and summary row) and never worked around; this plan found none. Never modify `../MetalUI`, never edit the MetalUI path in `Package.swift`, never take screenshots.
- Never `pkill` or `killall` by a broad pattern (`swift-test`, `swift-build`, `xctest`): other sessions run `swift test` on this Mac. Kill only your own processes, by PID or by a pattern containing your own worktree path.
- Run every command from the worktree. Commit messages end with the session's attribution lines.

## Review Focus

Inputs and conditions the spec implies and no task's happy path exercises, most likely to bite first. Each has a test in the task named.

1. **A shape too small to mesh, or an intersection of disjoint shapes** gives an empty mesh. Expected: the Mesh node fails with a sentence ("There is no surface to mesh: the shape is empty, or smaller than a voxel."), never a crash or an empty file. Tests: Task 7 `nothingToMeshIsRefusedWithAMessage`, Task 13 `aShapeTooSmallToMeshIsRefusedWithAMessage`.
2. **An edit while the preview is refining.** Expected: the passes of the replaced mesh stop and none arrives late. Tests: Task 14 `aReplacementMeshStartsOverAndTheOldOneIsDropped`, `anEditStopsThePassesForTheOldShapeAndStartsTheNewOne`.
3. **Cancel in the middle of an export.** Expected: no file is left, no alert, the sheet goes. Tests: Task 15 `cancelStopsTheExportAndLeavesNoFile`, Task 16 `theSheetShowsTheExportAndItsCancelStopsIt`.
4. **A solid that is not closed, or a tessellation with welded-away slivers.** Expected: To Implicit says "This solid isn't closed, so inside and outside are undefined." and bakes nothing. Tests: Task 5 `anOpenMeshIsRefused`, `aFlippedFaceIsRefused`, `aSoupOfPerFaceVerticesGivesTheSameDistances`.
5. **No GPU, or a tree Metal cannot take** (a distance to a profile). Expected: the CPU evaluates it and the node shows "Running on the CPU" only when a GPU engine was available and the compile failed. Tests: Task 9 `aSolidTheGPUCannotDoRunsOnTheCPUWithAWarning`, `noGPUEngineMeansTheCPUAndNoWarning`, Task 8 `aDistanceToAProfileIsLeftToTheCPU`.
6. **A part more than 10 m from the origin.** Expected: a warning about single-precision coordinates, not a silently blurred mesh. Test: Task 13 `aPartFarFromTheOriginWarnsAboutSinglePrecision`.
7. **A request that does not fit in memory** (a 2 m sphere at 0.01 mm). Expected: refused before work starts, with the spec's sentence, naming the Mesh node. Tests: Task 6 `aJobOverTheLimitIsRefusedWithAWayOut`, Task 15 `aMeshTooBigForMemoryIsRefusedWithTheNodeThatSaysSo`.
8. **A blend radius, thickness or offset field that goes negative or has no known slope.** Expected: held at 0 with a warning; unknown slope taken as 10 per mm with "may be slow". Tests: Tasks 10 and 11.
9. **An old file.** Expected: formats 1 to 7 open unchanged and save as 8. Test: Task 17 `aFileOfAnyEarlierFormatOpensUnchangedAndSavesAsEight`.
10. **A platform with no Metal.** Expected: `CreatorImplicit` has no Apple-only import, the CPU is the engine, no warning is shown. Tests: Task 8 `ImplicitPortabilityTests`, Task 9 `noGPUEngineMeansTheCPUAndNoWarning`.
11. **One undo step per edit of the new inputs.** Expected: a Mesh resolution drag is one step named for the input ("Change" and its label); wiring an implicit into Place is one "Connect". Tests: Task 13 `ImplicitUndoTests`.

The spec calls it the lane "7c-1g" after the Metal evaluator task | (a) a separate plan, 7c-1g, branching off 7c-core after it merges (b) extra tasks in this plan, after Task 9, taking it to about 23 tasks (c) after 7c-2 and 7c-3 | (a): the lane is its own deliverable (a kernel, an agreement suite, a re-measurement), 7c-1 then stays reviewable at 19 tasks and merges with a CPU mesher that is the reference anyway, and nothing here is renamed when 7c-1g lands because the protocol already has its D13 name. D12 and D13 are the user's already; this only says how 7c-1 reports. |
| U7 | Strings the spec does not give | the plan's: "There is no surface to mesh: the shape is empty, or smaller than a voxel."; "“blend radius” is below 0 in places; it is held at 0 there."; "“thickness” changes at a rate that isn't known, so the preview may be slow."; "The field can change by X per mm, more than “max slope” (Y); the preview may have holes."; "This part reaches X mm from the origin; beyond 10,000 mm the GPU's single-precision coordinates blur its surface."; "The distance reaches X mm from the surface, past the Y mm To Implicit measured exactly, so it may be inaccurate."; "The part is big for this tolerance, so it was measured every X mm instead of every Y mm; thin features may be lost."; "Exporting “Name”", "Meshing at full resolution", "Cancelling…" | accept, or reword in the copy pass |

## Risks

- **The Metal path is verified on this Mac's GPU only, and the portable build on no other platform.** The agreement suite holds every node and every field kind to 1e-4 mm on 600 fixed points, and is skipped on a machine without Metal. Portability (D11) is enforced by a test of imports, not by a Linux build: `CreatorImplicit` also depends on `CreatorGraph`, `CreatorGeometry` and `CreatorKernel`, whose own portability belongs to the cross-platform track. Single precision near lattice boundaries of noise is the likeliest place for a flaky miss; the points are fixed and no flake was seen.
- **Performance targets i-2, i-3 and i-4 are not met in the first (loaded) run**; see U5 and U6, Errata D12 and D13 and `docs/verification/performance.md`. i-2 and i-4 are expected to miss until GPU meshing (7c-1g) and the streamed export (7c-3). i-3 is the CPU bake of the bracket (a tessellation, a BVH and a few thousand blocks).
- **The Swift 6.4 optimizer crashed** (LLVM coroutine splitting) compiling one async test that mixed `defer`, `try #require` and `guard case` in the release build that `scripts/bench.sh` makes. The plan's tests avoid that shape (`ExportMeshTests.Target` replaces the `defer`s). If a later test hits it again, restructure the test; do not skip the release build.
- **Debug-build test time grows**: `CreatorAppTests` takes about twice as long (mesh tests run the CPU mesher in debug). The GPU is not used in tests.
- **A baked solid is exact only within one block of its surface** and assumes no fillet tighter than 2 mm at the default tolerance; Shell and Offset warn past the band.
- **Memory at 0.1 mm export** is about 150 bytes a triangle (positions, normals, indices, the edge-key map). Meshes of a hundred million triangles are refused by the estimate or run out of memory first; streaming bricks to the file is the fix, decided by the user (D12) and built in 7c-3.
- **`ImplicitGPUEngine` has no meshing yet.** Its D13 name is final; the mesh-bricks requirement arrives with 7c-1g, so an engine written before that (none is) would need it added.
- **New MetalUI gaps: none.** `ProgressView`, `GlassPanel` and `Button` did what was needed.

## File structure

**`Sources/CreatorApp/Export/`**

- `ExportPlan.swift`: What the Output nodes hold, read from the current results before an export asks where to save (implicit spec §5.3).
- `ExportRun.swift`: An export in progress (implicit spec §5.3): what the export sheet shows, a progress bar and Cancel. The export evalua…
- `ExportSheetDock.swift`: Puts the export sheet in the middle of the window while an export runs (`AppModel.exportRun`); nothing when none does…
- `ExportSheetView.swift`: The export sheet (implicit spec §5.3): what is being exported, a bar for how far the meshing has got, and Cancel. All…

**`Sources/CreatorApp/MeshPreview/`**

- `MeshBadgeDock.swift`: Puts the mesh badge at the viewport's lower right, clear of the docked panels (`AppLayout.badgeInsets`). It fills the…
- `MeshBadgeView.swift`: The badge on a mesh preview (implicit spec §5.1): "Preview 0.4 mm", and MetalUI's spinner while the passes are still…
- `MeshRefinementModel.swift`: The progressive preview of meshes (implicit spec §4.3): the evaluator hands the viewport the first, coarse pass of ea…
- `MeshScene.swift`: What `SceneBuilder` needs to show meshes: the solids that display them, and the refined passes that have replaced the…
- `MeshSolids.swift`: The display solid of each mesh the viewport shows (implicit spec §5.1). A mesh is shown as a `Solid` that holds only…
- `TriangleMesh+Display.swift`

**`Sources/CreatorEditor/`**

- `InspectorBuilder+Implicit.swift`

**`Sources/CreatorGeometry/Fields/`**

- `FieldMath.swift`: The scalar maths of the field nodes (fields spec §3.2), shared by `FieldSampler` (CreatorNodes) and the implicit eval…

**`Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/`**

- `Field+Slope.swift`: How fast a field changes (implicit spec §3.3). `value` is in field units per millimetre.

**`Sources/CreatorGraph/Implicit/`**

- `ImplicitEngineChoice.swift`: Which engine evaluates implicit solids (implicit spec §4.1). The evaluator carries it to the Mesh node in `EvalContext`.
- `ImplicitPayload.swift`: What `CreatorGraph` knows of an implicit solid (implicit spec §2.1). The solid itself, `ImplicitSolid`, lives in
- `ImplicitReference.swift`: An implicit solid as a value on a wire (`Scalar.implicit`) and as a field leaf (`Field.implicit`): an `ImplicitPayload`
- `MeshPayload.swift`: What `CreatorGraph` knows of a triangle mesh (implicit spec §2.2): `TriangleMesh` lives in `CreatorImplicit` for the…
- `MeshProgress.swift`: Where a long meshing reports how far it has got (implicit spec §5.3): a fraction from 0 to 1, called from any thread.…
- `MeshQuality.swift`: How finely a Mesh node works (implicit spec §4.3): `preview` meshes at a coarse resolution that the viewport then ref…
- `PayloadFact.swift`: One line the inspector shows about an implicit solid or a mesh (implicit spec §5.2): "Triangles" and "1,234,000".
- `Value+Implicit.swift`

**`Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/`**

- `DistanceBaker.swift`: Bakes a signed distance function onto `DistanceBlocks` (implicit spec §4.4).
- `GradingBaker.swift`: Makes a `Grading` from a field (implicit spec §3.3, §4.1): bakes the field's distances to solids, face sets and edge…
- `ImplicitBakeCache.swift`: Distance blocks baked from solids (implicit spec §4.4), kept so editing a number downstream of To Implicit doesn't ba…
- `SignedDistanceMesh.swift`: Why a mesh can't give inside and outside.
- `TriangleBVH.swift`: A bounding volume hierarchy over triangles (implicit spec §4.4): a binary tree of boxes, split at the median of the t…

- `ImplicitSolid+Evaluation.swift`: An implicit solid travels along wires and sits in field leaves as an `ImplicitPayload` (CreatorGraph cannot name this…

- `STLWriter.swift`: Writes binary STL (implicit spec §5.3): the 80-byte header, the triangle count, and for each triangle its normal, its…

- `MeshNode.swift`: Makes triangles from an implicit solid (implicit spec §3.6, §4.3): marching cubes over the sparse bricks the surface…

---

### Task 1: The implicit and mesh sockets, their values and conversions

Add the two socket types and the two scalar cases, the `Field.implicit` leaf, the two library categories and their colour roles, so every later task has somewhere to put an implicit solid or a mesh. CreatorGraph cannot name `ImplicitSolid` (it will live in CreatorImplicit, which depends on CreatorGraph for `Field`), so a value on a wire is held as a protocol: see "Spec corrections" 1.

**Files:**

- Modify: `Sources/CreatorEditor/Palette.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+Description.swift`
- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+OwnPlane.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `Scalar`, `SocketType`, `Field`, `NodeInputs`, `NodeCategory`, `Palette`, `ThemeColors` as on master.
- Produces (the public declarations this task adds; later tasks use these exact names):

````swift
public protocol ImplicitPayload: Sendable
public func isEqual(to other: any ImplicitPayload) -> Bool
public func hashPayload(into hasher: inout Hasher)
public struct ImplicitReference: Hashable, Sendable
public let payload: any ImplicitPayload
public init(_ payload: any ImplicitPayload)
public static func == (lhs: ImplicitReference, rhs: ImplicitReference) -> Bool
public func hash(into hasher: inout Hasher)
public protocol MeshPayload: Sendable
public enum MeshQuality: String, Sendable, Hashable, Codable
````


**Notes for the implementer:**

- `Scalar.converted(to: .mesh)` returns an implicit unchanged. Meshing is work that needs the run's quality and can be cancelled, so the node that takes a mesh socket does it (Task 13's `MeshInput`).
- The two theme roles are borrowed by nothing else: `implicitHeader` and `meshHeader` colour the category headers, the sockets and the wires. Dracula `#f1fa8c` / `#c5c8de`, Nord `#ebcb8b` / `#d8dee9`, Alucard `#846e15` / `#5a6b73`; the built-in themes' legibility test (text on headers at least 3:1) covers them.

**Create `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Implicit/ImplicitValueTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGraph

/// The implicit and mesh socket types, their values and conversions (implicit spec §2.1 to §2.3).
struct ImplicitValueTests {
    @Test func implicitSocketsAcceptOnlyImplicitsAndAny() {
        for source in SocketType.allCases {
            let expected = source == .implicit || source == .any
            #expect(SocketType.implicit.accepts(source) == expected, "implicit accepts \(source)")
        }
    }

    @Test func aMeshSocketAcceptsMeshesImplicitsAndAny() {
        for source in SocketType.allCases {
            let expected = source == .mesh || source == .implicit || source == .any
            #expect(SocketType.mesh.accepts(source) == expected, "mesh accepts \(source)")
        }
    }

    @Test func aFieldSocketAcceptsAnImplicitAndNothingElseAcceptsAMeshOrAField() {
        #expect(SocketType.field.accepts(.implicit))
        #expect(!SocketType.field.accepts(.mesh))
        for type in SocketType.allCases where type != .any && type != .mesh {
            #expect(!type.accepts(.mesh), "\(type) must not accept a mesh")
        }
        for type in SocketType.allCases where type != .any && type != .field {
            #expect(!type.accepts(.field), "\(type) must not accept a field")
        }
        #expect(!SocketType.solid.accepts(.implicit) && !SocketType.implicit.accepts(.solid) && !SocketType.implicit.accepts(.field))
        #expect(!SocketType.number.accepts(.implicit) && !SocketType.implicit.accepts(.mesh) && !SocketType.mesh.accepts(.solid))
    }

    @Test func theTypesHaveTheirIndefiniteNames() {
        #expect(SocketType.implicit.indefiniteName == "an implicit solid")
        #expect(SocketType.mesh.indefiniteName == "a mesh")
    }

    @Test func scalarsReportTheirTypes() {
        #expect(Scalar.implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference).type == .implicit)
        #expect(Scalar.mesh(StubMesh(triangleCount: 4)).type == .mesh)
    }

    @Test func anImplicitConvertsToAFieldThatIsItsDistance() throws {
        let solid = StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference
        let converted = try #require(Scalar.implicit(solid).converted(to: .field))
        guard case .field(let field) = converted else { Issue.record("not a field"); return }
        #expect(field == .implicit(solid))
        #expect(field.description == "implicit(stub(radius 5.0))")
        #expect(field.isSignedDistance && field.unit == .millimetres && !field.measuresWithKernel)
        #expect(field.children.isEmpty && field.ownPlane == nil)
    }

    @Test func anImplicitPassesToAMeshSocketUnchangedAndConvertsToNothingElse() throws {
        let scalar = Scalar.implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference)
        #expect(scalar.converted(to: .mesh)?.type == .implicit, "the node that takes it meshes it")
        for target in SocketType.allCases where ![.implicit, .field, .mesh, .any].contains(target) {
            #expect(scalar.converted(to: target) == nil, "an implicit is not \(target)")
        }
        #expect(Scalar.mesh(StubMesh(triangleCount: 1)).converted(to: .implicit) == nil)
        #expect(Scalar.mesh(StubMesh(triangleCount: 1)).converted(to: .field) == nil)
        #expect(Value.list([scalar, scalar]).converted(to: .field)?.items.count == 2)
    }

    @Test func implicitLeavesAreEqualWhenTheirSolidsAre() {
        #expect(Field.implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference) == .implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference))
        #expect(Field.implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference) != .implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 6).reference))
        #expect(Set([Field.implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference), .implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference)]).count == 1)
    }

    @Test func sizesFollowTheSpec() {
        let leaf = Field.implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference)
        #expect(leaf.nodeCount == 1 && leaf.estimatedBytes == 64 + 32 + 96)
        #expect(Scalar.implicit(StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference).estimatedBytes == 96)
        #expect(Scalar.mesh(StubMesh(triangleCount: 10)).estimatedBytes == 16 * 30 + 120)
    }

    @Test func nodeInputsReadAnImplicit() throws {
        let solid = StubImplicit(radius: 5).reference
        let inputs = NodeInputs(item: 0, slots: ["shape": .item(.implicit(solid)), "n": .item(.number(1))])
        #expect(try inputs.implicit("shape") == solid)
        #expect(throws: NodeError.typeMismatch("n", expected: .implicit)) { try inputs.implicit("n") }
    }

    @Test func theCategoriesComeAfterFieldsAndBeforeOutput() {
        #expect(NodeCategory.implicit.title == "Implicit" && NodeCategory.mesh.title == "Mesh")
        #expect(NodeCategory.allCases.suffix(4) == [.fields, .implicit, .mesh, .output])
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Support/StubImplicit.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph

/// A sphere at the origin standing in for `ImplicitSolid` (which lives in CreatorImplicit) in the graph's tests.
struct StubImplicit: ImplicitPayload, Hashable {
    var radius: Double

    var summary: String { "stub(radius \(radius))" }
    var estimatedBytes: Int { 96 }
    var payloadBounds: BoundingBox { BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-radius, -radius, -radius), max: Vector3(radius, radius, radius)) }
    var lipschitz: Double { 1 }
    func signedDistance(at point: Vector3) -> Double { point.length - radius }

    var reference: ImplicitReference { ImplicitReference(self) }
}

/// A mesh payload with a fixed size.
struct StubMesh: MeshPayload {
    var triangleCount: Int
    var estimatedBytes: Int { 16 * 3 * triangleCount + 12 * triangleCount }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorNodesTests/Fields/FieldSamplerImplicitTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import Testing
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// An implicit read as a field is its signed distance (implicit spec §2.3), and the maths nodes combine it like any field.
struct FieldSamplerImplicitTests {
    /// A sphere at the origin, standing in for `ImplicitSolid` (CreatorImplicit has its own tests).
    struct Ball: ImplicitPayload, Hashable {
        var radius: Double
        var summary: String { "ball" }
        var estimatedBytes: Int { 96 }
        var payloadBounds: BoundingBox { BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-radius, -radius, -radius), max: Vector3(radius, radius, radius)) }
        var lipschitz: Double { 1 }
        func signedDistance(at point: Vector3) -> Double { point.length - radius }
    }

    @Test func anImplicitLeafSamplesToItsSignedDistance() async throws {
        let field = Field.implicit(ImplicitReference(Ball(radius: 5)))
        let values = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: [.zero, Vector3(5, 0, 0), Vector3(0, 8, 0)], kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(values == [-5, 0, 3])
    }

    @Test func anImplicitLeafCombinesWithTheMaths() async throws {
        let ball = Field.implicit(ImplicitReference(Ball(radius: 5)))
        let field = Field.remap(ball, fromLow: -5, fromHigh: 5, toLow: 0, toHigh: 1, clamped: true)
        let values = try await FieldSampler.sample(field, at: [.zero, Vector3(10, 0, 0)], kernel: FakeKernel())
        #expect(values == [0, 1])
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorGraph/Implicit/ImplicitPayload.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// What `CreatorGraph` knows of an implicit solid (implicit spec §2.1). The solid itself, `ImplicitSolid`, lives in
/// `CreatorImplicit`, which depends on this module for `Field`; a value that flows along a wire, or sits in a field leaf,
/// can't name it, so it is held as this protocol and read back with `ImplicitReference.solid` in `CreatorImplicit`.
public protocol ImplicitPayload: Sendable {
    /// A one-line recipe such as `sphere(radius 10 mm)`, like `Field.description` (implicit spec §2.1).
    var summary: String { get }
    /// 64 plus 32 per node, plus any baked distance blocks (implicit spec §2.1).
    var estimatedBytes: Int { get }
    /// A box that contains the surface.
    var payloadBounds: BoundingBox { get }
    /// A constant L with |f(a) − f(b)| ≤ L · |a − b| for all a and b; at least 1.
    var lipschitz: Double { get }
    /// The signed distance in millimetres at `point`: negative inside, 0 on the surface (the reference evaluation, in Double).
    func signedDistance(at point: Vector3) -> Double
    /// Whether `other` is the same solid (the previews ask "is this what I last sampled?").
    func isEqual(to other: any ImplicitPayload) -> Bool
    func hashPayload(into hasher: inout Hasher)
}

extension ImplicitPayload where Self: Hashable {
    public func isEqual(to other: any ImplicitPayload) -> Bool { (other as? Self) == self }
    public func hashPayload(into hasher: inout Hasher) { hash(into: &hasher) }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorGraph/Implicit/ImplicitReference.swift`**

````swift
/// An implicit solid as a value on a wire (`Scalar.implicit`) and as a field leaf (`Field.implicit`): an `ImplicitPayload`
/// with value equality, so `Field` stays `Hashable`.
public struct ImplicitReference: Hashable, Sendable {
    public let payload: any ImplicitPayload

    public init(_ payload: any ImplicitPayload) {
        self.payload = payload
    }

    public static func == (lhs: ImplicitReference, rhs: ImplicitReference) -> Bool { lhs.payload.isEqual(to: rhs.payload) }

    public func hash(into hasher: inout Hasher) { payload.hashPayload(into: &hasher) }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorGraph/Implicit/MeshPayload.swift`**

````swift
/// What `CreatorGraph` knows of a triangle mesh (implicit spec §2.2): `TriangleMesh` lives in `CreatorImplicit` for the same
/// reason `ImplicitSolid` does (`ImplicitPayload`).
public protocol MeshPayload: Sendable {
    var triangleCount: Int { get }
    /// 16 per vertex and 12 per triangle (implicit spec §2.2).
    var estimatedBytes: Int { get }
}
````

### Task 2: The CreatorImplicit module and its value types

Create the module and the new test target, and the immutable values everything else computes: `ImplicitSolid` with its bounds, lipschitz constant, exact reach and recipe; `Grading`; `RigidMove`; `DistanceBlocks`; `TriangleMesh` with `MeshTopology`. No evaluation yet. From this task a full `swift test` prints 12 "Test run with" lines instead of 11 (the new `CreatorImplicitTests` target).

- Produces (the public declarations this task adds; later tasks use these exact names):



**Notes for the implementer:**

- `ImplicitSolid` is an `indirect enum`; every `switch` over it has no `default`, so a case 7c-2 adds (a lattice) is a compile error in `children`, `bounds`, `lipschitz`, `exactReach`, `description`, `gradings`, the CPU evaluator and the compiler until each says what it is.
- A smooth blend raises the lipschitz constant by a quarter of the blend radius's slope; an offset or a shell multiplies by one plus its distance's slope (the spec's rule). `DistanceBlocks.lipschitz` is computed from the samples (the trilinear gradient is largest at a corner of a cell, so the bound is the largest vector of sample differences over the cells near the surface), because a ball's distance read through a coarse grid really does exceed 1.
- `exactReach` is the plan's addition: how far from the surface the value is the true distance. Task 11's Shell and Offset warn when they reach past it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/DistanceBlocksTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct DistanceBlocksTests {
    @Test func aLinearFieldIsReproducedExactlyInsideTheGrid() {
        let blocks = BlocksFixture.make(origin: Vector3(-4, -4, -4), spacing: 0.5, blocks: 2) { 2 * $0.x - $0.y + 3 }
        for point in [Vector3(-3.2, 0.1, 1), Vector3(0.7, 2.2, -2.9), Vector3(2.4, -3.3, 3.1)] {
            #expect(abs(blocks.value(at: point) - (2 * point.x - point.y + 3)) < 1e-5, "at \(point)")
        }
    }

    @Test func outsideTheGridTheValueGrowsWithTheDistanceFromIt() {
        let blocks = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1) { _ in 5 }
        // Samples span 0 ... 7 on each axis.
        #expect(blocks.bounds == BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(7, 7, 7)))
        #expect(abs(blocks.value(at: Vector3(10, 0, 0)) - 8) < 1e-9)
        #expect(abs(blocks.value(at: Vector3(10, 11, 7)) - (5 + 5.0.squareRoot() * 0 + (3 * 3 + 4 * 4).squareRoot())) < 1e-9)
    }

    @Test func aBlockThatIsNotStoredReadsItsBound() {
        let size = DistanceBlocks.blockSize
        var pool = [Float](repeating: 1, count: size * size * size)
        pool[0] = 1
        // Two blocks along x: the first stored, the second not (bound 9).
        let blocks = DistanceBlocks(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blockCounts: [2, 1, 1], table: [0, -1], pool: pool, coarse: [0, 9], band: 3)
        #expect(blocks.storedBlockCount == 1)
        #expect(abs(blocks.value(at: Vector3(3, 3, 3)) - 1) < 1e-9)
        #expect(abs(blocks.value(at: Vector3(12, 3, 3)) - 9) < 1e-9)
        // Between the last stored sample (x = 7) and the first of the unstored block (x = 8) it blends.
        #expect(abs(blocks.value(at: Vector3(7.5, 3, 3)) - 5) < 1e-9)
    }

    @Test func sizesAndEquality() {
        let a = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1) { $0.x }
        let b = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1) { $0.x }
        #expect(a == a && a != b)
        #expect(a.estimatedBytes == 512 * 4 + 8 + 128 && a.blockLength == 8)
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitSolidStructureTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// Bounds, lipschitz constants, reach, sizes and recipes of the solid tree (implicit spec §2.1, §3.3).
struct ImplicitSolidStructureTests {
    let sphere = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)
    let cube = ImplicitSolid.box(halfSize: Vector3(5, 5, 5), cornerRadius: 0)

    func slopeGrading(_ value: Double, slope: Double) -> Grading {
        Grading(field: .linearGradient(plane: .xy, length: 1 / slope), range: value...value, slope: slope)
    }

    @Test func primitivesHaveTheirBoxes() {
        #expect(sphere.bounds == BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-10, -10, -10), max: Vector3(10, 10, 10)))
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.box(halfSize: Vector3(1, 2, 3), cornerRadius: 0.5).bounds
            == BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-1, -2, -3), max: Vector3(1, 2, 3)))
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.cylinder(radius: 4, halfHeight: 6).bounds == BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-4, -4, -6), max: Vector3(4, 4, 6)))
    }

    @Test func aMoveBoundsTheMovedBox() throws {
        let move = try #require(RigidMove(plane: Plane(origin: Vector3(100, 0, 0), normal: .unitX, xAxis: .unitY)))
        let bounds = ImplicitSolid.moved(cube, move).bounds
        // +Z goes to +X, so the box keeps its size and is centred on the plane's origin.
        #expect((bounds.center - Vector3(100, 0, 0)).length < 1e-9)
        #expect((bounds.size - Vector3(10, 10, 10)).length < 1e-9)
    }

    @Test func combinersBoundTheirChildren() {
        let shifted = ImplicitSolid.moved(cube, RigidMove(origin: Vector3(20, 0, 0), xAxis: .unitX, yAxis: .unitY, zAxis: .unitZ))
        let union = ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, shifted], blend: .constant(0))
        #expect(union.bounds == BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-10, -10, -10), max: Vector3(25, 10, 10)))
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.subtract([sphere, shifted], blend: .constant(0)).bounds == sphere.bounds)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.intersect([sphere, cube], blend: .constant(0)).bounds == cube.bounds)
        let apart = ImplicitSolid.intersect([sphere, shifted], blend: .constant(0)).bounds
        #expect(apart.size.length == 0, "no overlap: a point")
    }

    @Test func aSmoothUnionBulgesAQuarterOfItsBlendRadius() {
        let union = ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, cube], blend: .constant(4))
        #expect(union.bounds == BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-11, -11, -11), max: Vector3(11, 11, 11)))
    }

    @Test func offsetsAndShellsGrowByTheirLargestDistance() {
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.offset(sphere, distance: .constant(3)).bounds.max.x == 13)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.offset(sphere, distance: .constant(-3)).bounds.max.x == 10, "shrinking never widens")
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.shell(sphere, thickness: .constant(2), side: .inward).bounds.max.x == 10)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.shell(sphere, thickness: .constant(2), side: .outward).bounds.max.x == 12)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.shell(sphere, thickness: .constant(2), side: .centred).bounds.max.x == 11)
    }

    @Test func lipschitzIsOneForExactNodesAndGrowsWithSlopes() {
        #expect(sphere.lipschitz == 1 && cube.lipschitz == 1)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, cube], blend: .constant(2)).lipschitz == 1)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, cube], blend: slopeGrading(2, slope: 0.4)).lipschitz == 1.1)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.offset(sphere, distance: slopeGrading(1, slope: 0.5)).lipschitz == 1.5)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.shell(sphere, thickness: slopeGrading(1, slope: 2), side: .centred).lipschitz == 3)
        let region = BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-1, -1, -1), max: Vector3(1, 1, 1))
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.isosurface(slopeGrading(0, slope: 4), level: 0, maxSlope: 2, region: region).lipschitz == 2)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.isosurface(slopeGrading(0, slope: 4), level: 0, maxSlope: 8, region: region).lipschitz == 1)
    }

    @Test func aBakedDistanceIsExactOnlyWithinItsBand() {
        let blocks = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1, band: 6) { $0.x - 4 }
        let baked = ImplicitSolid.baked(blocks, label: "Plate")
        #expect(baked.exactReach == 6)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.offset(baked, distance: .constant(2)).exactReach == 4)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.shell(baked, thickness: .constant(9), side: .inward).exactReach == 0)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, baked], blend: .constant(0)).exactReach == 6)
        #expect(sphere.exactReach == .infinity)
    }

    @Test func sizesAreSixtyFourPlusThirtyTwoPerNode() {
        #expect(sphere.nodeCount == 1 && sphere.estimatedBytes == 96)
        let tree = ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, cube], blend: .constant(0))
        #expect(tree.nodeCount == 3)
        // Plus the blend's field (64 + 32).
        #expect(tree.estimatedBytes == 64 + 32 * 3 + Grading.constant(0).estimatedBytes)
    }

    @Test func recipesReadLikeFieldRecipes() {
        #expect(sphere.description == "sphere(radius 10 mm)")
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.box(halfSize: Vector3(10, 5, 2.5), cornerRadius: 1).description
            == "box(20 × 10 × 5 mm, corner radius 1 mm)")
        #expect(cube.description == "box(10 × 10 × 10 mm)")
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.cylinder(radius: 5, halfHeight: 6).description == "cylinder(radius 5 mm, height 12 mm)")
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, cube], blend: .constant(0)).description == "union(sphere(radius 10 mm), box(10 × 10 × 10 mm))")
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.subtract([sphere, cube], blend: .constant(2)).description
            == "subtract(sphere(radius 10 mm), box(10 × 10 × 10 mm), blend 2 mm)")
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.offset(sphere, distance: .constant(-2)).description == "offset(sphere(radius 10 mm), −2 mm)")
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.shell(sphere, thickness: .constant(1.5), side: .inward).description
            == "shell(sphere(radius 10 mm), inward 1.5 mm)")
        let blocks = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1) { $0.x }
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.baked(blocks, label: "Bracket").description == "implicit(Bracket)")
        let graded = Grading(field: .noise(seed: 1, scale: 5, octaves: 2), range: 0...1, slope: 1)
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.offset(sphere, distance: graded).description.contains("noise(seed 1, scale 5, octaves 2)"))
    }

    @Test func aLongRecipeIsCutForATooltip() {
        let long = ImplicitSolid.union([sphere, sphere, sphere, sphere, sphere], blend: .constant(0))
        let text = long.description(limit: 80)
        #expect(text.count == 80 && text.hasSuffix("…"))
        #expect(sphere.description(limit: 80) == sphere.description)
    }

    @Test func equalSolidsAreEqual() {
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 3) == .sphere(radius: 3))
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 3) != .sphere(radius: 4))
        let blocks = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1) { $0.x }
        let other = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1) { $0.x }
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.baked(blocks, label: "a") == .baked(blocks, label: "a"))
        #expect(ImplicitSolid.baked(blocks, label: "a") != .baked(other, label: "a"), "blocks are equal by identity")
    }
}
````

````swift
/// Small closed meshes for the mesh tests.
enum MeshFixtures {
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/MeshTopologyTests.swift`**

````swift
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct MeshTopologyTests {
    @Test func aTetrahedronIsAClosedSphere() {
        let topology = MeshTopology(MeshFixtures.tetrahedron)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold)
        #expect(topology.usedVertexCount == 4 && topology.edgeCount == 6 && topology.triangleCount == 4)
        #expect(topology.eulerCharacteristic == 2)
    }

    @Test func aCubeIsAClosedSphere() {
        let topology = MeshTopology(MeshFixtures.cube)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold && topology.eulerCharacteristic == 2)
    }

    @Test func aMissingFaceLeavesOpenEdges() {
        var mesh = MeshFixtures.tetrahedron
        mesh.triangles.removeLast(3)
        let topology = MeshTopology(mesh)
        #expect(!topology.isClosedManifold && topology.openEdgeCount == 3)
    }

    @Test func aFlippedFaceIsInconsistent() {
        var mesh = MeshFixtures.tetrahedron
        mesh.triangles.swapAt(9, 10)
        let topology = MeshTopology(mesh)
        #expect(!topology.isClosedManifold && topology.inconsistentEdgeCount == 3)
    }

    @Test func aThirdFaceOnAnEdgeIsOverfull() {
        var mesh = MeshFixtures.tetrahedron
        mesh.positions.append(SIMD3<Float>(5, 5, 5))
        mesh.triangles += [0, 1, 4]
        #expect(MeshTopology(mesh).overfullEdgeCount >= 1)
        #expect(!MeshTopology(mesh).isClosedManifold)
    }

    @Test func aDegenerateTriangleIsCounted() {
        var mesh = MeshFixtures.tetrahedron
        mesh.triangles += [0, 0, 1]
        #expect(MeshTopology(mesh).degenerateTriangleCount == 1 && !MeshTopology(mesh).isClosedManifold)
    }

    @Test func twoCubesTouchingAtACornerShareANonManifoldVertex() {
        var positions = MeshFixtures.cube.positions
        var triangles = MeshFixtures.cube.triangles
        // A second cube whose corner (0, 0, 0) is the first cube's corner (1, 1, 1): they share vertex 6.
        let shift = SIMD3<Float>(1, 1, 1)
        let base = UInt32(positions.count)
        for (index, position) in MeshFixtures.cube.positions.enumerated() {
            positions.append(index == 0 ? positions[6] : position + shift)
        }
        triangles += MeshFixtures.cube.triangles.map { $0 == 0 ? 6 : $0 + base }
        let topology = MeshTopology(TriangleMesh(positions: positions, triangles: triangles))
        #expect(topology.openEdgeCount == 0 && topology.nonManifoldVertexCount == 1 && !topology.isClosedManifold)
    }
}
````

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct RigidMoveTests {
    @Test func theMovePutsTheLocalFrameOnThePlane() throws {
        let plane = Plane(origin: Vector3(1, 2, 3), normal: .unitX, xAxis: .unitY)
        let move = try #require(RigidMove(plane: plane))
        #expect((move.toWorld(.zero) - Vector3(1, 2, 3)).length < 1e-12)
        #expect((move.toWorld(.unitZ) - Vector3(2, 2, 3)).length < 1e-12, "local +Z goes along the normal")
        #expect((move.toWorld(.unitX) - Vector3(1, 3, 3)).length < 1e-12, "local +X goes along the plane's x axis")
    }

    @Test func toLocalUndoesToWorld() throws {
        let move = try #require(RigidMove(plane: Plane(origin: Vector3(5, -3, 2), normal: Vector3(1, 1, 1), xAxis: Vector3(1, -1, 0))))
        for point in [Vector3.zero, Vector3(1, 2, 3), Vector3(-7, 4, 0.5)] {
            #expect((move.toLocal(move.toWorld(point)) - point).length < 1e-12)
        }
    }

    @Test func anXAxisOffTheNormalIsMadePerpendicular() throws {
        let move = try #require(RigidMove(plane: Plane(origin: .zero, normal: .unitZ, xAxis: Vector3(1, 0, 0.5))))
        #expect(abs(move.xAxis.dot(move.zAxis)) < 1e-12 && abs(move.xAxis.length - 1) < 1e-12)
    }

    @Test func aPlaneWithoutADirectionHasNoMove() {
        #expect(RigidMove(plane: Plane(origin: .zero, normal: .zero, xAxis: .unitX)) == nil)
        #expect(RigidMove(plane: Plane(origin: .zero, normal: .unitZ, xAxis: .unitZ)) == nil)
        #expect(RigidMove(plane: Plane(origin: Vector3(.nan, 0, 0), normal: .unitZ, xAxis: .unitX)) == nil)
    }

    @Test func theBoundsOfAMovedBoxContainItsCorners() throws {
        let move = try #require(RigidMove(plane: Plane(origin: .zero, normal: .unitZ, xAxis: Vector3(1, 1, 0))))
        let box = BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-1, -1, -1), max: Vector3(1, 1, 1))
        let moved = move.bounds(of: box)
        #expect(abs(moved.max.x - 2.0.squareRoot()) < 1e-12 && abs(moved.max.z - 1) < 1e-12)
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/BlocksFixture.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorImplicit

/// Distance blocks filled from a function at every sample, for tests (the real baker is `DistanceBaker`).
enum BlocksFixture {
    /// `blocks` blocks on each axis, with sample (0, 0, 0) at `origin`.
    static func make(origin: Vector3, spacing: Double, blocks: Int, band: Double = 1000,
                     _ distance: (Vector3) -> Double) -> DistanceBlocks {
        let size = DistanceBlocks.blockSize
        let samples = blocks * size
        var pool: [Float] = []
        pool.reserveCapacity(blocks * blocks * blocks * size * size * size)
        for bz in 0..<blocks {
            for by in 0..<blocks {
                for bx in 0..<blocks {
                    for z in 0..<size {
                        for y in 0..<size {
                            for x in 0..<size {
                                let index = Vector3(Double(bx * size + x), Double(by * size + y), Double(bz * size + z))
                                pool.append(Float(distance(origin + index * spacing)))
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        _ = samples
        let count = blocks * blocks * blocks
        return DistanceBlocks(origin: origin, spacing: spacing, blockCounts: [blocks, blocks, blocks],
                              table: (0..<count).map(Int32.init), pool: pool, coarse: [Float](repeating: 0, count: count), band: band)
    }
}
````

````swift
/// Triangles a check flagged, and what it says about them (implicit spec §2.2). The viewport tints them in the theme's warning role.
public struct MeshHighlight: Hashable, Sendable {
    /// Indices of the flagged triangles (triangle `t` is `triangles[3t ..< 3t + 3]`).
    public var triangles: [Int]
    public var label: String

    public init(triangles: [Int], label: String) {
        self.triangles = triangles
        self.label = label
    }
}
````

````swift
/// Where a preview mesh came from, so the viewport can refine it (implicit spec §4.3): the implicit it meshes and the resolution the
/// refinement works towards. A mesh made at `.export` quality has none.
public struct MeshSource: Sendable {
    public var solid: ImplicitSolid
    /// The voxel size the last refinement pass meshes at, in millimetres.
    public var targetResolution: Double

    public init(solid: ImplicitSolid, targetResolution: Double) {
        self.solid = solid
        self.targetResolution = targetResolution
    }
}
````
