**Create `Sources/CreatorGraph/Field/Field+Slope.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation

/// How fast a field changes (implicit spec §3.3). `value` is in field units per millimetre.
public struct FieldSlope: Hashable, Sendable {
    public var value: Double
    /// False when some part of the field has no bound, and `value` is `Field.unknownSlope` or more.
    public var isKnown: Bool

    public init(value: Double, isKnown: Bool = true) {
        self.value = value
        self.isKnown = isKnown
    }
}

extension Field {
    /// What an unknown bound is taken as, per millimetre, and what the node that used it warns about ("the preview may be slow").
    public static let unknownSlope = 10.0

    /// A conservative bound on |f(a) − f(b)| ÷ |a − b|: analytic for distance, linear and radial sources, a declared bound for noise
    /// (`FieldNoise.unitSlope`), finite differences over the pixels of an image, the bound of an implicit solid for an implicit, and
    /// the usual rules for the maths: sums add, a product weighs each side's slope by the other's largest value, a remap scales by its
    /// range ratio, an extremum or a clamp keeps the larger. A product with a side of unknown range, or a part with no bound, makes
    /// the whole bound unknown, and then it is at least `unknownSlope`.
    public var slopeBound: FieldSlope {
        let bound = slope
        return bound.known ? FieldSlope(value: bound.value) : FieldSlope(value: max(bound.value, Self.unknownSlope), isKnown: false)
    }

    /// Every value the field can take, or `nil` when that is unbounded or unknown. A distance, a gradient or a product of such has no
    /// range; noise and images are in 0…1.
    public var valueRange: ClosedRange<Double>? {
        switch self {
        case .constant(let value):
            return value...value
        case .distance, .linearGradient, .radialGradient, .implicit:
            return nil
        case .noise, .image:
            return 0...1
        case .add(let a, let b):
            guard let x = a.valueRange, let y = b.valueRange else { return nil }
            return (x.lowerBound + y.lowerBound)...(x.upperBound + y.upperBound)
        case .multiply(let a, let b):
            guard let x = a.valueRange, let y = b.valueRange else { return nil }
            let products = [x.lowerBound * y.lowerBound, x.lowerBound * y.upperBound, x.upperBound * y.lowerBound,
                            x.upperBound * y.upperBound,
            ]
            guard let low = products.min(), let high = products.max() else { return nil }
            return low...high
        case .minimum(let a, let b):
            guard let x = a.valueRange, let y = b.valueRange else { return nil }
            return min(x.lowerBound, y.lowerBound)...min(x.upperBound, y.upperBound)
        case .maximum(let a, let b):
            guard let x = a.valueRange, let y = b.valueRange else { return nil }
            return max(x.lowerBound, y.lowerBound)...max(x.upperBound, y.upperBound)
        case .smoothMinimum(let a, let b, let width):
            guard let x = a.valueRange, let y = b.valueRange else { return nil }
            return (min(x.lowerBound, y.lowerBound) - max(width, 0) / 4)...min(x.upperBound, y.upperBound)
        case .remap(let inner, let fromLow, let fromHigh, let toLow, let toHigh, let clamped):
            if clamped { return min(toLow, toHigh)...max(toLow, toHigh) }
            guard let range = inner.valueRange, fromHigh != fromLow else { return fromHigh == fromLow ? toLow...toLow : nil }
            let ends = [range.lowerBound, range.upperBound].map { toLow + ($0 - fromLow) / (fromHigh - fromLow) * (toHigh - toLow) }
            guard let low = ends.min(), let high = ends.max() else { return nil }
            return low...high
        case .clamp(_, let low, let high):
            return low...high
        }
    }

    // The slope with a flag for "some part had no bound". One branch per case, with no `default`.
    private var slope: (value: Double, known: Bool) {
        switch self {
        case .constant:
            return (0, true)
        case .distance:
            return (1, true)
        case .linearGradient(_, let length):
            return (1 / abs(length), true)
        case .radialGradient(_, let radius):
            return (1 / abs(radius), true)
        case .noise(_, let scale, let octaves):
            return (FieldNoise.slopeBound(scale: scale, octaves: octaves), true)
        case .image(let image, _, let width, let height, let edges):
            return (image.slopeBound(width: width, height: height, edges: edges), true)
        case .implicit(let solid):
            return (solid.payload.lipschitz, true)
        case .add(let a, let b):
            let (x, y) = (a.slope, b.slope)
            return (x.value + y.value, x.known && y.known)
        case .multiply(let a, let b):
            let (x, y) = (a.slope, b.slope)
            guard let rangeA = a.valueRange, let rangeB = b.valueRange else { return (x.value + y.value, false) }
            let (largestA, largestB) = (max(abs(rangeA.lowerBound), abs(rangeA.upperBound)),
                                        max(abs(rangeB.lowerBound), abs(rangeB.upperBound)))
            return (largestB * x.value + largestA * y.value, x.known && y.known)
        case .minimum(let a, let b), .maximum(let a, let b), .smoothMinimum(let a, let b, _):
            let (x, y) = (a.slope, b.slope)
            return (max(x.value, y.value), x.known && y.known)
        case .remap(let inner, let fromLow, let fromHigh, let toLow, let toHigh, _):
            let slope = inner.slope
            guard fromHigh != fromLow else { return (0, true) }
            return (slope.value * abs((toHigh - toLow) / (fromHigh - fromLow)), slope.known)
        case .clamp(let inner, _, _):
            return inner.slope
        }
    }
}

extension GreyImage {
    /// A bound on how fast the picture's grey changes over the field's rectangle, in value per millimetre: bilinear interpolation
    /// has each gradient component at most the largest difference of neighbouring pixels along that axis over the pixel size, so the
    /// bound is the length of the two. A tiling picture also counts the step from its last pixel back to its first.
    func slopeBound(width: Double, height: Double, edges: ImageEdges) -> Double {
        var across: UInt16 = 0, down: UInt16 = 0
        for row in 0..<self.height {
            for column in 0..<self.width {
                let here = pixels[row * self.width + column]
                let right = column + 1 < self.width ? pixels[row * self.width + column + 1]
                    : (edges == .tile ? pixels[row * self.width] : here)
                let below = row + 1 < self.height ? pixels[(row + 1) * self.width + column]
                    : (edges == .tile ? pixels[column] : here)
                across = max(across, here > right ? here - right : right - here)
                down = max(down, here > below ? here - below : below - here)
            }
        }
        let perMillimetreX = Double(across) / 65_535 / (abs(width) / Double(self.width))
        let perMillimetreY = Double(down) / 65_535 / (abs(height) / Double(self.height))
        return (perMillimetreX * perMillimetreX + perMillimetreY * perMillimetreY).squareRoot()
    }
}
````

### Task 5: Baked distances: the BVH, the pseudonormal sign, the baker, the cache

Turn a closed triangle mesh into `DistanceBlocks` (spec §4.4): weld OCCT's per-face vertices, refuse a mesh with an open edge, find the nearest triangle with a BVH, take the sign from the angle-weighted pseudonormal, sample sparse 8³ blocks in parallel, cache the result (`ImplicitBakeCache`, an actor), and bake the kernel distances inside a field setting (`GradingBaker`).

**Files:**

- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/DistanceBaker.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/GradingBaker.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/ImplicitBakeCache.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/SignedDistanceMesh.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/TriangleBVH.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/DistanceBakerTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/GradingBakerTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitBakeCacheTests.swift`
- Modify: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/MeshFixtures.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/SignedDistanceMeshTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `Kernel.tessellate`, `Kernel.distances`, `FieldDistanceCache`, `DistanceTarget.Identity` on master; `DistanceBlocks`, `Grading` (Task 2); `CPUEvaluation` (Task 3); `Field.slopeBound`, `Field.valueRange` (Task 4).
- Produces (the public declarations this task adds; later tasks use these exact names):

````swift
public enum DistanceBaker
public struct Result: Sendable
public var blocks: DistanceBlocks
public var spacing: Double
public var seconds: Double
public static let maximumGridBlocks = 4_000_000
public static let maximumStoredBlocks = 40_000
public static func spacing(forTolerance tolerance: Double) -> Double
public static func bake(bounds: BoundingBox, spacing requested: Double, bandBlocks: Double = 1,
public enum GradingBaker
public static let cellsAcross = 40
public static func grading(for field: Field, over region: BoundingBox, kernel: any Kernel,
public actor ImplicitBakeCache
public struct Baked: Sendable
public var blocks: DistanceBlocks
public var spacing: Double
public var seconds: Double
public var tolerance: Double
public static let shared = ImplicitBakeCache()
public let capacity: Int
public private(set) var bakeCount = 0
public init(capacity: Int = 512 * 1024 * 1024)
public var entryCount: Int
public var totalBytes: Int
public func baked(for solid: Solid, tolerance: Double, kernel: any Kernel) async throws -> Baked
public enum MeshDistanceError: Error, Equatable, Sendable
public var userMessage: String
public struct SignedDistanceMesh: Sendable
public let bounds: BoundingBox
public init(positions: [Vector3], indices: [UInt32], weldTolerance: Double = 1e-6) throws(MeshDistanceError)
public var triangleCount: Int
public func signedDistance(at point: Vector3) -> Double
````


**Notes for the implementer:**

- The baker's spacing is `sqrt(16 * tolerance)`: 0.4 mm for the default 0.01 mm. Trilinear interpolation of a distance field is off by about spacing² / (8 R) where the surface curves with radius R, so this assumes no fillet tighter than 2 mm; a tighter one shows an error up to that. The spacing grows by 26% steps until the grid has at most 4,000,000 blocks and stores at most 40,000.
- The band stored is one block (not the spec's four): the bracket's bake must fit 500 ms (row i-3). `ImplicitSolid.exactReach` is the band, and Shell and Offset warn past it.
- `SignedDistanceMesh` welds vertices on a grid of 1 µm, drops triangles with no area, and throws `MeshDistanceError.notClosed` unless every edge has two faces running opposite ways. That is exactly the spec's message "This solid isn't closed, so inside and outside are undefined."

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/DistanceBakerTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct DistanceBakerTests {
    static let ball: @Sendable (Vector3) -> Double = { $0.length - 10 }
    static let box = BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-10, -10, -10), max: Vector3(10, 10, 10))

    @Test func theBakeReproducesTheFunctionNearTheSurface() async throws {
        let result = try await DistanceBaker.bake(bounds: Self.box, spacing: 0.5, distance: Self.ball)
        let random = SeededRandom(seed: 3, stream: 1)
        var checked = 0
        for index in 0..<2000 {
            let point = Vector3(random.unit(at: 3 * index) * 24 - 12, random.unit(at: 3 * index + 1) * 24 - 12,
                                random.unit(at: 3 * index + 2) * 24 - 12)
            guard abs(Self.ball(point)) < 3 else { continue }
            checked += 1
            // Trilinear error of a ball of radius 10 at 0.5 mm spacing is about 0.5² / 80 = 0.003.
            #expect(abs(result.blocks.value(at: point) - Self.ball(point)) < 0.01, "at \(point)")
        }
        #expect(checked > 200)
    }

    @Test func onlyBlocksNearTheSurfaceAreStored() async throws {
        let result = try await DistanceBaker.bake(bounds: Self.box, spacing: 0.25, distance: Self.ball)
        let blocks = result.blocks
        let total = blocks.blockCounts.reduce(1, *)
        #expect(blocks.storedBlockCount > 0 && blocks.storedBlockCount < total / 2)
        // A far corner of the grid and the middle of the ball read a sign and a lower bound that is not above the truth.
        let far = blocks.bounds.max - Vector3(0.1, 0.1, 0.1)
        #expect(blocks.value(at: far) > 0 && blocks.value(at: far) <= Self.ball(far) + 1e-6)
        #expect(blocks.value(at: .zero) < 0 && blocks.value(at: .zero) >= Self.ball(.zero) - 1e-6)
        #expect(abs(abs(blocks.value(at: .zero)) - 10) < 4, "the bound is conservative but not absurd")
    }

    @Test func aBakeIsTheSameEveryTime() async throws {
        let first = try await DistanceBaker.bake(bounds: Self.box, spacing: 1, distance: Self.ball)
        let second = try await DistanceBaker.bake(bounds: Self.box, spacing: 1, distance: Self.ball)
        #expect(first.blocks.pool == second.blocks.pool && first.blocks.table == second.blocks.table)
        #expect(first.blocks.coarse == second.blocks.coarse)
    }

    @Test func aPartTooBigForTheBudgetIsBakedCoarser() async throws {
        let result = try await DistanceBaker.bake(bounds: Self.box, spacing: 0.25, gridLimit: 2000, storedLimit: 300,
                                                  distance: Self.ball)
        #expect(result.spacing > 0.25)
        #expect(result.blocks.blockCounts.reduce(1, *) <= 2000 && result.blocks.storedBlockCount <= 300)
        #expect(abs(result.blocks.value(at: Vector3(10, 0, 0))) < 0.5, "still a ball")
    }

    @Test func theLimitsAreTheSpecsBudgets() {
        #expect(DistanceBaker.maximumGridBlocks == 4_000_000 && DistanceBaker.maximumStoredBlocks == 40_000)
    }

    @Test func theSpacingFollowsTheTolerance() {
        #expect(abs(DistanceBaker.spacing(forTolerance: 0.01) - 0.4) < 1e-12)
        #expect(DistanceBaker.spacing(forTolerance: 0.04) > DistanceBaker.spacing(forTolerance: 0.01))
    }

    @Test func cancellingStopsTheBake() async {
        let task = Task { try await DistanceBaker.bake(bounds: Self.box, spacing: 0.25, distance: Self.ball) }
        task.cancel()
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/GradingBakerTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct GradingBakerTests {
    let kernel = FakeKernel()
    let tag = NodeTag(node: NodeID(), item: 0)
    let region = BoundingBox(min: Vector3(-15, -15, -5), max: Vector3(15, 15, 15))

    func cube() async throws -> Solid {
        try await kernel.extrude(.rectangle(width: 10, height: 10, plane: .xy), distance: 10, mode: .oneSided, tag: tag)
    }

    @Test func aConstantNeedsNoBakingAndKeepsItsValue() async throws {
        let grading = try await GradingBaker.grading(for: .constant(2), over: region, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(grading == .constant(2) && grading.constantValue == 2)
    }

    @Test func aFieldWithoutKernelDistancesIsNotBaked() async throws {
        let field = Field.remap(.noise(seed: 1, scale: 6, octaves: 2), fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 1, toLow: 0.6, toHigh: 1.6, clamped: true)
        let grading = try await GradingBaker.grading(for: field, over: region, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(grading.baked.isEmpty && grading.range == 0.6...1.6 && grading.slope == field.slopeBound.value)
        #expect(grading.constantValue == nil)
    }

    @Test func aDistanceToASolidIsBakedAndReadBack() async throws {
        let solid = try await cube()
        let field = Field.distance(.kernel(.solid(solid)), signed: true)
        let grading = try await GradingBaker.grading(for: field, over: region, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(grading.baked.count == 1)
        let blocks = try #require(grading.blocks(for: DistanceTarget.solid(solid).identity, signed: true))
        #expect(grading.blocks(for: DistanceTarget.solid(solid).identity, signed: false) == nil)
        // The shell of a big ball, 1 mm thick, graded by the cube's distance: read through the evaluator.
        let graded = ImplicitSolid.offset(.sphere(radius: 20), distance: grading)
        for point in [Vector3(8, 0, 5), Vector3(0, 0, 11), Vector3(0, 12, 5)] {
            let expected = point.length - 20 - blocks.value(at: point)
            #expect(abs(graded.signedDistance(at: point) - expected) < 1e-9)
        }
        #expect(abs(blocks.value(at: Vector3(8, 0, 5)) - 3) < 0.2, "within the interpolation error of a 0.75 mm grid")
    }

    @Test func theRangeAndSlopeCoverWhatTheGridSees() async throws {
        let solid = try await cube()
        let field = Field.distance(.kernel(.solid(solid)), signed: true)
        let grading = try await GradingBaker.grading(for: field, over: region, kernel: kernel)
        // Signed distance over the region runs from −5 (the cube's middle) to the far corner: (15, 15, 15) is √(10² + 10² + 5²) = 15 away.
        #expect(grading.range.lowerBound <= -4.5 && grading.range.upperBound >= 14.5)
        #expect(grading.slope >= 1)
        #expect(grading.slope == field.slopeBound.value * max(1, grading.baked[0].blocks.lipschitz))
    }

    @Test func aClampedDistanceHasItsClampAsRange() async throws {
        let solid = try await cube()
        let field = Field.clamp(.distance(.kernel(.solid(solid)), signed: false), low: 0, high: 4)
        let grading = try await GradingBaker.grading(for: field, over: region, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(grading.range == 0...4)
    }

    @Test func eachDistinctDistanceIsBakedOnce() async throws {
        let solid = try await cube()
        let near = Field.distance(.kernel(.solid(solid)), signed: true)
        let field = Field.add(near, .multiply(near, .distance(.kernel(.solid(solid)), signed: false)))
        let grading = try await GradingBaker.grading(for: field, over: region, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(grading.baked.count == 2, "signed and unsigned are two bakes")
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitBakeCacheTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct ImplicitBakeCacheTests {
    let kernel = FakeKernel()
    let tag = NodeTag(node: NodeID(), item: 0)

    func block(_ size: Double) async throws -> Solid {
        try await kernel.extrude(.rectangle(width: size, height: size, plane: .xy), distance: size, mode: .oneSided, tag: tag)
    }

    @Test func aSolidBakesToItsSignedDistance() async throws {
        let cache = ImplicitBakeCache()
        let baked = try await cache.baked(for: try await block(10), tolerance: 0.01, kernel: kernel)
        // A 10 mm cube standing on z = 0, centred on x and y. Near a face the distance is exact; at the middle, where the true
        // distance has a ridge, the interpolation cuts it a little short.
        #expect(abs(baked.blocks.value(at: Vector3(0, 0, 0.5)) + 0.5) < 0.01)
        #expect(abs(baked.blocks.value(at: Vector3(8, 0, 5)) - 3) < 0.01)
        #expect(abs(baked.blocks.value(at: Vector3(0, 0, 5)) + 5) < 0.5)
        #expect(baked.seconds > 0 && baked.spacing >= 0.4 && baked.tolerance == 0.01)
    }

    @Test func theSameSolidAndToleranceBakeOnce() async throws {
        let cache = ImplicitBakeCache()
        let solid = try await block(10)
        let first = try await cache.baked(for: solid, tolerance: 0.01, kernel: kernel)
        let second = try await cache.baked(for: solid, tolerance: 0.01, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(first.blocks === second.blocks)
        #expect(await cache.bakeCount == 1)
        _ = try await cache.baked(for: solid, tolerance: 0.02, kernel: kernel)
        _ = try await cache.baked(for: try await block(10), tolerance: 0.01, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(await cache.bakeCount == 3)
        #expect(await cache.entryCount == 3)
    }

    @Test func theLeastRecentlyUsedEntriesGoWhenTheCacheIsFull() async throws {
        let cache = ImplicitBakeCache(capacity: 1)
        let (a, b) = (try await block(10), try await block(12))
        let first = try await cache.baked(for: a, tolerance: 0.01, kernel: kernel)
        _ = try await cache.baked(for: b, tolerance: 0.01, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(await cache.entryCount == 1, "only the newest stays")
        let again = try await cache.baked(for: a, tolerance: 0.01, kernel: kernel)
        #expect(first.blocks !== again.blocks)
        #expect(await cache.bakeCount == 3)
    }
}
````

**Modify `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/MeshFixtures.swift`**

````diff
@@ -1,3 +1,4 @@
+import CreatorGeometry
 import CreatorImplicit
 
 /// Small closed meshes for the mesh tests.
@@ -17,3 +18,38 @@ enum MeshFixtures {
             0, 2, 1, 0, 3, 2, 4, 5, 6, 4, 6, 7, 0, 1, 5, 0, 5, 4, 1, 2, 6, 1, 6, 5, 2, 3, 7, 2, 7, 6, 3, 0, 4, 3, 4, 7,
         ])
 }
+
+extension MeshFixtures {
+    /// A sphere of `radius` about the origin, made by splitting the faces of an octahedron `subdivisions` times and pushing the
+    /// vertices out to the sphere. Wound counter-clockwise seen from outside.
+    static func sphere(radius: Double, subdivisions: Int) -> (positions: [Vector3], indices: [UInt32]) {
+        var positions = [Vector3(1, 0, 0), Vector3(-1, 0, 0), Vector3(0, 1, 0), Vector3(0, -1, 0), Vector3(0, 0, 1), Vector3(0, 0, -1)]
+        var triangles: [[Int]] = [[0, 2, 4], [2, 1, 4], [1, 3, 4], [3, 0, 4], [2, 0, 5], [1, 2, 5], [3, 1, 5], [0, 3, 5]]
+        for _ in 0..<subdivisions {
+            var midpoints: [[Int]: Int] = [:]
+            func middle(_ a: Int, _ b: Int) -> Int {
+                let key = [min(a, b), max(a, b)]
+                if let existing = midpoints[key] { return existing }
+                positions.append(((positions[a] + positions[b]) * 0.5).normalized ?? .unitX)
+                midpoints[key] = positions.count - 1
+                return positions.count - 1
+            }
+            triangles = triangles.flatMap { triangle -> [[Int]] in
+                let (a, b, c) = (triangle[0], triangle[1], triangle[2])
+                let (ab, bc, ca) = (middle(a, b), middle(b, c), middle(c, a))
+                return [[a, ab, ca], [ab, b, bc], [ca, bc, c], [ab, bc, ca]]
+            }
+        }
+        return (positions.map { $0 * radius }, triangles.flatMap { $0.map { UInt32($0) } })
+    }
+
+    /// The cube mesh with its vertices doubled per face, as OCCT makes them: every triangle has three vertices of its own.
+    static var cubeSoup: (positions: [Vector3], indices: [UInt32]) {
+        let cube = MeshFixtures.cube
+        let positions = cube.triangles.map { index -> Vector3 in
+            let p = cube.positions[Int(index)]
+            return Vector3(Double(p.x), Double(p.y), Double(p.z))
+        }
+        return (positions, (0..<UInt32(positions.count)).map { $0 })
+    }
+}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/SignedDistanceMeshTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// The signed distance to a closed mesh (implicit spec §4.4, §7): hand-worked values, welding, refusal of open meshes, and the BVH
/// against a brute-force search.
struct SignedDistanceMeshTests {
    static func vectors(_ mesh: TriangleMesh) -> [Vector3] { (0..<mesh.vertexCount).map { mesh.point($0) } }

    func near(_ a: Double, _ b: Double, _ tolerance: Double = 1e-9) -> Bool { abs(a - b) <= tolerance }

    func cube() throws -> SignedDistanceMesh {
        try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: Self.vectors(MeshFixtures.cube), indices: MeshFixtures.cube.triangles)
    }

    @Test func aCubeIsMeasuredToItsFacesEdgesAndCorners() throws {
        let cube = try cube()
        #expect(near(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0.5, 0.5, 0.5)), -0.5))
        #expect(near(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(2, 0.5, 0.5)), 1))
        #expect(near(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(2, 2, 0.5)), 2.0.squareRoot()), "past an edge")
        #expect(near(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(2, 2, 2)), 3.0.squareRoot()), "past a corner")
        #expect(near(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0.9, 0.5, 0.5)), -0.1))
        #expect(near(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(-1, -1, -1)), 3.0.squareRoot()))
        #expect(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(1, 0.5, 0.5)) == 0)
    }

    @Test func theSignIsRightNextToEdgesAndCornersFromBothSides() throws {
        let cube = try cube()
        // Just inside and just outside an edge and a corner, where a plain "nearest face" sign would be unreliable.
        #expect(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0.99, 0.99, 0.5)) < 0)
        #expect(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(1.01, 1.01, 0.5)) > 0)
        #expect(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0.99, 0.99, 0.99)) < 0)
        #expect(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(1.01, 1.01, 1.01)) > 0)
        #expect(cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(1.01, 0.99, 0.5)) > 0 && cube.signedDistance(at: Vector3(0.99, 1.01, 0.5)) > 0)
    }

    @Test func aSoupOfPerFaceVerticesGivesTheSameDistances() throws {
        let soup = MeshFixtures.cubeSoup
        let welded = try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: soup.positions, indices: soup.indices)
        let reference = try cube()
        #expect(welded.triangleCount == 12)
        for point in [Vector3(0.5, 0.5, 0.5), Vector3(2, 2, 2), Vector3(-0.3, 0.4, 0.9), Vector3(0.2, 0.1, 0.05)] {
            #expect(near(welded.signedDistance(at: point), reference.signedDistance(at: point)))
        }
    }

    @Test func anOpenMeshIsRefused() {
        var triangles = MeshFixtures.cube.triangles
        triangles.removeLast(3)
        #expect(throws: MeshDistanceError.notClosed) {
            try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: Self.vectors(MeshFixtures.cube), indices: triangles)
        }
        #expect(MeshDistanceError.notClosed.userMessage == "This solid isn't closed, so inside and outside are undefined.")
    }

    @Test func aFlippedFaceIsRefused() {
        var triangles = MeshFixtures.cube.triangles
        triangles.swapAt(1, 2)
        #expect(throws: MeshDistanceError.notClosed) {
            try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: Self.vectors(MeshFixtures.cube), indices: triangles)
        }
    }

    @Test func anEmptyMeshIsRefused() {
        #expect(throws: MeshDistanceError.notClosed) { try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: [], indices: []) }
    }

    @Test func aSphereMeshIsCloseToASphere() throws {
        let sphere = MeshFixtures.sphere(radius: 5, subdivisions: 3)
        let mesh = try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: sphere.positions, indices: sphere.indices)
        let random = SeededRandom(seed: 5, stream: 1)
        for index in 0..<300 {
            let point = Vector3(random.unit(at: 3 * index) * 16 - 8, random.unit(at: 3 * index + 1) * 16 - 8,
                                random.unit(at: 3 * index + 2) * 16 - 8)
            // 512 triangles whose edges run up to 1.2 mm: the facets sag up to about 0.07 mm inside the sphere.
            #expect(abs(mesh.signedDistance(at: point) - (point.length - 5)) < 0.1, "at \(point)")
        }
    }

    @Test func theBVHMatchesABruteForceSearch() throws {
        let sphere = MeshFixtures.sphere(radius: 5, subdivisions: 2)
        let mesh = try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: sphere.positions, indices: sphere.indices)
        let random = SeededRandom(seed: 6, stream: 1)
        for index in 0..<200 {
            let point = Vector3(random.unit(at: 3 * index) * 14 - 7, random.unit(at: 3 * index + 1) * 14 - 7,
                                random.unit(at: 3 * index + 2) * 14 - 7)
            var best = Double.infinity
            for base in stride(from: 0, to: sphere.indices.count, by: 3) {
                let corners = (0..<3).map { sphere.positions[Int(sphere.indices[base + $0])] }
                best = min(best, Self.distance(from: point, toTriangle: corners))
            }
            #expect(abs(abs(mesh.signedDistance(at: point)) - best) < 1e-9, "at \(point)")
        }
    }

    /// The distance to a triangle: to its face when the projection falls inside, else to the nearest edge.
    static func distance(from p: Vector3, toTriangle t: [Vector3]) -> Double {
        let normal = (t[1] - t[0]).cross(t[2] - t[0])
        let area = normal.length
        if let unit = normal.normalized {
            let projected = p - unit * (p - t[0]).dot(unit)
            let inside = (0..<3).allSatisfy { side in
                (t[(side + 1) % 3] - t[side]).cross(projected - t[side]).dot(normal) >= -1e-12 * area
            }
            if inside { return abs((p - t[0]).dot(unit)) }
        }
        return (0..<3).map { side -> Double in
            let (a, b) = (t[side], t[(side + 1) % 3])
            let along = min(1, max(0, (p - a).dot(b - a) / (b - a).dot(b - a)))
            return (p - (a + (b - a) * along)).length
        }.min() ?? .infinity
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/DistanceBaker.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation

/// Bakes a signed distance function onto `DistanceBlocks` (implicit spec §4.4).
public enum DistanceBaker {
    /// What a bake made.
    public struct Result: Sendable {
        public var blocks: DistanceBlocks
        /// The sample spacing used, which is more than asked for when the part was too big for the block budget.
        public var spacing: Double
        public var seconds: Double
    }

    /// The most blocks the grid may have, stored or not, and the most it may store. A part that needs more is baked coarser.
    public static let maximumGridBlocks = 4_000_000
    public static let maximumStoredBlocks = 40_000

    /// The sample spacing a tolerance asks for. Trilinear interpolation of a distance field is off by about `spacing² ÷ (8 R)` where
    /// the surface curves with radius R; taking R = 2 mm (a fillet that tight is rare) gives `√(16 · tolerance)`: 0.4 mm for the
    /// default 0.01 mm.
    public static func spacing(forTolerance tolerance: Double) -> Double {
        (16 * tolerance).squareRoot()
    }

    /// Bakes `distance` over `bounds` (the surface's box).
    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - spacing: the sample spacing; raised in steps of 26% until the grid and the stored blocks fit the limits.
    ///   - gridLimit: the most blocks the grid may have, stored or not; `storedLimit`, the most it may store.
    ///   - bandBlocks: how far from the surface blocks are stored, in blocks (implicit spec §4.4 says 4; the default 1 stores the blocks
    ///     the surface passes through and their neighbours' worth of margin).
    ///   - distance: the exact signed distance, called from several tasks at once.
    public static func bake(bounds: BoundingBox, spacing requested: Double, bandBlocks: Double = 1,
                            gridLimit: Int = maximumGridBlocks, storedLimit: Int = maximumStoredBlocks,
                            distance: @escaping @Sendable (Vector3) -> Double) async throws -> Result {
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        let start = clock.now
        let size = DistanceBlocks.blockSize
        var spacing = requested
        while true {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let length = Double(size) * spacing
            let band = bandBlocks * length
            let origin = bounds.min - Vector3(band + length, band + length, band + length)
            let span = bounds.max + Vector3(band + length, band + length, band + length) - origin
            let counts = [span.x, span.y, span.z].map { max(1, Int(($0 / length).rounded(.up))) }
            let total = counts.reduce(1, *)
            if total > gridLimit { spacing *= 1.26; continue }
            // First, one probe at each block's centre decides which blocks are stored.
            let reach = length * 3.0.squareRoot() / 2
            var table = [Int32](repeating: -1, count: total)
            var coarse = [Float](repeating: 0, count: total)
            var stored: [Int] = []
            for block in 0..<total {
                if block % 4096 == 0 { try Task.checkCancellation() }
                let centre = origin + Vector3(Double(block % counts[0]) + 0.5, Double((block / counts[0]) % counts[1]) + 0.5,
                                              Double(block / (counts[0] * counts[1])) + 0.5) * length
                let value = distance(centre)
                let lower = abs(value) - reach
                if lower < band {
                    table[block] = Int32(stored.count)
                    stored.append(block)
                } else {
                    coarse[block] = Float(value < 0 ? -lower : lower)
                }
            }
            if stored.count > storedLimit { spacing *= 1.26; continue }
            let pool = try await samples(of: stored, counts: counts, origin: origin, spacing: spacing, distance: distance)
            let blocks = DistanceBlocks(origin: origin, spacing: spacing, blockCounts: counts, table: table, pool: pool, coarse: coarse,
                                        band: band)
            return Result(blocks: blocks, spacing: spacing, seconds: start.duration(to: clock.now) / .seconds(1))
        }
    }

    /// The 512 samples of each stored block, in `stored` order. Several tasks each take a run of blocks.
    private static func samples(of stored: [Int], counts: [Int], origin: Vector3, spacing: Double,
                                distance: @escaping @Sendable (Vector3) -> Double) async throws -> [Float] {
        let size = DistanceBlocks.blockSize
        let perBlock = size * size * size
        let tasks = max(1, min(ProcessInfo.processInfo.activeProcessorCount * 2, stored.count))
        let run = (stored.count + tasks - 1) / max(tasks, 1)
        return try await withThrowingTaskGroup(of: (Int, [Float]).self) { group in
            for first in stride(from: 0, to: stored.count, by: max(run, 1)) {
                let blocks = Array(stored[first..<min(first + run, stored.count)])
                group.addTask {
                    var values: [Float] = []
                    values.reserveCapacity(blocks.count * perBlock)
                    for block in blocks {
                        try Task.checkCancellation()
                        let (bx, by, bz) = (block % counts[0], (block / counts[0]) % counts[1], block / (counts[0] * counts[1]))
                        for z in 0..<size {
                            for y in 0..<size {
                                for x in 0..<size {
                                    let index = Vector3(Double(bx * size + x), Double(by * size + y), Double(bz * size + z))
                                    values.append(Float(distance(origin + index * spacing)))
                                }
                            }
                        }
                    }
                    return (first, values)
                }
            }
            var runs: [(Int, [Float])] = []
            for try await result in group { runs.append(result) }
            return runs.sorted { $0.0 < $1.0 }.flatMap(\.1)
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/GradingBaker.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorKernel

/// Makes a `Grading` from a field (implicit spec §3.3, §4.1): bakes the field's distances to solids, face sets and edge sets (which
/// need the kernel) onto grids over the region the setting applies to, and works out the range and the slope bound the solid's
/// bounds and lipschitz constant are made from.
public enum GradingBaker {
    /// The number of cells across the longest side of a baked distance grid.
    public static let cellsAcross = 40
    /// How many points go to the kernel at once.
    private static let chunk = 16_384

    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - field: the setting's field.
    ///   - region: the box the setting is read in (the solid's bounds, widened by what the setting itself adds).
    ///   - kernel: measures distances to solids.
    ///   - cache: the evaluator's cache of kernel distances.
    public static func grading(for field: Field, over region: BoundingBox, kernel: any Kernel,
                               cache: FieldDistanceCache? = nil) async throws -> Grading {
        if case .constant(let value) = field { return .constant(value) }
        var baked: [Grading.Baked] = []
        for (target, signed) in kernelLeaves(of: field) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            baked.append(Grading.Baked(target: target.identity, signed: signed,
                                       blocks: try await bake(target, signed: signed, over: region, kernel: kernel, cache: cache)))
        }
        let bakedSlope = baked.map(\.blocks.lipschitz).max() ?? 1
        let slope = field.slopeBound.value * max(1, bakedSlope)
        var grading = Grading(field: field, baked: baked, range: 0...0, slope: slope)
        if let known = field.valueRange {
            grading.range = known
            return grading
        }
        // No known range: sample a coarse grid and widen the extremes by what the slope allows between samples.
        let steps = 8
        let cell = region.size * (1 / Double(steps))
        let evaluation = CPUEvaluation()
        var low = Double.infinity, high = -Double.infinity
        for i in 0...steps {
            for j in 0...steps {
                for k in 0...steps {
                    let point = region.min + Vector3(cell.x * Double(i), cell.y * Double(j), cell.z * Double(k))
                    let value = evaluation.value(grading, at: point)
                    guard value.isFinite else { continue }
                    low = min(low, value)
                    high = max(high, value)
                }
            }
        }
        guard low <= high else { return grading }
        let slack = slope * cell.length / 2
        grading.range = (low - slack)...(high + slack)
        return grading
    }

    /// The distinct kernel distances in a field, in order of first appearance.
    static func kernelLeaves(of field: Field) -> [(DistanceTarget, signed: Bool)] {
        var found: [(DistanceTarget, signed: Bool)] = []
        func walk(_ field: Field) {
            if case .distance(.kernel(let target), let signed) = field,
               !found.contains(where: { $0.0.identity == target.identity && $0.signed == signed }) {
                found.append((target, signed))
            }
            field.children.forEach(walk)
        }
        walk(field)
        return found
    }

    /// The distance to `target` on a grid over `region`, every block stored.
    private static func bake(_ target: DistanceTarget, signed: Bool, over region: BoundingBox, kernel: any Kernel,
                             cache: FieldDistanceCache?) async throws -> DistanceBlocks {
        let size = DistanceBlocks.blockSize
        let longest = max(region.size.x, region.size.y, region.size.z, 1e-6)
        let spacing = longest / Double(cellsAcross)
        let counts = [region.size.x, region.size.y, region.size.z].map { max(1, Int(($0 / (spacing * Double(size))).rounded(.up))) }
        let samples = counts.map { $0 * size }
        var points: [Vector3] = []
        points.reserveCapacity(samples.reduce(1, *))
        // Blocks in order, and the samples of each in order.
        for bz in 0..<counts[2] {
            for by in 0..<counts[1] {
                for bx in 0..<counts[0] {
                    for z in 0..<size {
                        for y in 0..<size {
                            for x in 0..<size {
                                points.append(region.min + Vector3(Double(bx * size + x), Double(by * size + y),
                                                                   Double(bz * size + z)) * spacing)
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        var pool: [Float] = []
        pool.reserveCapacity(points.count)
        for start in stride(from: 0, to: points.count, by: chunk) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let slice = Array(points[start..<min(start + chunk, points.count)])
            let values: [Double]
            if let cache {
                values = try await cache.distances(from: slice, to: target, signed: signed, kernel: kernel)
            } else {
                values = try await kernel.distances(from: slice, to: target, signed: signed)
            }
            pool += values.map(Float.init)
        }
        let blockTotal = counts.reduce(1, *)
        return DistanceBlocks(origin: region.min, spacing: spacing, blockCounts: counts, table: (0..<blockTotal).map(Int32.init),
                              pool: pool, coarse: [Float](repeating: 0, count: blockTotal), band: region.size.length)
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/ImplicitBakeCache.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Distance blocks baked from solids (implicit spec §4.4), kept so editing a number downstream of To Implicit doesn't bake again. Keyed
/// by the solid object (its identity) and the tolerance; least recently used entries go first once the blocks hold more than
/// `capacity` bytes (512 MB by default). An entry holds its solid, so the identity can't be reused while the entry exists.
public actor ImplicitBakeCache {
    /// What a bake made.
    public struct Baked: Sendable {
        public var blocks: DistanceBlocks
        /// The sample spacing used, which is more than `DistanceBaker.spacing(forTolerance:)` when the part was too big for the budget.
        public var spacing: Double
        /// How long the tessellation, the BVH and the bake took.
        public var seconds: Double
        /// The tolerance the OCCT tessellation was made at.
        public var tolerance: Double
    }

    /// The cache every To Implicit node shares.
    public static let shared = ImplicitBakeCache()

    private struct Key: Hashable {
        var solid: ObjectIdentifier
        var tolerance: Double
    }

    private struct Entry {
        var solid: Solid
        var baked: Baked
        var used: Int
    }

    public let capacity: Int
    private var entries: [Key: Entry] = [:]
    private var clock = 0
    /// How many bakes have run (a hit doesn't count).
    public private(set) var bakeCount = 0

    public init(capacity: Int = 512 * 1024 * 1024) {
        self.capacity = capacity
    }

    public var entryCount: Int { entries.count }
    public var totalBytes: Int { entries.values.reduce(0) { $0 + $1.baked.blocks.estimatedBytes } }

    /// The baked distance of `solid`, from the cache or made now: the kernel tessellates it at `tolerance`, a BVH measures the
    /// distance and the sign comes from pseudonormals, and `DistanceBaker` samples it.
    /// - Throws: `MeshDistanceError.notClosed` for a solid whose tessellation has holes, and anything the kernel throws.
    public func baked(for solid: Solid, tolerance: Double, kernel: any Kernel) async throws -> Baked {
        let key = Key(solid: ObjectIdentifier(solid), tolerance: tolerance)
        clock += 1
        if var hit = entries[key] {
            hit.used = clock
            entries[key] = hit
            return hit.baked
        }
        let clockStart = ContinuousClock.now
        let mesh = try await kernel.tessellate(solid, tolerance: tolerance)
        let distance = try SignedDistanceMesh(positions: mesh.positions, indices: mesh.indices)
        let result = try await DistanceBaker.bake(bounds: distance.bounds, spacing: DistanceBaker.spacing(forTolerance: tolerance)) {
            distance.signedDistance(at: $0)
        }
        let baked = Baked(blocks: result.blocks, spacing: result.spacing, seconds: clockStart.duration(to: .now) / .seconds(1),
                          tolerance: tolerance)
        bakeCount += 1
        clock += 1
        entries[key] = Entry(solid: solid, baked: baked, used: clock)
        evict()
        return baked
    }

    /// Drops the least recently used entries until the rest fit, keeping the newest.
    private func evict() {
        var bytes = totalBytes
        for (key, entry) in entries.sorted(by: { $0.value.used < $1.value.used }) where bytes > capacity && entries.count > 1 {
            entries[key] = nil
            bytes -= entry.baked.blocks.estimatedBytes
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/SignedDistanceMesh.swift`**

````swift
import Foundation
import CreatorGeometry

/// Why a mesh can't give inside and outside.
public enum MeshDistanceError: Error, Equatable, Sendable {
    /// The mesh has an edge with one face, three or more, or two faces that run the same way (To Implicit's message is
    /// `userMessage`).
    case notClosed

    public var userMessage: String { "This solid isn't closed, so inside and outside are undefined." }
}

/// The signed distance to a closed triangle mesh (implicit spec §4.4): the unsigned distance is the nearest triangle, found by a BVH;
/// the sign comes from the angle-weighted pseudonormal of the nearest feature (Bærentzen and Aanæs, 2005), which is exact for any
/// closed, consistently oriented mesh, with no ray casting and no tolerance. The mesh may be a soup of per-face meshes, as OCCT
/// makes: vertices closer than `weldTolerance` are one vertex first. Negative is inside. Triangles must be counter-clockwise seen from
/// outside, as `Kernel.tessellate` gives them.
public struct SignedDistanceMesh: Sendable {
    private let vertices: [SIMD3<Double>]
    private let triangles: [SIMD3<Int32>]
    private let faceNormals: [SIMD3<Double>]
    /// The angle-weighted normal at each vertex.
    private let vertexNormals: [SIMD3<Double>]
    /// For each triangle, the normal of each of its edges (a, b), (b, c), (c, a): the sum of the two faces' normals.
    private let edgeNormals: [SIMD3<Double>]
    private let bvh: TriangleBVH

    /// The box around the mesh.
    public let bounds: BoundingBox

    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - positions: vertex positions in millimetres.
    ///   - indices: three per triangle.
    ///   - weldTolerance: vertices closer than this (per axis, after rounding to a grid of it) are merged.
    /// - Throws: `MeshDistanceError.notClosed`.
    public init(positions: [Vector3], indices: [UInt32], weldTolerance: Double = 1e-6) throws(MeshDistanceError) {
        let (welded, kept) = Self.weld(positions, indices, tolerance: weldTolerance)
        guard !kept.isEmpty, Self.isClosed(kept) else { throw .notClosed }
        let normals = Self.normals(vertices: welded, triangles: kept)
        self.vertices = welded
        self.triangles = kept
        self.faceNormals = normals.faces
        self.vertexNormals = normals.vertices
        self.edgeNormals = normals.edges
        self.bvh = TriangleBVH(vertices: welded, triangles: kept)
        let low = welded.dropFirst().reduce(welded[0]) { pointwiseMin($0, $1) }
        let high = welded.dropFirst().reduce(welded[0]) { pointwiseMax($0, $1) }
        self.bounds = BoundingBox(min: Vector3(low.x, low.y, low.z), max: Vector3(high.x, high.y, high.z))
    }

    private struct EdgeKey: Hashable {
        var low: Int32
        var high: Int32

        init(_ from: Int32, _ to: Int32) {
            low = min(from, to)
            high = max(from, to)
        }
    }

    /// Merges vertices that round to the same cell of `tolerance` and drops the triangles that merging collapsed or that have no area.
    private static func weld(_ positions: [Vector3], _ indices: [UInt32],
                             tolerance: Double) -> ([SIMD3<Double>], [SIMD3<Int32>]) {
        struct Cell: Hashable {
            var x: Int
            var y: Int
            var z: Int
        }
        var merged: [Cell: Int32] = [:]
        var welded: [SIMD3<Double>] = []
        var remap = [Int32](repeating: 0, count: positions.count)
        for (index, position) in positions.enumerated() {
            let cell = Cell(x: Int((position.x / tolerance).rounded()), y: Int((position.y / tolerance).rounded()),
                            z: Int((position.z / tolerance).rounded()))
            if let existing = merged[cell] {
                remap[index] = existing
            } else {
                merged[cell] = Int32(welded.count)
                remap[index] = Int32(welded.count)
                welded.append(SIMD3(position.x, position.y, position.z))
            }
        }
        var kept: [SIMD3<Int32>] = []
        for base in stride(from: 0, to: indices.count - 2, by: 3) {
            let triangle = SIMD3(remap[Int(indices[base])], remap[Int(indices[base + 1])], remap[Int(indices[base + 2])])
            guard triangle.x != triangle.y, triangle.y != triangle.z, triangle.z != triangle.x else { continue }
            let (a, b, c) = (welded[Int(triangle.x)], welded[Int(triangle.y)], welded[Int(triangle.z)])
            let area = cross(b - a, c - a)
            if (area * area).sum() > 1e-24 { kept.append(triangle) }
        }
        return (welded, kept)
    }

    /// Whether every edge is shared by two faces that run along it in opposite directions.
    private static func isClosed(_ triangles: [SIMD3<Int32>]) -> Bool {
        var faces: [EdgeKey: (count: Int, forward: Int)] = [:]
        for triangle in triangles {
            for side in 0..<3 {
                let (from, to) = (triangle[side], triangle[(side + 1) % 3])
                var entry = faces[EdgeKey(from, to)] ?? (0, 0)
                entry.count += 1
                if from < to { entry.forward += 1 }
                faces[EdgeKey(from, to)] = entry
            }
        }
        return faces.values.allSatisfy { $0.count == 2 && $0.forward == 1 }
    }

    /// The face normals, the angle-weighted vertex normals, and each triangle's three edge normals.
    private static func normals(vertices: [SIMD3<Double>], triangles: [SIMD3<Int32>])
        -> (faces: [SIMD3<Double>], vertices: [SIMD3<Double>], edges: [SIMD3<Double>]) {
        var faces: [SIMD3<Double>] = []
        var vertexNormals = [SIMD3<Double>](repeating: .zero, count: vertices.count)
        var edgeSums: [EdgeKey: SIMD3<Double>] = [:]
        for triangle in triangles {
            let corners = [vertices[Int(triangle.x)], vertices[Int(triangle.y)], vertices[Int(triangle.z)]]
            let raw = cross(corners[1] - corners[0], corners[2] - corners[0])
            let normal = raw / (raw * raw).sum().squareRoot()
            faces.append(normal)
            for corner in 0..<3 {
                let (next, previous) = (corners[(corner + 1) % 3] - corners[corner], corners[(corner + 2) % 3] - corners[corner])
                let cosine = (next * previous).sum() / ((next * next).sum() * (previous * previous).sum()).squareRoot()
                vertexNormals[Int(triangle[corner])] += normal * acos(min(1, max(-1, cosine)))
                edgeSums[EdgeKey(triangle[corner], triangle[(corner + 1) % 3]), default: .zero] += normal
            }
        }
        let edges = triangles.flatMap { triangle in
            (0..<3).map { edgeSums[EdgeKey(triangle[$0], triangle[($0 + 1) % 3])] ?? .zero }
        }
        return (faces, vertexNormals, edges)
    }

    /// How many triangles remain after welding and dropping slivers.
    public var triangleCount: Int { triangles.count }

    /// The signed distance from `point` to the surface: negative inside.
    public func signedDistance(at point: Vector3) -> Double {
        let p = SIMD3(point.x, point.y, point.z)
        var best = Double.infinity
        var bestPoint = p
        var bestNormal = SIMD3<Double>.zero
        bvh.search(from: p, bound: { best }, visit: { index in
            let triangle = triangles[index]
            let (a, b, c) = (vertices[Int(triangle.x)], vertices[Int(triangle.y)], vertices[Int(triangle.z)])
            let (closest, feature) = Self.closestPoint(on: (a, b, c), to: p)
            let offset = p - closest
            let squared = (offset * offset).sum()
            guard squared < best else { return }
            best = squared
            bestPoint = closest
            switch feature {
            case .face: bestNormal = faceNormals[index]
            case .vertex(let corner): bestNormal = vertexNormals[Int(triangle[corner])]
            case .edge(let side): bestNormal = edgeNormals[3 * index + side]
            }
        })
        guard best.isFinite else { return .infinity }
        let distance = best.squareRoot()
        let side = ((p - bestPoint) * bestNormal).sum()
        return side < 0 ? -distance : distance
    }

    // MARK: Closest point on a triangle

    private enum Feature {
        case face
        case vertex(Int)
        case edge(Int)
    }

    private static func cross(_ a: SIMD3<Double>, _ b: SIMD3<Double>) -> SIMD3<Double> {
        SIMD3(a.y * b.z - a.z * b.y, a.z * b.x - a.x * b.z, a.x * b.y - a.y * b.x)
    }

    /// The nearest point of triangle `abc` to `p` and the feature it lies on (Ericson, Real-Time Collision Detection, 5.1.5).
    private static func closestPoint(on triangle: (SIMD3<Double>, SIMD3<Double>, SIMD3<Double>),
                                     to p: SIMD3<Double>) -> (SIMD3<Double>, Feature) {
        let (a, b, c) = triangle
        let (ab, ac, ap) = (b - a, c - a, p - a)
        let d1 = (ab * ap).sum(), d2 = (ac * ap).sum()
        if d1 <= 0, d2 <= 0 { return (a, .vertex(0)) }
        let bp = p - b
        let d3 = (ab * bp).sum(), d4 = (ac * bp).sum()
        if d3 >= 0, d4 <= d3 { return (b, .vertex(1)) }
        let vc = d1 * d4 - d3 * d2
        if vc <= 0, d1 >= 0, d3 <= 0 { return (a + ab * (d1 / (d1 - d3)), .edge(0)) }
        let cp = p - c
        let d5 = (ab * cp).sum(), d6 = (ac * cp).sum()
        if d6 >= 0, d5 <= d6 { return (c, .vertex(2)) }
        let vb = d5 * d2 - d1 * d6
        if vb <= 0, d2 >= 0, d6 <= 0 { return (a + ac * (d2 / (d2 - d6)), .edge(2)) }
        let va = d3 * d6 - d5 * d4
        if va <= 0, d4 - d3 >= 0, d5 - d6 >= 0 {
            return (b + (c - b) * ((d4 - d3) / ((d4 - d3) + (d5 - d6))), .edge(1))
        }
        let denominator = 1 / (va + vb + vc)
        return (a + ab * (vb * denominator) + ac * (vc * denominator), .face)
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Bake/TriangleBVH.swift`**

````swift
/// A bounding volume hierarchy over triangles (implicit spec §4.4): a binary tree of boxes, split at the median of the triangles'
/// centres along the longest axis, with at most four triangles in a leaf. It answers "which triangle is nearest to this point" by
/// walking the tree nearest box first and skipping boxes farther than the best found.
struct TriangleBVH: Sendable {
    struct Node: Sendable {
        var low: SIMD3<Double>
        var high: SIMD3<Double>
        /// For a leaf the index of its first triangle in `order`, else the index of the left child.
        var first: Int32
        /// For an inner node the index of the right child.
        var second: Int32
        /// The number of triangles in a leaf; 0 for an inner node.
        var count: Int32
    }

    private(set) var nodes: [Node] = []
    /// Triangle numbers in leaf order.
    private(set) var order: [Int32] = []

    private static let leafSize = 4

    init(vertices: [SIMD3<Double>], triangles: [SIMD3<Int32>]) {
        order = triangles.indices.map(Int32.init)
        guard !triangles.isEmpty else { return }
        let boxes = triangles.map { triangle -> (low: SIMD3<Double>, high: SIMD3<Double>, centre: SIMD3<Double>) in
            let (a, b, c) = (vertices[Int(triangle.x)], vertices[Int(triangle.y)], vertices[Int(triangle.z)])
            let low = pointwiseMin(a, pointwiseMin(b, c)), high = pointwiseMax(a, pointwiseMax(b, c))
            return (low, high, (a + b + c) / 3)
        }
        nodes.reserveCapacity(2 * triangles.count / Self.leafSize + 1)
        _ = build(range: 0..<triangles.count, boxes: boxes)
    }

    /// Builds the subtree over `order[range]` and returns its node's index.
    private mutating func build(range: Range<Int>, boxes: [(low: SIMD3<Double>, high: SIMD3<Double>, centre: SIMD3<Double>)]) -> Int {
        var low = SIMD3<Double>(repeating: .infinity), high = SIMD3<Double>(repeating: -.infinity)
        var centreLow = low, centreHigh = high
        for slot in range {
            let box = boxes[Int(order[slot])]
            low = pointwiseMin(low, box.low)
            high = pointwiseMax(high, box.high)
            centreLow = pointwiseMin(centreLow, box.centre)
            centreHigh = pointwiseMax(centreHigh, box.centre)
        }
        let index = nodes.count
        nodes.append(Node(low: low, high: high, first: Int32(range.lowerBound), second: 0, count: Int32(range.count)))
        guard range.count > Self.leafSize else { return index }
        let spread = centreHigh - centreLow
        let axis = spread.x >= spread.y && spread.x >= spread.z ? 0 : (spread.y >= spread.z ? 1 : 2)
        let middle = range.lowerBound + range.count / 2
        // Ties on the centre are broken by triangle number, so the build is the same on every run.
        order[range].sort { lhs, rhs in
            let (a, b) = (boxes[Int(lhs)].centre[axis], boxes[Int(rhs)].centre[axis])
            return a == b ? lhs < rhs : a < b
        }
        let left = build(range: range.lowerBound..<middle, boxes: boxes)
        let right = build(range: middle..<range.upperBound, boxes: boxes)
        nodes[index].first = Int32(left)
        nodes[index].second = Int32(right)
        nodes[index].count = 0
        return index
    }

    /// The squared distance from `point` to a node's box.
    private func boxDistanceSquared(_ node: Node, _ point: SIMD3<Double>) -> Double {
        let outside = pointwiseMax(pointwiseMax(node.low - point, point - node.high), .zero)
        return (outside * outside).sum()
    }

    /// Calls `visit(triangle)` for the triangles whose boxes could hold something nearer than `bound()`, nearest box first. `visit`
    /// returns nothing; the caller tightens what `bound()` returns as it finds closer triangles.
    func search(from point: SIMD3<Double>, bound: () -> Double, visit: (Int) -> Void) {
        guard !nodes.isEmpty else { return }
        var stack: [Int] = [0]
        stack.reserveCapacity(64)
        while let index = stack.popLast() {
            let node = nodes[index]
            guard boxDistanceSquared(node, point) < bound() else { continue }
            if node.count > 0 {
                for slot in Int(node.first)..<Int(node.first + node.count) { visit(Int(order[slot])) }
            } else {
                let (left, right) = (Int(node.first), Int(node.second))
                // Push the farther child first, so the nearer is searched first.
                if boxDistanceSquared(nodes[left], point) <= boxDistanceSquared(nodes[right], point) {
                    stack.append(right)
                    stack.append(left)
                } else {
                    stack.append(left)
                    stack.append(right)
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
````

### Task 6: The sparse octree

Find which 8×8×8-voxel bricks the surface can pass through by pruning cells whose centre value divided by the lipschitz constant exceeds half the diagonal (spec §4.2), batch-evaluate their samples, and refuse an export that would not fit in half of physical memory.

**Files:**

- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Sampling/BrickPlan.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Sampling/ImplicitBatchEvaluator.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Sampling/MeshingBudget.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/BrickPlanTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/CPUBatchEvaluatorTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/MeshingBudgetTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `ImplicitSolid.bounds`, `.lipschitz` (Task 2); `CPUEvaluation` (Task 3).
- Produces (the public declarations this task adds; later tasks use these exact names):

````swift
public struct BrickPlan: Sendable
public static let brickSize = 8
public let origin: Vector3
public let resolution: Double
public let brickCounts: [Int]
public let bricks: [SIMD3<Int32>]
public let evaluatedCells: Int
public static func build(for solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double,
public func point(_ i: Int, _ j: Int, _ k: Int) -> Vector3
public func samples(of bricks: ArraySlice<SIMD3<Int32>>, using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws -> [Float]
public protocol ImplicitBatchEvaluator: Sendable
public struct CPUBatchEvaluator: ImplicitBatchEvaluator
public let solid: ImplicitSolid
public init(_ solid: ImplicitSolid)
public var engineName: String
public func evaluate(_ points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float]
public enum ImplicitMeshingError: Error, Equatable, Sendable
public var userMessage: String
public struct MeshingBudget: Sendable
public let limit: Int
public init(physicalMemory: UInt64 = ProcessInfo.processInfo.physicalMemory)
public static func estimate(for solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double,
public func check(_ solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double, using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws
````


**Notes for the implementer:**

- `ImplicitBatchEvaluator` is the per-solid sampling seam the octree and mesher call: `evaluate(_ points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float]`. `CPUBatchEvaluator` is a complete engine on every platform (Errata D11); Task 8's `GPUBatchEvaluator` adapts an `ImplicitGPUEngine` to it.
- `MeshingBudget.estimate` meshes a coarse octree (about 64 voxels across), counts the bricks and scales by the square of the resolution ratio; a brick costs 12,800 bytes (64 surface cubes, two triangles each, about 100 bytes a triangle). The refusal message and the suggested resolution are the spec's: "This needs about N GB at 0.1 mm; coarsen to X mm or split the part."

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/BrickPlanTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// The sparse octree (implicit spec §4.2, §7): it never prunes a brick the surface passes through, and it prunes most of the rest.
struct BrickPlanTests {
    /// Every voxel of the lattice that has a sign change among its eight corners, found by evaluating everything.
    func crossingVoxels(of solid: ImplicitSolid, plan: BrickPlan) -> [SIMD3<Int>] {
        let size = BrickPlan.brickSize
        let counts = plan.brickCounts.map { $0 * size + 1 }
        let evaluation = CPUEvaluation()
        var values = [Double](repeating: 0, count: counts[0] * counts[1] * counts[2])
        for k in 0..<counts[2] {
            for j in 0..<counts[1] {
                for i in 0..<counts[0] {
                    values[(k * counts[1] + j) * counts[0] + i] = evaluation.distance(solid, at: plan.point(i, j, k))
                }
            }
        }
        var found: [SIMD3<Int>] = []
        for k in 0..<counts[2] - 1 {
            for j in 0..<counts[1] - 1 {
                for i in 0..<counts[0] - 1 {
                    let corners = (0..<8).map { values[((k + $0 >> 2) * counts[1] + j + ($0 >> 1 & 1)) * counts[0] + i + ($0 & 1)] }
                    if corners.contains(where: { $0 < 0 }) && corners.contains(where: { $0 >= 0 }) { found.append(SIMD3(i, j, k)) }
                }
            }
        }
        return found
    }

    @Test(arguments: [("sphere", 0.5), ("box", 0.7), ("smooth union", 0.5), ("graded offset", 0.6), ("baked", 0.8), ("isosurface", 0.6)])
    func everyCrossingVoxelIsInASurvivingBrick(_ name: String, _ resolution: Double) async throws {
        let solid = try #require(ImplicitFixtures.all.first { $0.name == name }?.solid)
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: resolution, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid))
        let kept = Set(plan.bricks.map { SIMD3<Int>(Int($0.x), Int($0.y), Int($0.z)) })
        let crossings = crossingVoxels(of: solid, plan: plan)
        #expect(!crossings.isEmpty && !plan.bricks.isEmpty)
        for voxel in crossings {
            let brick = SIMD3(voxel.x / BrickPlan.brickSize, voxel.y / BrickPlan.brickSize, voxel.z / BrickPlan.brickSize)
            #expect(kept.contains(brick), "\(name): voxel \(voxel) is in brick \(brick), which was pruned")
        }
    }

    @Test func aSphereNeedsAboutAsManyBricksAsItsSurfaceHas() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: 0.2, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid))
        // Area 4π·10² = 1257 mm²; a brick is 1.6 mm across, so 491 bricks tile it flat, and a sphere cuts up to twice that.
        #expect(plan.bricks.count > 491 && plan.bricks.count < 1100)
        let dense = plan.brickCounts.reduce(1, *)
        #expect(plan.bricks.count < dense / 2, "prunes the inside and the outside: \(plan.bricks.count) of \(dense)")
        #expect(plan.evaluatedCells < dense, "and never evaluates the dense grid: \(plan.evaluatedCells)")
    }

    @Test func theBricksAreSortedAndInsideTheGrid() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitFixtures.box
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: 0.5, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid))
        let keys = plan.bricks.map { [$0.x, $0.y, $0.z] }
        #expect(keys == keys.sorted { $0.lexicographicallyPrecedes($1) })
        for brick in plan.bricks {
            #expect(Int(brick.x) < plan.brickCounts[0] && Int(brick.y) < plan.brickCounts[1] && Int(brick.z) < plan.brickCounts[2])
        }
        #expect(Set(keys).count == keys.count)
    }

    @Test func theGridIsTheBoundsPaddedByOneVoxel() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: 0.5, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid))
        #expect(plan.origin == Vector3(-10.5, -10.5, -10.5))
        // 21 mm over 4 mm bricks.
        #expect(plan.brickCounts == [6, 6, 6])
    }

    @Test func aSolidWithNothingToMeshHasNoBricks() async throws {
        // Two shapes that don't meet: an intersection has an empty (point-sized) box and no crossing.
        let move = RigidMove(origin: Vector3(20, 0, 0), xAxis: .unitX, yAxis: .unitY, zAxis: .unitZ)
        let apart = ImplicitSolid.intersect([.sphere(radius: 3), .moved(.sphere(radius: 3), move)], blend: .constant(0))
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: apart, resolution: 0.5, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(apart))
        #expect(plan.bricks.isEmpty)
    }

    @Test func samplesAreNineCubedPerBrickAndSharedFacesAgree() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitFixtures.cylinder
        let evaluator = CPUBatchEvaluator(solid)
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: 0.5, using: evaluator)
        let samples = try await plan.samples(of: plan.bricks[...], using: evaluator)
        #expect(samples.count == plan.bricks.count * 729)
        // Two bricks side by side read the same values on the face they share.
        let index = Dictionary(uniqueKeysWithValues: plan.bricks.enumerated().map { (SIMD3<Int>(Int($1.x), Int($1.y), Int($1.z)), $0) })
        var compared = 0
        for (brick, number) in index {
            guard let next = index[brick &+ SIMD3(1, 0, 0)] else { continue }
            for z in 0...8 {
                for y in 0...8 {
                    #expect(samples[number * 729 + (z * 9 + y) * 9 + 8] == samples[next * 729 + (z * 9 + y) * 9])
                    compared += 1
                }
            }
        }
        #expect(compared > 0)
    }

    @Test func cancellingStopsThePlan() async {
        let solid = ImplicitFixtures.sphere
        let task = Task { try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: 0.1, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid)) }
        task.cancel()
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }
}
````

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct MeshingBudgetTests {
    let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)

    @Test func theEstimateGrowsWithTheSquareOfTheFinerResolution() async throws {
        let evaluator = CPUBatchEvaluator(solid)
        let coarse = try await MeshingBudget.estimate(for: solid, resolution: 0.5, using: evaluator)
        let fine = try await MeshingBudget.estimate(for: solid, resolution: 0.25, using: evaluator)
        #expect(coarse > 0)
        // Twice as fine is four times the surface voxels (the coarse pass is the same, so the ratio is exactly the scale).
        #expect(abs(Double(fine) / Double(coarse) - 4) < 0.2, "ratio \(Double(fine) / Double(coarse))")
    }

    @Test func theLimitIsHalfOfPhysicalMemory() {
        #expect(MeshingBudget(physicalMemory: 16_000_000_000).limit == 8_000_000_000)
    }

    @Test func aJobWithinTheLimitPasses() async throws {
        try await MeshingBudget(physicalMemory: 64_000_000_000).check(solid, resolution: 0.5, using: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid))
    }

    @Test func aJobOverTheLimitIsRefusedWithAWayOut() async throws {
        let budget = MeshingBudget(physicalMemory: 2_000_000)
        let evaluator = CPUBatchEvaluator(solid)
        let bytes = try await MeshingBudget.estimate(for: solid, resolution: 0.1, using: evaluator)
        do {
            try await budget.check(solid, resolution: 0.1, using: evaluator)
            Issue.record("the job should have been refused")
        } catch let error as ImplicitMeshingError {
            guard case .tooBig(let reported, let resolution, let suggestion) = error else { Issue.record("wrong error"); return }
            #expect(reported == bytes && resolution == 0.1)
            // The suggestion makes the job fit: bytes follow 1 / resolution².
            #expect(suggestion > 0.1)
            let fits = Double(bytes) * (0.1 / suggestion) * (0.1 / suggestion)
            #expect(fits <= Double(budget.limit) * 1.001)
            let gigabytes = (Double(bytes) / 1_000_000_000).formatted(.number.precision(.fractionLength(1)))
            let advice = "coarsen to \(suggestion.formatted()) mm or split the part."
            #expect(error.userMessage == "This needs about \(gigabytes) GB at 0.1 mm; \(advice)")
        }
    }

    @Test func theMessagesAreTheSpecs() {
        #expect(ImplicitMeshingError.tooBig(bytes: 12_340_000_000, resolution: 0.1, suggestion: 0.35).userMessage
            == "This needs about 12.3 GB at 0.1 mm; coarsen to 0.35 mm or split the part.")
        #expect(ImplicitMeshingError.empty.userMessage == "There is no surface to mesh: the shape is empty, or smaller than a voxel.")
    }
}
````
