**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Sampling/BrickPlan.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// The sparse octree over an implicit solid (implicit spec §4.2): which 8 × 8 × 8 bricks of voxels the surface can pass through.
///
/// The root is a cube of bricks that covers the solid's bounds padded by one voxel. Each cell evaluates the solid at its centre; when
/// |f| ÷ lipschitz is more than half the cell's diagonal the surface can't be inside (the value can't change that fast) and the cell
/// is pruned. Cells that don't touch the bounds are pruned without evaluating. What survives down to a single brick is a brick to
/// mesh. Memory and time follow the surface area, not the volume.
public struct BrickPlan: Sendable {
    /// Voxels along each side of a brick; a brick has this many plus one lattice points on a side (the last is shared).
    public static let brickSize = 8

    /// The world position of lattice point (0, 0, 0).
    public let origin: Vector3
    /// The voxel size in millimetres.
    public let resolution: Double
    /// How many bricks the padded bounds span on each axis.
    public let brickCounts: [Int]
    /// The bricks the surface can pass through, as integer brick coordinates, sorted by x, then y, then z.
    public let bricks: [SIMD3<Int32>]
    /// How many cells the octree evaluated, bricks and pruned cells together.
    public let evaluatedCells: Int

    /// The most points sent to the evaluator at once while pruning.
    static let batch = 1 << 20

    public static func build(for solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double,
                             using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws -> BrickPlan {
        let size = brickSize
        let box = solid.bounds
        let origin = box.min - Vector3(resolution, resolution, resolution)
        let span = box.max + Vector3(resolution, resolution, resolution) - origin
        let counts = [span.x, span.y, span.z].map { max(1, Int(($0 / (resolution * Double(size))).rounded(.up))) }
        let longest = counts.max() ?? 1
        var cellBricks = 1
        while cellBricks < longest { cellBricks *= 2 }
        let brickLength = resolution * Double(size)
        let lipschitz = solid.lipschitz
        let reachable = BoundingBox(min: box.min - Vector3(resolution, resolution, resolution),
                                    max: box.max + Vector3(resolution, resolution, resolution))

        var cells: [SIMD3<Int32>] = [.zero]
        var survivors: [SIMD3<Int32>] = []
        var evaluated = 0
        while !cells.isEmpty {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let length = brickLength * Double(cellBricks)
            // Cells that can't touch the bounds hold no surface.
            let touching = cells.filter { cell in
                let low = origin + Vector3(Double(cell.x), Double(cell.y), Double(cell.z)) * brickLength
                return low.x <= reachable.max.x && low.x + length >= reachable.min.x && low.y <= reachable.max.y
                    && low.y + length >= reachable.min.y && low.z <= reachable.max.z && low.z + length >= reachable.min.z
            }
            evaluated += touching.count
            let undecided = try await undecidedCells(touching, origin: origin, cellLength: length, brickLength: brickLength,
                                                     lipschitz: lipschitz, using: evaluator)
            if cellBricks == 1 {
                survivors += undecided
                break
            }
            let half = Int32(cellBricks / 2)
            cells = undecided.flatMap { cell in
                (0..<8).compactMap { child -> SIMD3<Int32>? in
                    let next = SIMD3(cell.x + (child & 1 == 0 ? 0 : half), cell.y + (child & 2 == 0 ? 0 : half),
                                     cell.z + (child & 4 == 0 ? 0 : half))
                    return Int(next.x) < counts[0] && Int(next.y) < counts[1] && Int(next.z) < counts[2] ? next : nil
                }
            }
            cellBricks /= 2
        }
        survivors.sort { ($0.x, $0.y, $0.z) < ($1.x, $1.y, $1.z) }
        return BrickPlan(origin: origin, resolution: resolution, brickCounts: counts, bricks: survivors, evaluatedCells: evaluated)
    }

    /// The cells whose centre value can't rule the surface out: |f| ÷ lipschitz is not more than half the cell's diagonal.
    private static func undecidedCells(_ cells: [SIMD3<Int32>], origin: Vector3, cellLength length: Double, brickLength: Double,
                                       lipschitz: Double, using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws -> [SIMD3<Int32>] {
        var undecided: [SIMD3<Int32>] = []
        let halfDiagonal = length * 3.0.squareRoot() / 2
        for start in stride(from: 0, to: cells.count, by: batch) {
            let slice = Array(cells[start..<min(start + batch, cells.count)])
            let centres = slice.map { cell -> SIMD3<Float> in
                let centre = origin + (Vector3(Double(cell.x), Double(cell.y), Double(cell.z)) * brickLength)
                    + Vector3(length / 2, length / 2, length / 2)
                return SIMD3(Float(centre.x), Float(centre.y), Float(centre.z))
            }
            let values = try await evaluator.evaluate(centres)
            for (cell, value) in zip(slice, values) where !(Double(abs(value)) / lipschitz > halfDiagonal) {
                undecided.append(cell)
            }
        }
        return undecided
    }

    /// The world position of lattice point (i, j, k).
    public func point(_ i: Int, _ j: Int, _ k: Int) -> Vector3 {
        origin + Vector3(Double(i), Double(j), Double(k)) * resolution
    }

    /// The solid's value at the 9 × 9 × 9 lattice points of each brick in `bricks`, brick after brick, x fastest.
    public func samples(of bricks: ArraySlice<SIMD3<Int32>>, using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws -> [Float] {
        let size = Self.brickSize
        var points: [SIMD3<Float>] = []
        points.reserveCapacity(bricks.count * (size + 1) * (size + 1) * (size + 1))
        for brick in bricks {
            for z in 0...size {
                for y in 0...size {
                    for x in 0...size {
                        let p = point(Int(brick.x) * size + x, Int(brick.y) * size + y, Int(brick.z) * size + z)
                        points.append(SIMD3(Float(p.x), Float(p.y), Float(p.z)))
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        return try await evaluator.evaluate(points)
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Sampling/ImplicitBatchEvaluator.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation

/// Evaluates an implicit solid at a batch of points (implicit spec §4.1). The sparse octree and the mesher call it with thousands of
/// points at a time; `CPUBatchEvaluator` is a complete engine on every platform (implicit spec Errata D11), and `GPUBatchEvaluator`
/// (Task 8) is the fast path where an `ImplicitGPUEngine` exists. Coordinates and values are `Float`, as on the GPU.
public protocol ImplicitBatchEvaluator: Sendable {
    /// The value at each of `points`, in order. Throws `CancellationError` when the task is cancelled between chunks.
    func evaluate(_ points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float]

    /// "CPU" or the GPU engine's name ("Metal"), for the preview badge and the inspector.
    var engineName: String { get }
}

/// The reference evaluation, in `Double`, spread over the processors.
public struct CPUBatchEvaluator: ImplicitBatchEvaluator {
    public let solid: ImplicitSolid

    public init(_ solid: ImplicitSolid) {
        self.solid = solid
    }

    public var engineName: String { "CPU" }

    /// How many points one task handles between cancellation checks.
    static let chunk = 2048

    public func evaluate(_ points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float] {
        try Task.checkCancellation()
        guard points.count > Self.chunk else { return evaluate(points[...]) }
        let solid = solid
        let tasks = max(1, ProcessInfo.processInfo.activeProcessorCount)
        let run = max(Self.chunk, (points.count + tasks - 1) / tasks)
        return try await withThrowingTaskGroup(of: (Int, [Float]).self) { group in
            for start in stride(from: 0, to: points.count, by: run) {
                let slice = points[start..<min(start + run, points.count)]
                group.addTask {
                    var values: [Float] = []
                    values.reserveCapacity(slice.count)
                    let evaluation = CPUEvaluation()
                    for first in stride(from: slice.startIndex, to: slice.endIndex, by: Self.chunk) {
                        try Task.checkCancellation()
                        for point in slice[first..<min(first + Self.chunk, slice.endIndex)] {
                            values.append(Float(evaluation.distance(solid, at: Vector3(Double(point.x), Double(point.y), Double(point.z)))))
                        }
                    }
                    return (start, values)
                }
            }
            var runs: [(Int, [Float])] = []
            for try await result in group { runs.append(result) }
            return runs.sorted { $0.0 < $1.0 }.flatMap(\.1)
        }
    }

    private func evaluate(_ points: ArraySlice<SIMD3<Float>>) -> [Float] {
        let evaluation = CPUEvaluation()
        return points.map { Float(evaluation.distance(solid, at: Vector3(Double($0.x), Double($0.y), Double($0.z)))) }
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Sampling/MeshingBudget.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation

/// Why a mesh can't be made.
public enum ImplicitMeshingError: Error, Equatable, Sendable {
    /// The estimate is above half of physical memory (implicit spec §4.2).
    case tooBig(bytes: Int, resolution: Double, suggestion: Double)
    /// The solid has no surface: nothing to mesh.
    case empty

    public var userMessage: String {
        switch self {
        case .tooBig(let bytes, let resolution, let suggestion):
            let gigabytes = (Double(bytes) / 1_000_000_000).formatted(.number.precision(.fractionLength(1)).locale(.messages))
            return "This needs about \(gigabytes) GB at \(resolution.millimetreText(locale: .messages)) mm; "
                + "coarsen to \(suggestion.millimetreText(locale: .messages)) mm or split the part."
        case .empty:
            return "There is no surface to mesh: the shape is empty, or smaller than a voxel."
        }
    }
}

/// The memory a mesh will take, estimated from a coarse pass before the work starts (implicit spec §4.2).
public struct MeshingBudget: Sendable {
    /// The memory the estimate may use: half of physical memory.
    public let limit: Int

    public init(physicalMemory: UInt64 = ProcessInfo.processInfo.physicalMemory) {
        limit = Int(physicalMemory / 2)
    }

    /// Bytes per brick the surface passes through: its surface cubes (about 64 on a plane), two triangles each, with the vertex
    /// tables of the mesher (positions, normals, the edge-key map) at about 100 bytes a triangle.
    static let bytesPerBrick = 64 * 2 * 100

    /// An estimate of the bytes meshing `solid` at `resolution` takes. A coarse octree at about 64 voxels across counts the bricks the
    /// surface needs there; surface area grows with the square of the finer resolution.
    public static func estimate(for solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double,
                                using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws -> Int {
        let size = solid.bounds.size
        let coarse = max(resolution, max(size.x, size.y, size.z) / 64)
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: coarse, using: evaluator)
        let scale = (coarse / resolution) * (coarse / resolution)
        return Int((Double(plan.bricks.count) * scale * Double(bytesPerBrick)).rounded(.up))
    }

    /// Throws `ImplicitMeshingError.tooBig` when meshing `solid` at `resolution` is estimated over the limit.
    public func check(_ solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double, using evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws {
        let bytes = try await Self.estimate(for: solid, resolution: resolution, using: evaluator)
        guard bytes > limit else { return }
        // Bytes follow 1 / resolution², so the resolution that fits the limit is this much coarser.
        let suggestion = (resolution * (Double(bytes) / Double(max(limit, 1))).squareRoot() * 100).rounded(.up) / 100
        throw ImplicitMeshingError.tooBig(bytes: bytes, resolution: resolution, suggestion: suggestion)
    }
}
````

### Task 7: The manifold mesher

Mesh the bricks by marching cubes with a table generated from a written rule, global edge keys, a Newton snap and smooth normals (spec §4.3), and the schedule of the progressive preview. Every mesh the mesher makes is closed, consistently wound and vertex-manifold, checked by `MeshTopology` on random sign lattices as well as on spheres and a ring.

**Files:**

- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Meshing/ImplicitMesher.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Meshing/MarchingCubesTable.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Meshing/PreviewSchedule.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitMesherTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/MarchingCubesTableTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/PreviewScheduleTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `BrickPlan`, `ImplicitBatchEvaluator`, `MeshingBudget` (Task 6); `TriangleMesh`, `MeshTopology` (Task 2).
- Produces (the public declarations this task adds; later tasks use these exact names):

````swift
public struct ImplicitMesher: Sendable
public let evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator
public let budget: MeshingBudget
public let bricksPerBatch: Int
public init(evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator, budget: MeshingBudget = MeshingBudget(), bricksPerBatch: Int = 4096)
public func mesh(_ solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double, quality: MeshQuality, snap: Bool = true,
public struct PreviewSchedule: Sendable
public enum Target: Hashable, Sendable
public static let budgetSeconds = 1.0
public static let firstPassCells = 128.0
public static let finest = 0.05
public let target: Target
public init(_ target: Target)
public func firstResolution(for box: BoundingBox) -> Double
public func next(after resolution: Double, seconds: Double) -> Double?
````


**Notes for the implementer:**

- The table is generated, not typed (`MarchingCubesTable`): never join inside corners across a face (so both cubes draw the same outline on a shared face), then fan each loop from a corner that leaves no triangle flat in a face. The second rule matters: without it two cubes on either side of an ambiguous face can make the very same triangle, which leaves a vertex with two fans (22 of 300 random lattices failed vertex-manifoldness before it; 0 of 2,000 after).
- `ImplicitMesher.mesh` checks the memory budget for `.export` quality only; a preview pass is bounded by its coarse resolution.
- `PreviewSchedule`: the first pass is 128 cells across the largest dimension (never finer than 0.05 mm), then the voxel size halves; `.fixed(r)` stops at `r`, `.automatic` goes on while four times the last pass's time is under a second.

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitMesherTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Synchronization
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// The mesher (implicit spec §4.3, §7): invariants on every mesh, volumes against the exact shapes, determinism.
struct ImplicitMesherTests {
    func mesher(_ solid: ImplicitSolid, bricks: Int = 4096) -> ImplicitMesher {
        ImplicitMesher(evaluator: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid), bricksPerBatch: bricks)
    }

    @Test func aSphereIsAClosedManifoldWithTheRightVolume() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)
        let mesh = try await mesher(solid).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .export)
        let topology = MeshTopology(mesh)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold)
        #expect(topology.eulerCharacteristic == 2)
        let exact = 4.0 / 3 * Double.pi * 1000
        #expect(abs(mesh.signedVolume - exact) / exact < 0.005, "volume \(mesh.signedVolume) against \(exact)")
        let area = 4 * Double.pi * 100
        #expect(abs(mesh.surfaceArea - area) / area < 0.01)
        #expect(mesh.resolution == 0.5 && mesh.quality == .export && mesh.triangleCount > 1000)
    }

    @Test func theNewtonStepPutsVerticesOnTheSurfaceWithOutwardNormals() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)
        let mesh = try await mesher(solid).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .preview)
        var worst: Float = 0
        var worstAlignment: Float = 1
        for (position, normal) in zip(mesh.positions, mesh.normals) {
            worst = max(worst, abs((position * position).sum().squareRoot() - 10))
            worstAlignment = min(worstAlignment, (position / (position * position).sum().squareRoot() * normal).sum())
        }
        #expect(worst < 0.01, "worst distance from the sphere \(worst)")
        #expect(worstAlignment > 0.999, "normals point away from the centre")
        let raw = try await mesher(solid).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .preview, snap: false)
        let rawWorst = raw.positions.map { abs(($0 * $0).sum().squareRoot() - 10) }.max() ?? 0
        #expect(worst < rawWorst, "snapping helps: \(worst) against \(rawWorst)")
        #expect(raw.normals.allSatisfy { $0 == .zero })
    }

    @Test func aRingHasAHoleThroughIt() async throws {
        let ring = ImplicitSolid.subtract([.cylinder(radius: 8, halfHeight: 3), .cylinder(radius: 4, halfHeight: 5)], blend: .constant(0))
        let mesh = try await mesher(ring).mesh(ring, resolution: 0.5, quality: .export)
        let topology = MeshTopology(mesh)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold)
        #expect(topology.eulerCharacteristic == 0, "a torus has Euler characteristic 0, not \(topology.eulerCharacteristic)")
        let exact = Double.pi * (64 - 16) * 6
        #expect(abs(mesh.signedVolume - exact) / exact < 0.02, "volume \(mesh.signedVolume) against \(exact)")
    }

    @Test func twoSeparatePiecesAreTwoSurfaces() async throws {
        let far = ImplicitSolid.moved(.sphere(radius: 3), RigidMove(origin: Vector3(12, 0, 0), xAxis: .unitX, yAxis: .unitY, zAxis: .unitZ))
        let pair = ImplicitSolid.union([.sphere(radius: 3), far], blend: .constant(0))
        let mesh = try await mesher(pair).mesh(pair, resolution: 0.4, quality: .preview)
        let topology = MeshTopology(mesh)
        #expect(topology.isClosedManifold && topology.eulerCharacteristic == 4)
    }

    @Test func aSmoothBlendOfTwoBallsIsOneClosedSurface() async throws {
        let move = RigidMove(origin: Vector3(7, 0, 0), xAxis: .unitX, yAxis: .unitY, zAxis: .unitZ)
        let moved = ImplicitSolid.moved(.sphere(radius: 5), move)
        let blend = ImplicitSolid.union([.sphere(radius: 5), moved], blend: .constant(4))
        let mesh = try await mesher(blend).mesh(blend, resolution: 0.4, quality: .preview)
        #expect(MeshTopology(mesh).isClosedManifold && MeshTopology(mesh).eulerCharacteristic == 2)
    }

    @Test func aMeshIsTheSameEveryTimeAndWhateverTheBatchSize() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitFixtures.cylinder
        let first = try await mesher(solid).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .preview)
        let second = try await mesher(solid).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .preview)
        let small = try await mesher(solid, bricks: 7).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .preview)
        #expect(first.positions == second.positions && first.triangles == second.triangles)
        #expect(first.positions == small.positions && first.triangles == small.triangles)
    }

    @Test func nothingToMeshIsRefusedWithAMessage() async {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 0.01)
        await #expect(throws: ImplicitMeshingError.empty) { try await mesher(solid).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .preview) }
    }

    @Test func progressRunsFromZeroToOne() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 6)
        let seen = ProgressLog()
        _ = try await mesher(solid, bricks: 10).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.5, quality: .preview) { seen.append($0) }
        let values = seen.values
        #expect(values.first == 0 && values.last == 1)
        #expect(values == values.sorted() && values.count > 3)
    }

    @Test func anExportTooBigForMemoryIsRefusedBeforeItStarts() async throws {
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)
        let small = ImplicitMesher(evaluator: CPUBatchEvaluator(solid), budget: MeshingBudget(physicalMemory: 2_000_000))
        await #expect(throws: ImplicitMeshingError.self) { try await small.mesh(solid, resolution: 0.1, quality: .export) }
        // A preview is never refused: its passes are bounded by their coarse resolution.
        _ = try await small.mesh(solid, resolution: 1, quality: .preview)
    }

    @Test func cancellingStopsTheMesher() async {
        let solid = ImplicitFixtures.sphere
        let task = Task { try await mesher(solid).mesh(solid, resolution: 0.1, quality: .preview) }
        task.cancel()
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }
}

/// Collects progress values reported from the mesher.
final class ProgressLog: Sendable {
    private let stored = Mutex<[Double]>([])

    func append(_ value: Double) {
        stored.withLock { $0.append(value) }
    }

    var values: [Double] { stored.withLock { $0 } }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/MarchingCubesTableTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// The marching cubes table (implicit spec §4.3, §7), checked as a whole instead of entry by entry.
struct MarchingCubesTableTests {
    static let corners: [Vector3] = (0..<8).map { Vector3(Double($0 & 1), Double($0 >> 1 & 1), Double($0 >> 2 & 1)) }

    func midpoint(_ edge: UInt8) -> Vector3 {
        let (a, b) = MarchingCubesTable.edgeCorners[Int(edge)]
        return (Self.corners[a] + Self.corners[b]) * 0.5
    }

    @Test func theTrivialPatternsHaveNoSurface() {
        #expect(MarchingCubesTable.triangles[0].isEmpty && MarchingCubesTable.triangles[255].isEmpty)
        #expect(MarchingCubesTable.triangles.count == 256)
    }

    @Test func aSingleInsideCornerMakesOneTriangleFacingOutwards() throws {
        for corner in 0..<8 {
            let table = MarchingCubesTable.triangles[1 << corner]
            #expect(table.count == 3, "corner \(corner)")
            let points = table.map(midpoint)
            let normal = (points[1] - points[0]).cross(points[2] - points[0])
            // Towards the outside corners: away from the inside corner.
            let outwards = (Vector3(0.5, 0.5, 0.5) - Self.corners[corner])
            #expect(normal.dot(outwards) > 0, "corner \(corner)")
        }
    }

    @Test func swappingInsideAndOutsideFlipsTheTriangles() {
        for pattern in 1..<255 {
            let a = MarchingCubesTable.triangles[pattern], b = MarchingCubesTable.triangles[255 - pattern]
            // The same crossing edges are used; the complement is the same surface seen from the other side.
            #expect(Set(a) == Set(b), "pattern \(pattern)")
        }
    }

    @Test func everyCrossingEdgeIsUsedAndNothingElse() {
        for pattern in 1..<255 {
            let crossing = Set(MarchingCubesTable.edgeCorners.indices.filter { edge in
                let (a, b) = MarchingCubesTable.edgeCorners[edge]
                return (pattern >> a & 1) != (pattern >> b & 1)
            }.map(UInt8.init))
            #expect(Set(MarchingCubesTable.triangles[pattern]) == crossing, "pattern \(pattern)")
        }
    }

    @Test func theTwoDiagonalCornersOfAFaceAreSeparated() {
        // Corners 0 and 3 are diagonal on the z = 0 face: two triangles, one cutting each corner.
        #expect(MarchingCubesTable.triangles[0b1001].count == 6)
    }

    @Test func noTriangleLiesFlatInAFaceOfTheCube() {
        for pattern in 0..<256 {
            let table = MarchingCubesTable.triangles[pattern]
            for base in stride(from: 0, to: table.count, by: 3) {
                #expect(!MarchingCubesTable.lieOnOneFace(Int(table[base]), Int(table[base + 1]), Int(table[base + 2])),
                        "pattern \(pattern): a triangle lies in a face")
            }
        }
    }

    @Test func triangleCountsAreWithinTheKnownBound() {
        let counts = MarchingCubesTable.triangles.map { $0.count / 3 }
        #expect(counts.max() ?? 0 <= 10)
        #expect(counts.filter { $0 == 0 }.count == 2)
    }

    /// The decisive test: random lattices of signs, padded with outside, mesh into closed, consistently oriented surfaces.
    @Test(arguments: 0..<60)
    func randomSignsMakeClosedSurfaces(_ seed: Int) async throws {
        let random = SeededRandom(seed: seed, stream: 17)
        let side = 6
        var inside = [Bool](repeating: false, count: side * side * side)
        for z in 1..<side - 1 { for y in 1..<side - 1 { for x in 1..<side - 1 {
            inside[(z * side + y) * side + x] = random.unit(at: (z * side + y) * side + x) < 0.45
        } } }
        let mesh = Self.surface(of: inside, side: side)
        let topology = MeshTopology(mesh)
        #expect(topology.openEdgeCount == 0, "seed \(seed): open edges")
        #expect(topology.overfullEdgeCount == 0, "seed \(seed): edges with three faces")
        #expect(topology.inconsistentEdgeCount == 0, "seed \(seed): flipped faces")
        #expect(topology.degenerateTriangleCount == 0)
        #expect(topology.nonManifoldVertexCount == 0, "seed \(seed): a vertex with two fans")
        #expect(mesh.signedVolume > 0, "seed \(seed): wound outwards")
    }

    /// Marching cubes on a `side`³ lattice of signs, with the same global edge keys the mesher uses.
    static func surface(of inside: [Bool], side: Int) -> TriangleMesh {
        var positions: [SIMD3<Float>] = []
        var triangles: [UInt32] = []
        var found: [[Int]: UInt32] = [:]
        for z in 0..<side - 1 { for y in 0..<side - 1 { for x in 0..<side - 1 {
            var pattern = 0
            for corner in 0..<8 where inside[((z + corner >> 2) * side + y + (corner >> 1 & 1)) * side + x + (corner & 1)] {
                pattern |= 1 << corner
            }
            for edge in MarchingCubesTable.triangles[pattern] {
                let (a, b) = MarchingCubesTable.edgeCorners[Int(edge)]
                let low = [x + (a & 1), y + (a >> 1 & 1), z + (a >> 2 & 1), (a ^ b) == 1 ? 0 : ((a ^ b) == 2 ? 1 : 2)]
                if let existing = found[low] {
                    triangles.append(existing)
                } else {
                    var point = SIMD3<Float>(Float(low[0]), Float(low[1]), Float(low[2]))
                    point[low[3]] += 0.5
                    found[low] = UInt32(positions.count)
                    triangles.append(UInt32(positions.count))
                    positions.append(point)
                }
            }
        } } }
        return TriangleMesh(positions: positions, triangles: triangles)
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/PreviewScheduleTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct PreviewScheduleTests {
    let box = BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(100, 50, 20))

    @Test func theFirstPassIsOneHundredTwentyEightCellsAcross() {
        #expect(PreviewSchedule(.automatic).firstResolution(for: box) == 100.0 / 128)
        #expect(PreviewSchedule(.fixed(2)).firstResolution(for: box) == 2, "a coarse target is the only pass")
        #expect(PreviewSchedule(.fixed(0.1)).firstResolution(for: box) == 100.0 / 128)
    }

    @Test func aFixedTargetHalvesUntilItIsReached() {
        let schedule = PreviewSchedule(.fixed(0.2))
        var resolution = schedule.firstResolution(for: box)
        var passes = [resolution]
        while let next = schedule.next(after: resolution, seconds: 0.01) {
            passes.append(next)
            resolution = next
        }
        #expect(passes.count == 3 && passes.last == 0.2)
        #expect(abs(passes[1] - passes[0] / 2) < 1e-12)
    }

    @Test func automaticKeepsGoingWhileThePassesAreFast() {
        let schedule = PreviewSchedule(.automatic)
        #expect(schedule.next(after: 0.8, seconds: 0.1) == 0.4)
        #expect(schedule.next(after: 0.8, seconds: 0.3) == nil, "four times 0.3 s is over a second")
        #expect(schedule.next(after: 0.05, seconds: 0.001) == nil, "nothing finer than 0.05 mm")
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Meshing/ImplicitMesher.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import Foundation

/// Meshes an implicit solid by marching cubes over the sparse bricks of a `BrickPlan` (implicit spec §4.3). The mesh is watertight and
/// manifold by construction: the table (`MarchingCubesTable`) draws the same outline on a face from either side, and a vertex on an edge
/// of the lattice is made once, whichever brick or cube reaches it first, by a global key. Each vertex then takes one Newton step
/// towards f = 0 along the gradient (central differences), at most half a voxel, and the gradient gives its normal.
public struct ImplicitMesher: Sendable {
    public let evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator
    public let budget: MeshingBudget
    /// How many bricks are sampled and meshed at a time (implicit spec §4.2).
    public let bricksPerBatch: Int

    public init(evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator, budget: MeshingBudget = MeshingBudget(), bricksPerBatch: Int = 4096) {
        self.evaluator = evaluator
        self.budget = budget
        self.bricksPerBatch = max(1, bricksPerBatch)
    }

    /// Meshes `solid` with voxels of `resolution` millimetres.
    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - quality: recorded on the mesh. An export is first checked against the memory budget (`MeshingBudget`).
    ///   - snap: whether vertices take the Newton step (tests turn it off to see what it does).
    ///   - progress: the fraction done, 0 to 1, from any thread.
    /// - Throws: `ImplicitMeshingError` and `CancellationError`.
    public func mesh(_ solid: ImplicitSolid, resolution: Double, quality: MeshQuality, snap: Bool = true,
                     progress: @Sendable (Double) -> Void = { _ in }) async throws -> TriangleMesh {
        progress(0)
        if quality == .export { try await budget.check(solid, resolution: resolution, using: evaluator) }
        let plan = try await BrickPlan.build(for: solid, resolution: resolution, using: evaluator)
        guard !plan.bricks.isEmpty else { throw ImplicitMeshingError.empty }
        var builder = MeshBuilder(plan: plan)
        let total = plan.bricks.count
        for start in stride(from: 0, to: total, by: bricksPerBatch) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let bricks = plan.bricks[start..<min(start + bricksPerBatch, total)]
            builder.add(bricks: bricks, samples: try await plan.samples(of: bricks, using: evaluator))
            progress(0.9 * Double(min(start + bricksPerBatch, total)) / Double(total))
        }
        guard !builder.triangles.isEmpty else { throw ImplicitMeshingError.empty }
        var positions = builder.positions
        var normals = [SIMD3<Float>](repeating: .zero, count: positions.count)
        if snap { try await Self.snap(&positions, &normals, solid: solid, resolution: resolution, evaluator: evaluator) }
        progress(1)
        return TriangleMesh(positions: positions, normals: normals, triangles: builder.triangles, resolution: resolution,
                            quality: quality)
    }

    /// Moves each vertex one Newton step towards f = 0 and sets its normal from the gradient. The gradient is the central difference
    /// over a quarter of a voxel; the move is limited to half a voxel.
    private static func snap(_ positions: inout [SIMD3<Float>], _ normals: inout [SIMD3<Float>], solid: ImplicitSolid,
                             resolution: Double, evaluator: any ImplicitBatchEvaluator) async throws {
        let step = Float(resolution / 4)
        let limit = Float(resolution / 2)
        let offsets: [SIMD3<Float>] = [
            SIMD3(step, 0, 0), SIMD3(-step, 0, 0), SIMD3(0, step, 0), SIMD3(0, -step, 0), SIMD3(0, 0, step), SIMD3(0, 0, -step),
        ]
        let batch = 100_000
        for start in stride(from: 0, to: positions.count, by: batch) {
            try Task.checkCancellation()
            let range = start..<min(start + batch, positions.count)
            var points: [SIMD3<Float>] = []
            points.reserveCapacity(range.count * 7)
            for index in range {
                points.append(positions[index])
                for offset in offsets { points.append(positions[index] + offset) }
            }
            let values = try await evaluator.evaluate(points)
            for (slot, index) in range.enumerated() {
                let base = slot * 7
                let gradient = SIMD3((values[base + 1] - values[base + 2]) / (2 * step), (values[base + 3] - values[base + 4]) / (2 * step),
                                     (values[base + 5] - values[base + 6]) / (2 * step))
                let squared = (gradient * gradient).sum()
                guard squared > 1e-12 else { continue }
                normals[index] = gradient / squared.squareRoot()
                var move = gradient * (-values[base] / squared)
                let length = (move * move).sum().squareRoot()
                if length > limit { move *= limit / length }
                positions[index] += move
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Collects the vertices and triangles of the bricks meshed so far.
struct MeshBuilder {
    let plan: BrickPlan
    private(set) var positions: [SIMD3<Float>] = []
    private(set) var triangles: [UInt32] = []
    /// A lattice edge's key → the vertex on it.
    private var vertexOfEdge: [Int: UInt32] = [:]

    init(plan: BrickPlan) {
        self.plan = plan
    }

    /// Meshes the cubes of `bricks`, whose 9³ samples each are in `samples`, brick after brick.
    mutating func add(bricks: ArraySlice<SIMD3<Int32>>, samples: [Float]) {
        let size = BrickPlan.brickSize
        let side = size + 1
        let perBrick = side * side * side
        let latticeX = plan.brickCounts[0] * size + 1, latticeY = plan.brickCounts[1] * size + 1
        var corners = [Float](repeating: 0, count: 8)
        for (number, brick) in bricks.enumerated() {
            let base = number * perBrick
            for z in 0..<size {
                for y in 0..<size {
                    for x in 0..<size {
                        var pattern = 0
                        for corner in 0..<8 {
                            let value = samples[base + ((z + corner >> 2) * side + y + (corner >> 1 & 1)) * side + x + (corner & 1)]
                            corners[corner] = value
                            if value < 0 { pattern |= 1 << corner }
                        }
                        let table = MarchingCubesTable.triangles[pattern]
                        guard !table.isEmpty else { continue }
                        let lattice = SIMD3(Int(brick.x) * size + x, Int(brick.y) * size + y, Int(brick.z) * size + z)
                        for edge in table {
                            let (a, b) = MarchingCubesTable.edgeCorners[Int(edge)]
                            let low = SIMD3(lattice.x + (a & 1), lattice.y + (a >> 1 & 1), lattice.z + (a >> 2 & 1))
                            let axis = (a ^ b) == 1 ? 0 : ((a ^ b) == 2 ? 1 : 2)
                            let key = ((low.z * latticeY + low.y) * latticeX + low.x) * 3 + axis
                            if let existing = vertexOfEdge[key] {
                                triangles.append(existing)
                            } else {
                                let (fa, fb) = (corners[a], corners[b])
                                let t = Double(fa / (fa - fb))
                                let start = plan.point(low.x, low.y, low.z)
                                let step = Vector3(axis == 0 ? 1 : 0, axis == 1 ? 1 : 0, axis == 2 ? 1 : 0) * plan.resolution
                                let point = start + step * t
                                let number = UInt32(positions.count)
                                positions.append(SIMD3(Float(point.x), Float(point.y), Float(point.z)))
                                vertexOfEdge[key] = number
                                triangles.append(number)
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Meshing/MarchingCubesTable.swift`**

````swift
/// The marching cubes table (implicit spec §4.3), made once by a fixed rule instead of typed in, so its choices are written down:
///
/// - Corner `c` of a cube is at offset (c & 1, c >> 1 & 1, c >> 2 & 1) from the cube's lowest lattice point, and is *inside* when the
///   solid's value there is below 0. Edge numbers are listed in `edgeCorners`.
/// - On each of the six faces, seen from outside, the inside region's outline is drawn as directed segments with the inside on the
///   left. The face's crossings (edges whose ends differ) alternate between *exits* (an inside corner to an outside one, going
///   counter-clockwise round the face) and *entries*; each exit is joined to the crossing just before it. On a face where two inside
///   corners sit diagonally (the ambiguous case) this separates them: **inside corners are never joined across a face**. The rule
///   depends on the face's four corners alone, so the cube on the other side of the face draws the same outline, and the surface has
///   no holes and no extra edges between bricks.
/// - Following the segments from edge to edge closes into loops. Each loop is a polygon whose corners are the crossing edges. The
///   outline keeps the inside on its left, which winds a loop clockwise seen from outside the solid, so a fan from the loop's first
///   corner takes the others in reverse to wind its triangles counter-clockwise seen from outside (towards increasing values).
enum MarchingCubesTable {
    /// The two corners each of the twelve edges joins, lower corner first.
    static let edgeCorners: [(Int, Int)] = [
        (0, 1), (2, 3), (4, 5), (6, 7),
        (0, 2), (1, 3), (4, 6), (5, 7),
        (0, 4), (1, 5), (2, 6), (3, 7),
    ]

    /// For each of the 256 inside/outside patterns, the triangles as triples of edge numbers.
    static let triangles: [[UInt8]] = (0..<256).map { Self.makeTriangles(inside: $0) }

    /// The corners of each cube face in counter-clockwise order seen from outside the cube.
    private static let faces: [[Int]] = (0..<3).flatMap { axis -> [[Int]] in
        let (u, v) = ((axis + 1) % 3, (axis + 2) % 3)
        func corner(_ a: Int, _ du: Int, _ dv: Int) -> Int { a << axis | du << u | dv << v }
        // (u, v, axis) is right-handed, so (0,0) (1,0) (1,1) (0,1) runs counter-clockwise seen from +axis.
        let ring = [(0, 0), (1, 0), (1, 1), (0, 1)]
        return [
            ring.map { corner(1, $0.0, $0.1) },
            ring.reversed().map { corner(0, $0.0, $0.1) },
        ]
    }

    private static func edge(between a: Int, _ b: Int) -> Int {
        edgeCorners.firstIndex { ($0.0 == a && $0.1 == b) || ($0.0 == b && $0.1 == a) } ?? 0
    }

    private static func makeTriangles(inside pattern: Int) -> [UInt8] {
        func isInside(_ corner: Int) -> Bool { pattern >> corner & 1 == 1 }
        // next[e]: the edge the outline reaches after crossing edge `e` (e is an exit of exactly one of its two faces).
        var next: [Int: Int] = [:]
        for face in faces {
            // The crossings in counter-clockwise order: (edge, isExit).
            var crossings: [(edge: Int, exit: Bool)] = []
            for index in 0..<4 {
                let (from, to) = (face[index], face[(index + 1) % 4])
                if isInside(from) != isInside(to) { crossings.append((edge(between: from, to), isInside(from))) }
            }
            for (index, crossing) in crossings.enumerated() where crossing.exit {
                next[crossing.edge] = crossings[(index + crossings.count - 1) % crossings.count].edge
            }
        }
        var result: [UInt8] = []
        var visited = Set<Int>()
        for start in next.keys.sorted() where !visited.contains(start) {
            var loop: [Int] = []
            var edge = start
            while visited.insert(edge).inserted {
                loop.append(edge)
                edge = next[edge] ?? start
            }
            result += fan(of: loop)
        }
        return result
    }

    /// Whether three crossing edges all lie on one face of the cube. A triangle made of them lies flat in that face, and the cube on
    /// the other side could make the very same triangle: two faces on one surface, a vertex that is no longer a single fan.
    static func lieOnOneFace(_ a: Int, _ b: Int, _ c: Int) -> Bool {
        faces.contains { face in [a, b, c].allSatisfy { edge in
            let (from, to) = edgeCorners[edge]
            return face.contains(from) && face.contains(to)
        } }
    }

    /// The loop cut into triangles by a fan from one of its corners: the first corner from which no triangle lies flat in a face (there
    /// is always one, which `MarchingCubesTableTests` checks for every pattern). The triangles run in reverse of the loop.
    private static func fan(of loop: [Int]) -> [UInt8] {
        var best: [UInt8] = []
        for apex in loop.indices {
            let ring = (0..<loop.count).map { loop[(apex + $0) % loop.count] }
            let triangles = (1..<(ring.count - 1)).map { [ring[0], ring[$0 + 1], ring[$0]] }
            best = triangles.flatMap { $0.map(UInt8.init) }
            if !triangles.contains(where: { lieOnOneFace($0[0], $0[1], $0[2]) }) { return best }
        }
        return best
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Meshing/PreviewSchedule.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// The passes of a progressive preview (implicit spec §4.3): the first at 128 cells across the largest dimension, then the voxel size
/// halves each pass until the preview resolution. With `.automatic` that is the finest the last pass says meshes in under a second.
public struct PreviewSchedule: Sendable {
    /// What the passes work towards.
    public enum Target: Hashable, Sendable {
        /// The finest resolution estimated to mesh in under `PreviewSchedule.budgetSeconds`.
        case automatic
        /// A voxel size in millimetres (the Mesh node's `preview resolution`).
        case fixed(Double)
    }

    public static let budgetSeconds = 1.0
    /// The cells across the largest dimension in the first pass.
    public static let firstPassCells = 128.0
    /// The finest resolution a preview pass uses on its own.
    public static let finest = 0.05

    public let target: Target

    public init(_ target: Target) {
        self.target = target
    }

    /// The first pass's voxel size for a solid in `box`.
    public func firstResolution(for box: BoundingBox) -> Double {
        let longest = max(box.size.x, box.size.y, box.size.z)
        let coarse = max(longest / Self.firstPassCells, Self.finest)
        if case .fixed(let wanted) = target { return max(coarse, wanted) }
        return coarse
    }

    /// The voxel size of the pass after one at `resolution` that took `seconds`, or `nil` when the preview is as fine as it goes.
    /// Halving the voxel size makes four times as many surface voxels, so the next pass is estimated at four times as long.
    public func next(after resolution: Double, seconds: Double) -> Double? {
        let finer = resolution / 2
        switch target {
        case .fixed(let wanted):
            guard resolution > wanted * (1 + 1e-9) else { return nil }
            return max(finer, wanted)
        case .automatic:
            guard finer >= Self.finest, 4 * seconds < Self.budgetSeconds else { return nil }
            return finer
        }
    }
}
````

### Task 8: The GPU engine protocol, the Metal compiler and engine, and the GPU agreement suite

Generate Metal for the whole tree (one statement per node, numbers in buffers so the source depends on the tree's shape only), run it from an actor that compiles each shape once, and hold it to the CPU reference within 1e-4 mm on every node and every field kind (spec §4.1). The GPU sits behind the protocol `ImplicitGPUEngine` (Errata D11 and D13); the Metal engine is the one file that imports Metal and is compiled only under `#if canImport(Metal)`, and a test of the module's imports keeps it so. The agreement suite is skipped without a Metal device; the compiler's shape tests and the protocol's tests (against an engine that is the CPU underneath) always run.

**Files:**

- Modify: `Sources/CreatorGeometry/Random/SeededRandom.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/GPU/GPUBatchEvaluator.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/GPU/ImplicitGPUEngine.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/GPU/ImplicitGPUEngines.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Metal/CompiledImplicit.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Metal/ImplicitCompiler.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Metal/ImplicitShaderLibrary.swift`
- Create: `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Metal/MetalImplicitEngine.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/GPUAgreementTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/GPUBatchEvaluatorTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitCompilerTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitPortabilityTests.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/GPUFixtures.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/StubGPUEngine.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `ImplicitSolid`, `Grading`, `DistanceBlocks` (Task 2); `CPUEvaluation` (Task 3); `ImplicitBatchEvaluator` (Task 6); `SeededRandom.streamStart` (added here).
- Produces (the public declarations this task adds; later tasks use these exact names):

````swift
public struct GPUBatchEvaluator: ImplicitBatchEvaluator
public let engine: any ImplicitGPUEngine
public let program: CompiledImplicit
public init(engine: any ImplicitGPUEngine, program: CompiledImplicit)
public var engineName: String
public func evaluate(_ points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float]
public protocol ImplicitGPUEngine: Sendable
public func compile(_ solid: ImplicitSolid) async throws(ImplicitCompileError) -> CompiledImplicit
public enum ImplicitGPUEngines
public static var platformDefault: (any ImplicitGPUEngine)?
public struct CompiledImplicit: Sendable
public let id = UUID()
public let source: String
public let parameters: [Float]
public let integers: [Int32]
public let tables: [Int32]
public let pool: [Float]
public let images: [Float]
public static let kernelName = "evaluateImplicit"
public enum ImplicitCompileError: Error, Equatable, Sendable
public enum ImplicitCompiler
public static func compile(_ solid: ImplicitSolid) throws(ImplicitCompileError) -> CompiledImplicit
public actor MetalImplicitEngine: ImplicitGPUEngine
public static let shared: MetalImplicitEngine? = MetalImplicitEngine()
public private(set) var compileCount = 0
public nonisolated let name = "Metal"
public init?()
public var deviceName: String
public func prepare(_ program: CompiledImplicit) async throws(ImplicitCompileError)
public func evaluate(_ program: CompiledImplicit, at points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float]
public func cancel()
````


**Notes for the implementer:**

- `half` is a type in Metal: a parameter named `half` fails to compile ("cannot combine with previous 'type-name' declaration specifier"). The library uses `halfSize`.
- Pictures go to the shader in a float buffer, not an `r32Float` texture (a deviation from the spec's wording, with the same result): a variable number of textures needs argument buffers, and a buffer is read by the same `images[...]` code on every tree.
- `ImplicitGPUEngine` is named as Errata D13 gives it and declares evaluation only (`name`, `prepare`, `compile`, `evaluate`, `cancel`); the mesh-bricks requirement and the Metal kernel for it are the lane 7c-1g. `ImplicitPortabilityTests` reads the module's sources: an Apple-only import anywhere but `MetalImplicitEngine.swift`, an `MTL…` type outside it, or a `Metal*.swift` file not wholly under `#if canImport(Metal)` fails it. `ImplicitCompiler`, `CompiledImplicit` and `ImplicitShaderLibrary` stay in `Metal/` and unguarded because they only build text.
- Check by hand as well, from the worktree: `grep -rnE '^(@preconcurrency )?import (Metal|simd|CoreGraphics|os|Accelerate)' Sources/CreatorImplicit` prints only `Metal/MetalImplicitEngine.swift`, and `grep -rn 'MTL' Sources/CreatorImplicit` prints only that file. Before Task 8 the module has no GPU file at all, so Tasks 2 to 7 are portable by construction; the same test guards every later change.
- A distance to a profile has no Metal form yet: `ImplicitCompiler.compile` throws `.unsupported`, and Task 9's `ImplicitEvaluators.make` falls back to the CPU with the warning "Running on the CPU".

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/GPUAgreementTests.swift`**

````swift
#if canImport(Metal)
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// The GPU against the CPU reference (implicit spec §4.1, §7): every node and every field kind on a fixed point set, within 1e-4 mm.
/// Compiled only where Metal exists, and skipped on a machine without a Metal device.
@Suite(.enabled(if: MetalImplicitEngine.shared != nil, "needs a Metal device"))
struct GPUAgreementTests {
    static let tolerance = 1e-4

    @Test(arguments: GPUFixtures.all.map(\.name))
    func theGPUAgreesWithTheCPU(_ name: String) async throws {
        let solid = try #require(GPUFixtures.all.first { $0.name == name }?.solid)
        let engine = try #require(MetalImplicitEngine.shared)
        let evaluator = GPUBatchEvaluator(engine: engine, program: try ImplicitCompiler.compile(solid))
        let gpu = try await evaluator.evaluate(GPUFixtures.points)
        let cpu = solid.signedDistances(at: GPUFixtures.points.map { Vector3(Double($0.x), Double($0.y), Double($0.z)) })
        #expect(gpu.count == cpu.count)
        var worst = 0.0
        for index in cpu.indices { worst = max(worst, abs(Double(gpu[index]) - cpu[index])) }
        #expect(worst < Self.tolerance, "\(name): the largest difference is \(worst) mm")
        #expect(evaluator.engineName == "Metal")
    }

    @Test func shapesShareOnePipeline() async throws {
        let engine = try #require(MetalImplicitEngine())
        let first = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.sphere(radius: 5))
        let second = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.sphere(radius: 25))
        try await engine.prepare(first)
        try await engine.prepare(second)
        #expect(await engine.compileCount == 1)
        let values = try await GPUBatchEvaluator(engine: engine, program: second).evaluate([SIMD3(30, 0, 0)])
        #expect(abs(values[0] - 5) < 1e-5, "the numbers still differ")
        try await engine.prepare(try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.box(halfSize: Vector3(1, 1, 1), cornerRadius: 0)))
        #expect(await engine.compileCount == 2)
    }

    @Test func aBatchLargerThanOneDispatchIsAnswered() async throws {
        let engine = try #require(MetalImplicitEngine.shared)
        let evaluator = GPUBatchEvaluator(engine: engine, program: try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.sphere(radius: 5)))
        let points = (0..<(MetalImplicitEngine.pointsPerDispatch + 10)).map { SIMD3<Float>(Float($0 % 100), 0, 0) }
        let values = try await evaluator.evaluate(points)
        let last = points.count - 1
        #expect(values.count == points.count && abs(values[7] - 2) < 1e-5)
        #expect(abs(values[last] - Float(last % 100 - 5)) < 1e-5)
    }

    @Test func anEmptyBatchAndACancelledOneAreHandled() async throws {
        let engine = try #require(MetalImplicitEngine.shared)
        let evaluator = GPUBatchEvaluator(engine: engine, program: try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.sphere(radius: 5)))
        #expect(try await evaluator.evaluate([]).isEmpty)
        let task = Task { try await evaluator.evaluate([SIMD3(1, 2, 3)]) }
        task.cancel()
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }

    @Test func cancelEndsTheBatchesInFlightAndNotTheOnesAfter() async throws {
        let engine = try #require(MetalImplicitEngine())
        let program = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.sphere(radius: 5))
        let points = (0..<(MetalImplicitEngine.pointsPerDispatch * 3)).map { SIMD3<Float>(Float($0 % 100), 0, 0) }
        let running = Task { try await engine.evaluate(program, at: points) }
        await engine.cancel()
        // Either the cancel came before the batch started (it ran whole) or after its first dispatch (it ended): never a wrong answer.
        do {
            let values = try await running.value
            #expect(values.count == points.count)
        } catch {
            #expect(error is CancellationError)
        }
        let after = try await engine.evaluate(program, at: [SIMD3(7, 0, 0)])
        #expect(after.count == 1 && abs(after[0] - 2) < 1e-5)
    }
}
#endif
````

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// The code that depends on `ImplicitGPUEngine`, run against an engine that is the CPU underneath, so it is tested on every platform
/// (implicit spec Errata D11).
struct GPUBatchEvaluatorTests {
    @Test func aBatchGoesThroughTheEngineAndAnswersInOrder() async throws {
        let engine = StubGPUEngine()
        let program = try await engine.compile(.sphere(radius: 5))
        let evaluator = GPUBatchEvaluator(engine: engine, program: program)
        let values = try await evaluator.evaluate([SIMD3(7, 0, 0), SIMD3(0, 0, 0), SIMD3(0, 9, 0)])
        #expect(values == [2, -5, 4])
        #expect(evaluator.engineName == "Stub")
        let counts = await (engine.prepared, engine.evaluated)
        #expect(counts == (1, 1))
    }

    @Test func theDefaultCompileGeneratesAndPreparesTheProgram() async throws {
        let engine = StubGPUEngine()
        let program = try await engine.compile(.box(halfSize: Vector3(1, 1, 1), cornerRadius: 0))
        #expect(program.source.contains(CompiledImplicit.kernelName))
        let prepared = await engine.prepared
        #expect(prepared == 1)
    }

    @Test func aTreeTheGPUHasNoFormForIsReportedAsUnsupported() async {
        let profile = Profile2D.rectangle(width: 4, height: 4, plane: .xy)
        let graded = Grading(field: .distance(.profile(profile), signed: false), range: 0...10, slope: 1)
        let solid = ImplicitSolid.offset(.sphere(radius: 3), distance: graded)
        let engine = StubGPUEngine()
        await #expect(throws: ImplicitCompileError.self) { _ = try await engine.compile(solid) }
    }

    @Test func aCancelledTaskEndsTheBatch() async throws {
        let engine = StubGPUEngine()
        let program = try await engine.compile(.sphere(radius: 5))
        let evaluator = GPUBatchEvaluator(engine: engine, program: program)
        let task = Task { try await evaluator.evaluate([SIMD3(1, 2, 3)]) }
        task.cancel()
        await #expect(throws: CancellationError.self) { try await task.value }
    }

    @Test func thePlatformEngineIsMetalOrNothing() async {
        // Where Metal exists and has a device this is the Metal engine; elsewhere it is nil and the CPU does everything.
        if let engine = ImplicitGPUEngines.platformDefault {
            #expect(engine.name == "Metal")
        } else {
            #expect(ImplicitGPUEngines.platformDefault == nil)
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitCompilerTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// What the compiler generates, without a GPU (implicit spec §4.1): the source depends on the shape of the tree and never on its numbers.
struct ImplicitCompilerTests {
    func source(_ solid: ImplicitSolid) throws -> String { try ImplicitCompiler.compile(solid).source }

    @Test func editingANumberDoesNotChangeTheSource() throws {
        #expect(try source(.sphere(radius: 5)) == source(.sphere(radius: 50)))
        let a = ImplicitSolid.shell(.box(halfSize: Vector3(1, 2, 3), cornerRadius: 0), thickness: .constant(1), side: .inward)
        let b = ImplicitSolid.shell(.box(halfSize: Vector3(9, 8, 7), cornerRadius: 2), thickness: .constant(4), side: .outward)
        #expect(try source(a) == source(b), "the side is a parameter too")
        #expect(try source(.union([.sphere(radius: 1), .sphere(radius: 2)], blend: .constant(0)))
            == source(.union([.sphere(radius: 3), .sphere(radius: 4)], blend: .constant(5))))
    }

    @Test func aFieldSettingsNumbersAreNotInTheSourceEither() throws {
        func graded(_ seed: Int, _ octaves: Int, _ level: Double) -> ImplicitSolid {
            let noise = Field.noise(seed: seed, scale: 4 + level, octaves: octaves)
            let field = Field.remap(noise, fromLow: 0, fromHigh: level + 1, toLow: 0, toHigh: 1, clamped: octaves.isMultiple(of: 2))
            return .offset(.sphere(radius: 3), distance: Grading(field: field, range: 0...1, slope: 1))
        }
        #expect(try source(graded(1, 1, 0.5)) == source(graded(77, 4, 3)))
    }

    @Test func aDifferentShapeIsADifferentSource() throws {
        let sphere = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 5)
        #expect(try source(sphere) != source(.box(halfSize: Vector3(1, 1, 1), cornerRadius: 0)))
        #expect(try source(.union([sphere, sphere], blend: .constant(0))) != source(.union([sphere, sphere, sphere], blend: .constant(0))))
        #expect(try source(.union([sphere, sphere], blend: .constant(0))) != source(.intersect([sphere, sphere], blend: .constant(0))))
        let graded = Grading(field: .linearGradient(plane: .xy, length: 3), range: 0...1, slope: 0.3)
        #expect(try source(.offset(sphere, distance: .constant(1))) != source(.offset(sphere, distance: graded)),
                "a field-valued setting is another shape from a constant")
    }

    @Test func theSourceHasTheKernelAndTheLibrary() throws {
        let text = try source(.sphere(radius: 5))
        #expect(text.contains("kernel void \(CompiledImplicit.kernelName)"))
        #expect(text.contains("inline float sdf(const thread Env& e, float3 p)"))
        #expect(text.contains("inline float smoothMin") && text.contains("inline float bakedValue"))
        #expect(text.contains("length(p) - e.P[0]"), "the radius is a slot, not a number")
    }

    @Test func parametersAreInTheBuffers() throws {
        let program = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.sphere(radius: 5))
        #expect(program.parameters == [5])
        let shell = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.shell(.sphere(radius: 5), thickness: .constant(2), side: .centred))
        #expect(shell.parameters == [5, 2] && shell.integers == [Int32(ShellSide.centred.rawValue)])
    }

    @Test func bakedBlocksAreUploadedOnceHoweverOftenTheyAreUsed() throws {
        let blocks = BlocksFixture.make(origin: .zero, spacing: 1, blocks: 1) { $0.x }
        let baked = ImplicitSolid.baked(blocks, label: "a")
        let program = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.union([baked, baked, baked], blend: .constant(0)))
        #expect(program.tables.count == 1 && program.pool.count == 512 + 1)
        #expect(program.integers.count == 6)
        // 6 integers [table, pool, coarse, nx, ny, nz] and 4 floats [origin, spacing] per distinct block set, plus the blend (1).
        #expect(program.parameters.count == 4 + 1)
    }

    @Test func noiseSeedsAreInTheTablesAndPicturesInTheirOwnBuffer() throws {
        let noisy = ImplicitSolid.offset(.sphere(radius: 3), distance: GPUFixtures.graded(.noise(seed: 3, scale: 5, octaves: 3)))
        let noise = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(noisy)
        #expect(noise.tables.count == 6, "a pair of words for each octave")
        let start = SeededRandom(seed: 3, stream: 0, purpose: .fieldNoise).streamStart
        #expect(UInt32(bitPattern: noise.tables[0]) == UInt32(start & 0xFFFF_FFFF))
        #expect(UInt32(bitPattern: noise.tables[1]) == UInt32(start >> 32))
        let image = try #require(GPUFixtures.picture)
        let picture = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.offset(.sphere(radius: 3), distance: GPUFixtures.graded(
            .image(image, plane: .xy, width: 10, height: 10, edges: .clamp))))
        #expect(picture.images.count == 30 && picture.images == image.pixels.map(Float.init))
    }

    @Test func aDistanceToAProfileIsLeftToTheCPU() {
        let profile = Profile2D.rectangle(width: 4, height: 4, plane: .xy)
        let graded = Grading(field: .distance(.profile(profile), signed: false), range: 0...10, slope: 1)
        #expect(throws: ImplicitCompileError.unsupported("a distance to a profile")) {
            try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.offset(.sphere(radius: 3), distance: graded))
        }
    }

    @Test func aKernelDistanceThatWasNotBakedIsLeftToTheCPU() {
        let graded = Grading(field: .distance(.kernel(.solid(GPUFixtures.stand)), signed: true), range: 0...10, slope: 1)
        #expect(throws: ImplicitCompileError.unsupported("a distance to a solid that was not baked")) {
            try ImplicitCompiler.compile(.offset(.sphere(radius: 3), distance: graded))
        }
    }

    @Test(arguments: GPUFixtures.all.map(\.name))
    func everyFixtureCompiles(_ name: String) throws {
        let solid = try #require(GPUFixtures.all.first { $0.name == name }?.solid)
        let program = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(solid)
        #expect(program.source.contains("return v"))
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/ImplicitPortabilityTests.swift`**

````swift
import Foundation
import Testing

/// Errata D11: `CreatorImplicit` builds on Linux and Windows. Nothing in it imports an Apple-only framework except the engines that live
/// in files named `Metal*.swift`, and those are compiled only under `#if canImport(Metal)`. This reads the sources, so it runs
/// everywhere and fails the day someone adds `import simd` to the mesher.
struct ImplicitPortabilityTests {
    /// Frameworks that exist on Apple platforms only.
    static let appleOnly = [
        "Metal", "MetalKit", "MetalPerformanceShaders", "simd", "CoreGraphics", "os", "Accelerate", "QuartzCore", "AppKit", "UIKit",
        "Darwin", "CoreFoundation", "ImageIO", "CoreImage", "Security",
    ]

    static let sources: [(name: String, lines: [String])] = {
        let module = URL(filePath: #filePath).deletingLastPathComponent().deletingLastPathComponent().deletingLastPathComponent()
            .appending(path: "Sources/CreatorImplicit")
        guard let walker = FileManager.default.enumerator(at: module, includingPropertiesForKeys: nil) else { return [] }
        var files: [(String, [String])] = []
        for case let url as URL in walker where url.pathExtension == "swift" {
            let text = (try? String(contentsOf: url, encoding: .utf8)) ?? ""
            files.append((url.lastPathComponent, text.split(separator: "\n", omittingEmptySubsequences: false).map(String.init)))
        }
        return files.sorted { $0.0 < $1.0 }
    }()

    private static func imports(_ line: String) -> String? {
        let words = line.split(separator: " ").map(String.init)
        guard let at = words.firstIndex(of: "import"), at <= 1, words[..<at].allSatisfy({ $0 == "@preconcurrency" }),
              at + 1 < words.count else { return nil }
        return words[at + 1]
    }

    @Test func theModuleIsFound() {
        #expect(Self.sources.count > 20, "the sources were not found from \(#filePath)")
    }

    @Test func nothingButTheMetalEngineImportsAnAppleOnlyFramework() {
        for (name, lines) in Self.sources {
            for line in lines {
                guard let framework = Self.imports(line), Self.appleOnly.contains(framework) else { continue }
                #expect(name.hasPrefix("Metal") && framework == "Metal", "\(name) imports \(framework)")
            }
        }
    }

    @Test func aMetalFileIsWhollyUnderCanImportMetal() {
        for (name, lines) in Self.sources where name.hasPrefix("Metal") {
            let code = lines.filter { !$0.trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespaces).isEmpty && !$0.hasPrefix("//") }
            #expect(code.first == "#if canImport(Metal)" && code.last == "#endif", "\(name) must be wrapped in #if canImport(Metal)")
        }
    }

    @Test func noMetalTypeIsNamedOutsideTheMetalEngine() {
        for (name, lines) in Self.sources where !name.hasPrefix("Metal") {
            for line in lines where !line.trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespaces).hasPrefix("//") {
                #expect(line.range(of: #"\bMTL[A-Z]\w*"#, options: .regularExpression) == nil, "\(name) names a Metal type: \(line)")
            }
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/GPUFixtures.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel

/// A solid with no geometry, only an identity, for the distance targets of baked gradings.
final class StandInStorage: SolidStorage {
    var estimatedBytes: Int { 64 }
}

/// The solids the GPU is checked against the CPU on: every fixture, and one for each kind of field.
enum GPUFixtures {
    static let stand = Solid(topology: Topology(faces: [], edges: []), bounds: BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: Vector3(1, 1, 1)),
                             storage: StandInStorage())

    /// A 6 × 5 picture with a gradient and some steps.
    static let picture = GreyImage(width: 6, height: 5, pixels: (0..<30).map { UInt16(($0 * 2_017 + ($0 % 4) * 9_000) % 65_536) })

    static func graded(_ field: Field, range: ClosedRange<Double> = 0...2, baked: [Grading.Baked] = []) -> Grading {
        Grading(field: field, baked: baked, range: range, slope: max(field.slopeBound.value, 0.05))
    }

    static var bakedGrading: Grading {
        let blocks = BlocksFixture.make(origin: Vector3(-20, -20, -20), spacing: 1.25, blocks: 4) { $0.length - 8 }
        let baked = Grading.Baked(target: DistanceTarget.solid(stand).identity, signed: true, blocks: blocks)
        let field = Field.remap(.distance(.kernel(.solid(stand)), signed: true), fromLow: -8, fromHigh: 8, toLow: 0.4, toHigh: 1.6,
                                clamped: true)
        return graded(field, range: 0.4...1.6, baked: [baked])
    }

    static var all: [(name: String, solid: ImplicitSolid)] {
        let sphere = ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 10)
        let noise = Field.noise(seed: 21, scale: 7, octaves: 3)
        let stretched = Field.remap(noise, fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 1, toLow: -1, toHigh: 2, clamped: true)
        let maths = Field.smoothMinimum(
            .multiply(.clamp(.linearGradient(plane: .xy, length: 12), low: 0, high: 1),
                      .radialGradient(center: Vector3(1, 2, 3), radius: 9)),
            .remap(.distance(.point(Vector3(-2, 0, 4)), signed: false), fromLow: 0, fromHigh: 20, toLow: 1.5, toHigh: 0.5, clamped: false),
            width: 0.7)
        var fixtures = ImplicitFixtures.all
        fixtures += [
            ("noise offset", .offset(sphere, distance: graded(stretched))),
            ("noise tile image", .shell(sphere, thickness: graded(.add(.constant(0.5), picture.map {
                .image($0, plane: .xy, width: 25, height: 20, edges: .tile)
            } ?? .constant(0))), side: .outward)),
            ("clamped image", .offset(sphere, distance: graded(picture.map {
                .image($0, plane: Plane(origin: Vector3(1, -2, 0), normal: .unitZ, xAxis: Vector3(1, 1, 0)), width: 14, height: 9,
                       edges: .clamp)
            } ?? .constant(0), range: 0...1))),
            ("maths", .union([sphere, ImplicitFixtures.cylinder], blend: graded(maths, range: -5...5))),
            ("kernel distance", .shell(sphere, thickness: bakedGrading, side: .centred)),
            ("implicit leaf", .offset(sphere, distance: graded(.implicit(ImplicitSolid.sphere(radius: 4).reference), range: -4...30))),
        ]
        return fixtures
    }

    /// 600 points around the origin, from a fixed seed.
    static let points: [SIMD3<Float>] = {
        let random = SeededRandom(seed: 99, stream: 4)
        return (0..<600).map { SIMD3(Float(random.unit(at: 3 * $0) * 36 - 18), Float(random.unit(at: 3 * $0 + 1) * 36 - 18),
                                     Float(random.unit(at: 3 * $0 + 2) * 36 - 18)) }
    }()
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/Support/StubGPUEngine.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation
@testable import CreatorImplicit

/// A GPU engine that is the CPU underneath, so the code that depends on `ImplicitGPUEngine` is tested on every platform.
actor StubGPUEngine: ImplicitGPUEngine {
    /// What `prepare` throws, when set.
    private let failure: ImplicitCompileError?
    private(set) var prepared = 0
    private(set) var evaluated = 0
    private(set) var cancels = 0
    private var solids: [UUID: ImplicitSolid] = [:]

    nonisolated let name = "Stub"
    var deviceName: String { "stub device" }

    init(failure: ImplicitCompileError? = nil) {
        self.failure = failure
    }

    /// Remembers which solid a program came from, so `evaluate` can answer with the CPU.
    func compile(_ solid: ImplicitSolid) async throws(ImplicitCompileError) -> CompiledImplicit {
        let program = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(solid)
        solids[program.id] = solid
        try await prepare(program)
        return program
    }

    func prepare(_ program: CompiledImplicit) async throws(ImplicitCompileError) {
        prepared += 1
        if let failure { throw failure }
    }

    func evaluate(_ program: CompiledImplicit, at points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float] {
        try Task.checkCancellation()
        evaluated += 1
        guard let solid = solids[program.id] else { return points.map { _ in 0 } }
        return try await CPUBatchEvaluator(solid).evaluate(points)
    }

    func cancel() async {
        cancels += 1
    }
}
````

````swift
/// The fast path of `ImplicitBatchEvaluator`: a compiled solid evaluated by an `ImplicitGPUEngine`.
public struct GPUBatchEvaluator: ImplicitBatchEvaluator {
    public let engine: any ImplicitGPUEngine
    public let program: CompiledImplicit

    public init(engine: any ImplicitGPUEngine, program: CompiledImplicit) {
        self.engine = engine
        self.program = program
    }

    public var engineName: String { engine.name }

    public func evaluate(_ points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float] {
        try await engine.evaluate(program, at: points)
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/GPU/ImplicitGPUEngine.swift`**

````swift
import Foundation

/// A GPU that evaluates compiled implicit solids (implicit spec §4.1, Errata D11 and D13). The rest of the engine depends on this
/// protocol and nothing else of any GPU API: `MetalImplicitEngine` is its only implementation in 7c-1, compiled only where Metal exists,
/// and an SDL3 GPU compute engine for Linux and Windows follows (track xp-c). Where there is no engine the CPU evaluator, which is
/// complete, does everything.
///
/// D13 adds meshing bricks to this protocol (plan 7c-1g); an implementation written now needs only evaluation.
public protocol ImplicitGPUEngine: Sendable {
    /// "Metal" (or the next implementation's name), for the preview badge and the inspector.
    nonisolated var name: String { get }

    /// The device's name, for the inspector.
    var deviceName: String { get async }

    /// Compiles a tree: generates its source (`ImplicitCompiler`, which needs no GPU API) and prepares the pipeline. Throws
    /// `.unsupported` for a tree the GPU has no form for; the caller then uses the CPU. The default does exactly that.
    func compile(_ solid: ImplicitSolid) async throws(ImplicitCompileError) -> CompiledImplicit

    /// Makes the pipeline for `program`'s shape if no solid of that shape has been compiled. Programs of one shape share it.
    func prepare(_ program: CompiledImplicit) async throws(ImplicitCompileError)

    /// The value of `program` at each of `points`, in order. Throws `CancellationError` when the task is cancelled, or `cancel()` is
    /// called, between dispatches.
    func evaluate(_ program: CompiledImplicit, at points: [SIMD3<Float>]) async throws -> [Float]

    /// Ends the batches in flight with `CancellationError`. Batches started after the call run normally.
    func cancel() async
}

extension ImplicitGPUEngine {
    public func compile(_ solid: ImplicitSolid) async throws(ImplicitCompileError) -> CompiledImplicit {
        let program = try ImplicitCompiler.compile(solid)
        try await prepare(program)
        return program
    }
}
````
