````swift
import CreatorImplicit
import Foundation

/// What `SceneBuilder` needs to show meshes: the solids that display them, and the refined passes that have replaced the meshes the
/// evaluator made (`MeshRefinementModel`).
struct MeshScene: Sendable {
    let solids: MeshSolids
    /// The finest pass so far for each preview mesh, by the ID of the mesh the evaluator made.
    var refined: [UUID: TriangleMesh] = [:]

    /// The mesh to show for one the evaluator made: its refinement when it has one.
    func shown(_ mesh: TriangleMesh) -> TriangleMesh { refined[mesh.id] ?? mesh }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorApp/MeshPreview/MeshSolids.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation
import Synchronization

/// The display solid of each mesh the viewport shows (implicit spec §5.1). A mesh is shown as a `Solid` that holds only its triangles
/// (`Solid(displaying:)`), and the viewport tells solids apart by identity, so a mesh must get the same one every time the scene is
/// rebuilt or the viewport would load it again. The solid lives as long as the scene shows its mesh.
final class MeshSolids: Sendable {
    private let solids = Mutex<[UUID: Solid]>([:])

    /// The solid that shows `mesh`.
    func solid(for mesh: TriangleMesh) -> Solid {
        solids.withLock { solids in
            if let known = solids[mesh.id] { return known }
            let made = Solid(displaying: mesh.displayMesh)
            solids[mesh.id] = made
            return made
        }
    }

    /// Forgets the solids of meshes not in `ids`.
    func keep(_ ids: Set<UUID>) {
        solids.withLock { $0 = $0.filter { ids.contains($0.key) } }
    }

    var count: Int { solids.withLock { $0.count } }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorApp/MeshPreview/TriangleMesh+Display.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel

extension TriangleMesh {
    /// The mesh as the viewport draws it (implicit spec §5.1): smooth normals (the mesher's, else the average of the faces at each
    /// vertex), one face for the whole mesh, no B-rep edges.
    var displayMesh: DisplayMesh {
        let points = (0..<vertexCount).map { point($0) }
        let shading = normals.count == points.count
            ? normals.map { Vector3(Double($0.x), Double($0.y), Double($0.z)) } : averagedNormals(of: points)
        return DisplayMesh(positions: points, normals: shading, indices: triangles,
                           triangleFaces: [FaceID](repeating: FaceID(0), count: triangleCount), edgePolylines: [:])
    }

    /// Each vertex's normal as the area-weighted average of the faces around it.
    private func averagedNormals(of points: [Vector3]) -> [Vector3] {
        var sums = [Vector3](repeating: .zero, count: points.count)
        for face in 0..<triangleCount {
            let corners = (0..<3).map { Int(triangles[3 * face + $0]) }
            let normal = (points[corners[1]] - points[corners[0]]).cross(points[corners[2]] - points[corners[0]])
            for corner in corners { sums[corner] += normal }
        }
        return sums.map { $0.normalized ?? .unitZ }
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorKernel/DisplayMeshStorage.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry

/// The payload of a `Solid` that is only something to look at: a mesh the viewport draws as it draws a tessellated part (implicit
/// spec §5.1). It has no faces or edges to pick and no B-rep behind it, so no kernel operation takes one; the viewport reads the mesh
/// back instead of asking the kernel to tessellate.
public final class DisplayMeshStorage: SolidStorage {
    public let mesh: DisplayMesh

    public init(_ mesh: DisplayMesh) {
        self.mesh = mesh
    }

    public var estimatedBytes: Int { mesh.positions.count * 48 + mesh.indices.count * 4 + mesh.triangleFaces.count * 8 }
}

extension Solid {
    /// A solid that is only `mesh` on display: no topology, bounds around its vertices.
    public convenience init(displaying mesh: DisplayMesh) {
        let bounds = BoundingBox(points: mesh.positions) ?? BoundingBox(min: .zero, max: .zero)
        self.init(topology: Topology(faces: [], edges: []), bounds: bounds, storage: DisplayMeshStorage(mesh))
    }

    /// The mesh this solid is, when it is only a mesh on display.
    public var displayedMesh: DisplayMesh? { (storage as? DisplayMeshStorage)?.mesh }
}
````

### Task 15: STL of meshes, the STEP refusal, the export run and the warnings summary

Export meshes: an Output holding a mesh is evaluated again at export quality on an evaluator of its own and written as one binary STL (with any solids, tessellated); STEP refuses a mesh in the spec's words; afterwards a summary lists the warnings of the nodes the export came from.

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `MeshProgress`, `EvalContext.meshQuality` (Task 9); `OutputNode` meshes (Task 13); `AppModel.outputNodes`, `Kernel.tessellate`.
- Produces (the public declarations this task adds; later tasks use these exact names):

````swift
public final class ExportRun
public let title: String
public private(set) var fraction = 0.0
public private(set) var isCancelling = false
public init(title: String)
public func cancel()
public struct STLWriter: Sendable
public private(set) var triangleCount = 0
public init() {}
public mutating func add(_ mesh: TriangleMesh)
public mutating func add(positions: [Vector3], indices: [UInt32])
public func data() -> Data
````


**Notes for the implementer:**

- The exact refusal text is `Output 'Insole' is a mesh; STEP needs solids. Export STL or 3MF, or convert it with Mesh to Solid.` (3MF and Mesh to Solid arrive in 7c-3; the sentence is the spec's.)
- A cancelled export writes nothing. An Output that cannot be exported names the node upstream that failed (the memory refusal comes out as the Mesh node's message).
- `ExportTests.anUnwiredOutputOrAnEmptyListIsNothingToExport` changed in Task 13, because an unwired Output now runs: the message is "Nothing to export / No solid is wired into an Output node."

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp

/// Exporting meshes (implicit spec §5.3): STL written from an export-quality evaluation, STEP refusing them, a summary of warnings, and
/// Cancel.
@MainActor
struct ExportMeshTests {
    /// Sphere → Mesh (export resolution 0.5) → Output named `name`.
    func sphereGraph(radius: Double = 3, export: Double = 0.5, name: String = "Insole") -> (graph: Graph, mesh: Node, output: Node) {
        var builder = GraphBuilder()
        let sphere = builder.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(radius)])
        let mesh = builder.add(MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(export)])
        var output = builder.add(OutputNode.self)
        builder.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        builder.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: output, "mesh")
        output.name = name
        builder.replace(output)
        return (builder.graph, mesh, output)
    }

    /// A place to export to that is cleaned up when the test is done.
    final class Target {
        let url: URL

        init(_ name: String = "out.stl") {
            url = URL.temporaryDirectory.appending(path: "\(UUID().uuidString)-\(name)")
        }

        deinit {
            try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: url)
        }

        var exists: Bool { FileManager.default.fileExists(atPath: url.path) }
    }

    /// How many triangles the document's own (preview) mesh of `node` has.
    func previewTriangles(_ app: AppModel, _ node: Node) -> Int? {
        guard case .mesh(let payload)? = app.document.results[node.id]?.outputs?["mesh"]?.items.first else { return nil }
        return payload.triangleCount
    }

    func stl(_ url: URL) throws -> STLFile {
        try #require(STLFile(try Data(contentsOf: url)))
    }

    @Test func aMeshOutputIsWrittenAsStlAtExportResolution() async throws {
        let fixture = sphereGraph()
        let app = await makeApp(fixture.graph)
        let target = Target()
        let picker = ScriptedPicker([target.url])
        await app.exportDocument(.stl, using: picker)
        #expect(app.alert == nil && app.exportRun == nil)
        #expect(picker.asked.count == 1 && picker.asked[0].name == "Insole.stl")
        let file = try stl(target.url)
        // The file is the export mesh, not the preview one: a 3 mm sphere at 0.5 mm has about 2,000 triangles, a preview far more.
        #expect(file.triangles.count > 200 && file.triangles.count != previewTriangles(app, fixture.mesh))
        let exact = 4.0 / 3 * Double.pi * 27
        #expect(abs(file.volume - exact) / exact < 0.02, "volume \(file.volume) against \(exact)")
    }

    @Test func theDocumentsOwnResultsAreStillThePreview() async throws {
        let fixture = sphereGraph()
        let app = await makeApp(fixture.graph)
        let target = Target()
        try await app.export(.stl, to: target.url)
        guard case .mesh(let payload)? = app.document.results[fixture.mesh.id]?.outputs?["mesh"]?.items.first else {
            Issue.record("no mesh")
            return
        }
        #expect((payload as? TriangleMesh)?.quality == .preview)
    }

    @Test func stepRefusesAMeshInTheSpecsWords() async throws {
        let fixture = sphereGraph()
        let app = await makeApp(fixture.graph)
        let picker = ScriptedPicker([Target("out.step").url])
        await app.exportDocument(.step, using: picker)
        #expect(app.alert == .problem(AppProblem(
            "The part can't be exported as STEP",
            "Output 'Insole' is a mesh; STEP needs solids. Export STL or 3MF, or convert it with Mesh to Solid.")))
        #expect(picker.asked.isEmpty, "refused before asking where to save")
    }

    @Test func anImplicitWiredStraightIntoAnOutputIsMeshedAtTheDefaultSettings() async throws {
        var builder = GraphBuilder()
        let sphere = builder.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(1)])
        let output = builder.add(OutputNode.self)
        builder.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: output, "mesh")
        let app = await makeApp(builder.graph)
        let target = Target()
        try await app.export(.stl, to: target.url)
        // 1 mm sphere at 0.1 mm: a few hundred triangles.
        #expect(try stl(target.url).triangles.count > 200)
    }

    @Test func aSolidAndAMeshShareOneStl() async throws {
        let fixture = sphereGraph()
        var builder = GraphBuilder()
        builder.graph = fixture.graph
        let box = builder.solid()
        builder.wire(box.extrude, "solid", to: fixture.output, "solid")
        let app = await makeApp(builder.graph)
        let target = Target(), alone = Target()
        try await app.export(.stl, to: target.url)
        let meshOnly = await makeApp(fixture.graph)
        try await meshOnly.export(.stl, to: alone.url)
        let (both, justMesh) = (try stl(target.url).triangles.count, try stl(alone.url).triangles.count)
        #expect(both == justMesh + 12, "FakeKernel tessellates a box as 12 triangles")
    }

    @Test func warningsAreSummarisedAfterTheFile() async throws {
        var builder = GraphBuilder()
        let spheres = builder.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(2)])
        let gradient = builder.add(LinearGradientNode.self, ["length": .number(10)])
        let union = builder.add(ImplicitUnionNode.self)
        let output = builder.add(OutputNode.self)
        builder.wire(spheres, "implicit", to: union, "shapes")
        builder.wire(gradient, "field", to: union, "blend radius")
        builder.wire(union, "implicit", to: output, "mesh")
        let app = await makeApp(builder.graph)
        let target = Target()
        try await app.export(.stl, to: target.url)
        #expect(app.alert == .problem(AppProblem("Exported with 1 warning",
                                                 "Union: “blend radius” is below 0 in places; it is held at 0 there.")))
    }

    @Test func solidsOnlyExportsSummariseTheirWarningsToo() async throws {
        var builder = GraphBuilder()
        let box = builder.box()
        let app = await makeApp(builder.graph)
        _ = box
        #expect(Self.warnings(in: app).isEmpty)
        #expect(AppModel.warnings(in: [:], graph: Graph(), outputs: []).isEmpty)
    }

    static func warnings(in app: AppModel) -> [String] {
        AppModel.warnings(in: app.document.results, graph: app.document.graph, outputs: app.outputNodes)
    }

    @Test func aWarningOnlyCountsWhenItIsUpstreamOfAnOutput() {
        var graph = Graph()
        let upstream = BuiltInNodes.registry.makeNode(ImplicitSphereNode.typeID)
        let aside = BuiltInNodes.registry.makeNode(ImplicitBoxNode.typeID)
        let output = BuiltInNodes.registry.makeNode(OutputNode.typeID)
        for node in [upstream, aside, output] { graph.nodes[node.id] = node }
        graph.links.append(Link(from: Endpoint(node: upstream.id, socket: "implicit"), to: Endpoint(node: output.id, socket: "mesh")))
        let results: [NodeID: NodeResult] = [
            upstream.id: NodeResult(state: .warning("one\ntwo")), aside.id: NodeResult(state: .warning("elsewhere")),
            output.id: NodeResult(state: .ok(duration: .zero)),
        ]
        #expect(AppModel.warnings(in: results, graph: graph, outputs: [output.id]) == ["Sphere: one", "Sphere: two"])
    }

    @Test func theSummaryCountsInTheSingularAndThePlural() {
        let app = AppModel(kernel: FakeKernel())
        app.summarize([])
        #expect(app.alert == nil)
        app.summarize(["A: x", "B: y"])
        #expect(app.alert == .problem(AppProblem("Exported with 2 warnings", "A: x\nB: y")))
    }

    @Test func aMeshTooBigForMemoryIsRefusedWithTheNodeThatSaysSo() async throws {
        let fixture = sphereGraph(radius: 1000, export: 0.01)
        let app = await makeApp(fixture.graph)
        let target = Target()
        await app.exportDocument(.stl, using: ScriptedPicker([target.url]))
        guard case .problem(let problem)? = app.alert else { Issue.record("no alert"); return }
        #expect(problem.title == "“Mesh” can't be exported")
        #expect(problem.message.hasPrefix("This needs about ") && problem.message.hasSuffix(" mm or split the part."))
        #expect(!target.exists && app.exportRun == nil)
    }

    @Test func cancelStopsTheExportAndLeavesNoFile() async throws {
        // A 10 mm sphere at 0.05 mm takes long enough to cancel.
        let fixture = sphereGraph(radius: 10, export: 0.05)
        let app = await makeApp(fixture.graph)
        let target = Target()
        let url = target.url
        let task = Task { try await app.export(.stl, to: url) }
        var attempts = 0
        while app.exportRun == nil, attempts < 2000 {
            try await Task.sleep(for: .milliseconds(5))
            attempts += 1
        }
        let run = try #require(app.exportRun)
        #expect(run.title == "Exporting “Insole”" && !run.isCancelling)
        run.cancel()
        #expect(run.isCancelling)
        try await task.value
        #expect(app.exportRun == nil && app.alert == nil)
        #expect(!target.exists)
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Support/STLFile.swift`**

````swift
// Test fixture file: a binary STL read back, to check what an export wrote.
import Foundation

struct STLFile {
    var triangles: [(normal: SIMD3<Float>, corners: [SIMD3<Float>])]

    /// `nil` unless `data` is exactly a header, a count and that many 50-byte triangles.
    init?(_ data: Data) {
        guard data.count >= 84 else { return nil }
        let count = data.subdata(in: 80..<84).withUnsafeBytes { Int(UInt32(littleEndian: $0.loadUnaligned(as: UInt32.self))) }
        guard data.count == 84 + 50 * count else { return nil }
        func float(_ offset: Int) -> Float {
            let bits = data.subdata(in: offset..<offset + 4).withUnsafeBytes { UInt32(littleEndian: $0.loadUnaligned(as: UInt32.self)) }
            return Float(bitPattern: bits)
        }
        triangles = (0..<count).map { index in
            let base = 84 + 50 * index
            let vectors = (0..<4).map { SIMD3(float(base + 12 * $0), float(base + 12 * $0 + 4), float(base + 12 * $0 + 8)) }
            return (vectors[0], Array(vectors[1...]))
        }
    }

    /// The enclosed volume of a closed, outward-wound surface.
    var volume: Double {
        triangles.reduce(0) { sum, triangle in
            let (a, b, c) = (triangle.corners[0], triangle.corners[1], triangle.corners[2])
            let cross = SIMD3(b.y * c.z - b.z * c.y, b.z * c.x - b.x * c.z, b.x * c.y - b.y * c.x)
            return sum + Double((a * cross).sum()) / 6
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorImplicitTests/STLWriterTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorImplicit

struct STLWriterTests {
    /// A binary STL read back: its triangles' normals and corners.
    struct File {
        var header: Data
        var triangles: [(normal: SIMD3<Float>, corners: [SIMD3<Float>])]

        init?(_ data: Data) {
            guard data.count >= 84 else { return nil }
            header = data.prefix(80)
            let count = data.subdata(in: 80..<84).withUnsafeBytes { Int(UInt32(littleEndian: $0.loadUnaligned(as: UInt32.self))) }
            guard data.count == 84 + 50 * count else { return nil }
            func float(_ offset: Int) -> Float {
                let bits = data.subdata(in: offset..<offset + 4).withUnsafeBytes { UInt32(littleEndian: $0.loadUnaligned(as: UInt32.self)) }
                return Float(bitPattern: bits)
            }
            triangles = (0..<count).map { index in
                let base = 84 + 50 * index
                let vectors = (0..<4).map { SIMD3(float(base + 12 * $0), float(base + 12 * $0 + 4), float(base + 12 * $0 + 8)) }
                return (vectors[0], Array(vectors[1...]))
            }
        }
    }

    @Test func aCubeIsTwelveTrianglesOfFiftyBytes() throws {
        var writer = STLWriter()
        writer.add(MeshFixtures.cube)
        let data = writer.data()
        #expect(writer.triangleCount == 12 && data.count == 84 + 50 * 12)
        let file = try #require(File(data))
        #expect(file.triangles.count == 12)
    }

    @Test func theHeaderIsNotMistakenForAsciiAndTheCountIsLittleEndian() throws {
        var writer = STLWriter()
        writer.add(MeshFixtures.tetrahedron)
        let data = writer.data()
        #expect(data.count >= 84 && !(String(bytes: data.prefix(5), encoding: .ascii) ?? "").lowercased().hasPrefix("solid"))
        #expect(Array(data[80..<84]) == [4, 0, 0, 0])
        #expect(Array(data[(84 + 48)..<(84 + 50)]) == [0, 0], "the attribute bytes")
    }

    @Test func normalsPointOutwardAndCornersKeepTheirOrder() throws {
        var writer = STLWriter()
        writer.add(MeshFixtures.cube)
        let file = try #require(File(writer.data()))
        let centre = SIMD3<Float>(0.5, 0.5, 0.5)
        for triangle in file.triangles {
            let middle = (triangle.corners[0] + triangle.corners[1] + triangle.corners[2]) / 3
            #expect(((middle - centre) * triangle.normal).sum() > 0, "normal \(triangle.normal)")
            #expect(abs((triangle.normal * triangle.normal).sum() - 1) < 1e-6)
        }
        // The first triangle of the fixture is (0, 2, 1) of the cube's vertices.
        #expect(file.triangles[0].corners == [SIMD3(0, 0, 0), SIMD3(1, 1, 0), SIMD3(1, 0, 0)])
    }

    @Test func solidsAndMeshesShareOneFile() throws {
        var writer = STLWriter()
        writer.add(positions: [Vector3(0, 0, 0), Vector3(2, 0, 0), Vector3(0, 2, 0)], indices: [0, 1, 2])
        writer.add(MeshFixtures.tetrahedron)
        let file = try #require(File(writer.data()))
        #expect(file.triangles.count == 5)
        #expect(file.triangles[0].normal == SIMD3(0, 0, 1) && file.triangles[0].corners[1] == SIMD3(2, 0, 0))
    }

    @Test func aTriangleWithNoAreaGetsAZeroNormal() throws {
        var writer = STLWriter()
        writer.add(positions: [Vector3(0, 0, 0), Vector3(1, 0, 0), Vector3(2, 0, 0)], indices: [0, 1, 2])
        let file = try #require(File(writer.data()))
        #expect(file.triangles[0].normal == .zero)
    }

    @Test func anEmptyWriterIsAValidEmptyFile() throws {
        let data = STLWriter().data()
        #expect(data.count == 84 && File(data)?.triangles.isEmpty == true)
    }
}
````

````diff
+    // MARK: Meshes
+
+    /// What an export run of the graph made.
+    private struct Collected {
+        var solids: [Solid] = []
+        var meshes: [TriangleMesh] = []
+        var warnings: [String] = []
+    }
+
+    /// The tolerance solids are tessellated at when they share an STL file with meshes, in millimetres.
+    private static let stlTolerance = 0.01
+
+    private func exportMeshes(to url: URL) async throws(AppProblem) {
+        let run = ExportRun(title: "Exporting “\(exportName)”")
+        exportRun = run
+        defer { exportRun = nil }
+        let work = Task { try await evaluateForExport(run) }
+        run.onCancel = { work.cancel() }
+        let collected: Collected
+        do {
+            collected = try await work.value
+        } catch is CancellationError {
+            return
+        } catch let problem as AppProblem {
+            throw problem
+        } catch {
+            throw AppProblem("The part couldn't be exported", Evaluator.message(for: error))
+        }
+        do {
+            var writer = STLWriter()
+            for solid in collected.solids {
+                let mesh = try await kernel.tessellate(solid, tolerance: Self.stlTolerance)
+                writer.add(positions: mesh.positions, indices: mesh.indices)
+            }
+            for mesh in collected.meshes { writer.add(mesh) }
+            try writer.data().write(to: url, options: .atomic)
+        } catch {
+            throw AppProblem("The STL file couldn't be written", Evaluator.message(for: error))
+        }
+        summarize(collected.warnings)
+    }
+
+    /// Evaluates the graph to the Output nodes at export quality on an evaluator of its own (the document's keeps its preview results),
+    /// reporting meshing progress to `run`.
+    private func evaluateForExport(_ run: ExportRun) async throws -> Collected {
+        let evaluator = Evaluator(registry: document.registry, kernel: kernel, implicitEngine: implicitEngine)
+        let progress = MeshProgress { fraction in Task { @MainActor in run.report(fraction) } }
+        let outputs = outputNodes
+        let report = try await evaluator.evaluate(document.graph, definitions: document.definitions, demand: Set(outputs),
+                                                  images: document.imageResolver, meshQuality: .export, progress: progress)
+        let graph = document.graph
+        var collected = Collected()
+        for id in outputs {
+            guard let output = graph.nodes[id] else { continue }
+            guard let result = report.results[id], result.state.isSuccess else {
+                // The Output only says it is waiting; the node that failed upstream says why.
+                let failed = graph.upstreamClosure(of: id).sorted().compactMap { node -> (Node, String)? in
+                    guard let node = graph.nodes[node], case .error(let message)? = report.results[node.id]?.state else { return nil }
+                    return (node, message)
+                }.first
+                throw AppProblem("“\(failed?.0.name ?? output.name)” can't be exported",
+                                 failed?.1 ?? Self.reason(report.results[id]?.state))
+            }
+            for case .solid(let solid) in result.outputs?["solid"]?.items ?? [] { collected.solids.append(solid) }
+            for case .mesh(let payload) in result.outputs?["mesh"]?.items ?? [] {
+                if let mesh = payload as? TriangleMesh { collected.meshes.append(mesh) }
+            }
+        }
+        collected.warnings = Self.warnings(in: report.results, graph: graph, outputs: outputs)
+        return collected
+    }
+
+    // MARK: Warnings
+
+    /// The warnings of the nodes the Output nodes come from, each as "Node name: message", in node order.
+    static func warnings(in results: [NodeID: NodeResult], graph: Graph, outputs: [NodeID]) -> [String] {
+        let nodes = outputs.reduce(into: Set<NodeID>()) { $0.formUnion(graph.upstreamClosure(of: $1)) }
+        return nodes.sorted().flatMap { id -> [String] in
+            guard let node = graph.nodes[id], case .warning(let message)? = results[id]?.state else { return [] }
+            return message.split(separator: "\n").map { "\(node.name): \($0)" }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// "Exported with 2 warnings", listing them, once the file is written; nothing when there are none.
+    func summarize(_ warnings: [String]) {
+        guard !warnings.isEmpty else { return }
+        let count = warnings.count.formatted(.number.locale(.messages))
+        alert = .problem(AppProblem("Exported with \(count) \(warnings.count == 1 ? "warning" : "warnings")",
+                                    warnings.joined(separator: "\n")))
+    }
 }
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorApp/Export/ExportRun.swift`**

````swift
import Foundation
import Observation

/// An export in progress (implicit spec §5.3): what the export sheet shows, a progress bar and Cancel. The export evaluates the graph
/// at full resolution, which can take a while; `fraction` is how far the meshing that is running has got, and `cancel()` stops it
/// between batches of bricks, leaving no file behind.
@MainActor
@Observable
public final class ExportRun {
    /// What is being exported: "Exporting “Insole”".
    public let title: String
    /// How far the meshing now running has got, from 0 to 1.
    public private(set) var fraction = 0.0
    /// True once Cancel has been pressed and the work has not yet stopped.
    public private(set) var isCancelling = false
    /// What stops the work; set by whoever started it.
    @ObservationIgnored var onCancel: (@MainActor () -> Void)?

    public init(title: String) {
        self.title = title
    }

    func report(_ fraction: Double) {
        guard !isCancelling else { return }
        self.fraction = min(max(fraction, 0), 1)
    }

    /// Stops the export.
    public func cancel() {
        isCancelling = true
        onCancel?()
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorImplicit/Export/STLWriter.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import Foundation

/// Writes binary STL (implicit spec §5.3): the 80-byte header, the triangle count, and for each triangle its normal, its three corners
/// and a two-byte attribute, all little-endian `Float`s in millimetres. Meshes and tessellated solids add to one writer and come out
/// as one file. A triangle's normal is computed from its corners (counter-clockwise seen from outside); a triangle with no area gets a
/// zero normal, which readers take as "work it out".
public struct STLWriter: Sendable {
    /// The header: no "solid" at the start, which would make some readers take the file for ASCII STL.
    static let header: [UInt8] = {
        var bytes = Array("MetalCreator binary STL, millimetres".utf8)
        bytes += [UInt8](repeating: 0, count: 80 - bytes.count)
        return bytes
    }()

    public private(set) var triangleCount = 0
    private var body = Data()

    public init() {}

    /// Adds every triangle of `mesh`.
    public mutating func add(_ mesh: TriangleMesh) {
        body.reserveCapacity(body.count + 50 * mesh.triangleCount)
        for index in 0..<mesh.triangleCount {
            let corners = (0..<3).map { mesh.positions[Int(mesh.triangles[3 * index + $0])] }
            append(corners[0], corners[1], corners[2])
        }
    }

    /// Adds the triangles `indices` (three per triangle) make of `positions`, such as a solid's tessellation.
    public mutating func add(positions: [Vector3], indices: [UInt32]) {
        body.reserveCapacity(body.count + 50 * (indices.count / 3))
        for base in stride(from: 0, to: indices.count - 2, by: 3) {
            let corners = (0..<3).map { positions[Int(indices[base + $0])] }
            append(SIMD3(Float(corners[0].x), Float(corners[0].y), Float(corners[0].z)),
                   SIMD3(Float(corners[1].x), Float(corners[1].y), Float(corners[1].z)),
                   SIMD3(Float(corners[2].x), Float(corners[2].y), Float(corners[2].z)))
        }
    }

    private mutating func append(_ a: SIMD3<Float>, _ b: SIMD3<Float>, _ c: SIMD3<Float>) {
        let (u, v) = (b - a, c - a)
        let cross = SIMD3(u.y * v.z - u.z * v.y, u.z * v.x - u.x * v.z, u.x * v.y - u.y * v.x)
        let length = (cross * cross).sum().squareRoot()
        let normal = length > 0 ? cross / length : SIMD3<Float>.zero
        for vector in [normal, a, b, c] {
            for component in [vector.x, vector.y, vector.z] {
                withUnsafeBytes(of: component.bitPattern.littleEndian) { body.append(contentsOf: $0) }
            }
        }
        body.append(contentsOf: [0, 0])
        triangleCount += 1
    }

    /// The finished file.
    public func data() -> Data {
        var file = Data(Self.header)
        withUnsafeBytes(of: UInt32(triangleCount).littleEndian) { file.append(contentsOf: $0) }
        file.append(body)
        return file
    }
}
````

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorNodes
import Foundation
import MetalUI
import MetalUITesting
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp

/// The export sheet in the app's real window (implicit spec §5.3): it appears while a mesh exports, its Cancel stops the export, and it
/// goes when the export ends. How it looks is a human check (group IM).
@MainActor
struct ExportSheetWindowTests {
    /// A sphere of radius 10 meshed at 0.05 mm on export: long enough to cancel.
    func open() async throws -> AppWindow {
        var builder = GraphBuilder()
        let sphere = builder.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(10)])
        let mesh = builder.add(MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(0.05)])
        let output = builder.add(OutputNode.self)
        builder.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        builder.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: output, "mesh")
        return try await AppWindow.open(builder.graph)
    }

    func waitForRun(_ app: AppWindow) async throws -> ExportRun {
        var attempts = 0
        while app.model.exportRun == nil, attempts < 2000 {
            try await Task.sleep(for: .milliseconds(5))
            attempts += 1
        }
        return try #require(app.model.exportRun)
    }

    func controls(_ label: String, in app: AppWindow) throws -> [TestElement] {
        try app.window.elements { $0.label == label && $0.role != .staticText }
    }

    @Test func noSheetWithoutAnExport() async throws {
        let app = try await open()
        #expect(try controls("Cancel", in: app).isEmpty)
        #expect(try app.window.elements { $0.label == "Exporting “Output”" || $0.value == "Exporting “Output”" }.isEmpty)
    }

    @Test func theSheetShowsTheExportAndItsCancelStopsIt() async throws {
        let app = try await open()
        let url = URL.temporaryDirectory.appending(path: "\(UUID().uuidString).stl")
        let task = Task { try await app.model.export(.stl, to: url) }
        let run = try await waitForRun(app)
        try await app.window.runUntilIdle()
        let title = run.title
        #expect(try app.window.elements { $0.label == title || $0.value == title }.count >= 1)
        let cancel = try #require(try controls("Cancel", in: app).first)
        try app.window.click(cancel)
        #expect(run.isCancelling)
        try await task.value
        try await app.window.runUntilIdle()
        #expect(app.model.exportRun == nil && !FileManager.default.fileExists(atPath: url.path))
        #expect(try controls("Cancel", in: app).isEmpty, "the sheet goes with the export")
    }
}
````

````swift
import MetalUI

/// Puts the export sheet in the middle of the window while an export runs (`AppModel.exportRun`); nothing when none does. It fills
/// the window so the card can stand in the middle; only the card itself is drawn.
struct ExportSheetDock: Component {
    let model: AppModel

    var content: some ElementGroup {
        if let run = model.exportRun {
            VStack(spacing: Pixels(0)) {
                Spacer()
                HStack(spacing: Pixels(0)) {
                    Spacer()
                    ExportSheetView(run: run)
                    Spacer()
                }
                Spacer()
            }
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Sources/CreatorApp/Export/ExportSheetView.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorEditor
import CreatorStyle
import MetalUI

/// The export sheet (implicit spec §5.3): what is being exported, a bar for how far the meshing has got, and Cancel. All behaviour is
/// `ExportRun`'s; this draws it.
struct ExportSheetView: Component {
    let run: ExportRun
    @Environment(ThemeStore.self) var themes: ThemeStore?

    /// The card's inner width in points.
    static let width = 320.0

    var content: some ElementGroup {
        GlassPanel(.exportSheet) {
            VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: Pixels(10)) {
                Text(run.title)
                    .font(.headline)
                    .foregroundStyle(Palette(themes).primaryText.color)
                ProgressView(value: run.fraction)
                Text(run.isCancelling ? "Cancelling…" : "Meshing at full resolution")
                    .font(.caption)
                    .foregroundStyle(Palette(themes).secondaryText.color)
                HStack(spacing: Pixels(0)) {
                    Spacer()
                    Button("Cancel") { run.cancel() }
                        .keyboardShortcut(.escape, modifiers: [])
                        .disabled(run.isCancelling)
                }
            }
            .frame(width: Self.width.px)
        }
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorGraphTests/Implicit/ImplicitFileTests.swift`**

````swift
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorGraph
@testable import CreatorKernel

/// File format 8 (implicit spec §5.4): `implicit` and `mesh` sockets on a group's interface, and files of formats 1 to 7 opening
/// unchanged.
struct ImplicitFileTests {
    func reopened(_ file: GraphFile) throws -> GraphFile {
        try GraphFileIO.decode(try GraphFileIO.encode(file), registry: testRegistry)
    }

    @Test func theFormatIsEight() {
        #expect(GraphFile.currentFormatVersion == 8)
    }

    @Test func aGroupInterfaceMayCarryImplicitAndMeshSockets() throws {
        var group = GroupFixture()
        group.definition.inputs = [SocketSpec("shape", .implicit), SocketSpec("count", .integer, defaultValue: .integer(2))]
        group.definition.outputs = [SocketSpec("solid", .implicit), SocketSpec("surface", .mesh, access: .list, optional: true)]
        let file = GraphFile(graph: Graph(), definitions: [group.definition.id: group.definition])
        let back = try reopened(file)
        #expect(back.formatVersion == 8)
        #expect(back.definitions[group.definition.id]?.inputs == group.definition.inputs)
        #expect(back.definitions[group.definition.id]?.outputs == group.definition.outputs)
        let text = try #require(String(bytes: try GraphFileIO.encode(file), encoding: .utf8))
        #expect(text.contains("\"implicit\"") && text.contains("\"mesh\""))
    }

    @Test func theSocketTypesKeepTheirNamesInFiles() throws {
        let encoded = try JSONEncoder().encode([SocketType.implicit, SocketType.mesh])
        #expect(String(bytes: encoded, encoding: .utf8) == #"["implicit","mesh"]"#)
        #expect(try JSONDecoder().decode([SocketType].self, from: encoded) == [.implicit, .mesh])
    }

    @Test(arguments: 1...7)
    func aFileOfAnyEarlierFormatOpensUnchangedAndSavesAsEight(_ version: Int) throws {
        let id = NodeID()
        let json = """
        {"formatVersion": \(version), "graph": {"nodes": [{"id": "\(id.rawValue.uuidString)", "typeID": "test.constant",
        "typeVersion": 1, "name": "Constant", "position": {"x": 0, "y": 0}, "isOutput": false,
        "inputValues": {"value": {"type": "number", "value": 4}}}], "links": []}}
        """
        let file = try GraphFileIO.decode(Data(json.utf8), registry: testRegistry)
        #expect(file.formatVersion == version && file.graph.nodes[id]?.inputValues["value"] == .number(4))
        let saved = try reopened(GraphFile(graph: file.graph, definitions: file.definitions, viewState: file.viewState))
        #expect(saved.formatVersion == 8 && saved.graph == file.graph)
    }
}

/// A one-socket group definition, to put sockets on.
private struct GroupFixture {
    var definition = GroupDefinition(id: GroupID(), name: "Shape", inputs: [], outputs: [], graph: Graph())
}
````

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorNodes

/// A document of Implicit and Mesh nodes saved and reopened (implicit spec §5.4): their settings are the existing integer and number
/// settings, and the solids and meshes themselves are recomputed, never saved.
struct ImplicitDocumentFileTests {
    func graph(_ h: inout Harness) -> (sphere: Node, shell: Node, mesh: Node, output: Node) {
        let sphere = h.add(ImplicitSphereNode.self, ["radius": .number(7)])
        let shell = h.add(ImplicitShellNode.self, ["thickness": .number(1.5), "side": .integer(2)])
        let mesh = h.add(MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(0.25), "preview resolution": .number(2)])
        let output = h.add(OutputNode.self, output: true)
        h.wire(sphere, "implicit", to: shell, "implicit")
        h.wire(shell, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        h.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: output, "mesh")
        return (sphere, shell, mesh, output)
    }

    @Test func theSettingsSurviveASaveAndReopen() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let nodes = graph(&h)
        let data = try GraphFileIO.encode(GraphFile(graph: h.graph))
        let file = try GraphFileIO.decode(data, registry: BuiltInNodes.registry)
        #expect(file.formatVersion == 8 && file.graph.nodes == h.graph.nodes)
        #expect(Set(file.graph.links) == Set(h.graph.links), "the links are kept in an order of their own")
        #expect(file.graph.nodes[nodes.shell.id]?.inputValues["side"] == .integer(2))
        #expect(file.graph.nodes[nodes.mesh.id]?.inputValues["export resolution"] == .number(0.25))
        let report = try await Evaluator(registry: BuiltInNodes.registry, kernel: FakeKernel())
            .evaluate(file.graph, demand: [nodes.output.id])
        let mesh = try #require(report.value(nodes.output, "mesh")?.triangleMeshes?.first)
        #expect(mesh.resolution == 2 && MeshTopology(mesh).isClosedManifold)
    }

    @Test func noSolidOrMeshIsWrittenToTheFile() throws {
        var h = Harness()
        _ = graph(&h)
        let text = try #require(String(bytes: try GraphFileIO.encode(GraphFile(graph: h.graph)), encoding: .utf8))
        #expect(!text.contains("positions") && !text.contains("triangles") && !text.contains("halfSize"))
        #expect(text.contains("creator.implicitSphere") && text.contains("creator.mesh"))
    }

    @Test func aFormatSevenFileWithoutImplicitNodesOpensAndEvaluatesAsBefore() async throws {
        var h = Harness()
        let box = h.box(10, 10, 10)
        let output = h.add(OutputNode.self, output: true)
        h.wire(box, "solid", to: output, "solid")
        let seven = GraphFile(formatVersion: 7, graph: h.graph)
        let file = try GraphFileIO.decode(try GraphFileIO.encode(seven), registry: BuiltInNodes.registry)
        #expect(file.formatVersion == 7)
        let report = try await Evaluator(registry: BuiltInNodes.registry, kernel: FakeKernel())
            .evaluate(file.graph, demand: [output.id])
        #expect(report.value(output, "solid")?.solids?.count == 1)
        #expect(report.value(output, "mesh")?.items.isEmpty == true, "the Output's new socket is simply empty")
    }
}
````

**Modify `Sources/CreatorGraph/GraphFile.swift`**

````diff
@@ -11,8 +11,11 @@ public struct GraphFile: Sendable, Codable, Equatable {
     /// files still load unchanged. Canvas comments (`Graph.stickies`, `Graph.frames`) add optional keys under 5: no bump.
     /// 7: the Image Field's `image` setting (`ConstantValue.image`) and the optional top-level `images` table of embedded
     /// pictures (fields spec §5), and a group interface may now carry a `field` socket (7b-1 refused it under 6); a
-    /// version-6 reader can decode none of these. Version-1 to -6 files open unchanged and are written as 7.
-    public static let currentFormatVersion = 7
+    /// version-6 reader can decode none of these. 8: the `implicit` and `mesh` socket types may appear on a group's interface
+    /// (implicit spec §5.4), and the Implicit and Mesh nodes keep their settings (Shell's side, Mesh's resolutions) as the existing
+    /// integer and number settings; a version-7 reader would show them as sockets it cannot type. Implicit solids and meshes are
+    /// computed, never saved. Version-1 to -7 files open unchanged and are written as 8.
+    public static let currentFormatVersion = 8
 
     public var formatVersion: Int
     public var graph: Graph
````

### Task 18: ImplicitBench rows i-1 to i-4

The benchmarks of spec §6 that belong to 7c-1, run only by `scripts/bench.sh ImplicitBench` (`METALCREATOR_BENCH=1`, release), with fixtures checked in the default run. Row i-1's gyroid is not available until 7c-2, so the fixture is a sheet of the same kind: the band of a noise field between two levels, held to a cube.

**Files:**

- Create: `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBench.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBenchFixtures.swift`
- Create: `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBenchFixturesTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `Evaluator`, `ImplicitBakeCache`, `AppModel.export` (Tasks 5, 9, 15); the existing `Bench`, `BenchSamples`, `makeBracketBenchApp`.

**Notes for the implementer:**

- A gyroid sheet of 8 mm cells in a 100 mm cube has about 150 million triangles at 0.1 mm (772,000 mm² of surface): more than an in-memory mesh holds in 4 GB. Row i-4's target is only reachable for a sparse sheet, so the fixture uses 50 mm noise; 7c-2 must choose its cell size knowing that. The user has decided the target stays and the export streams to the file (Errata D12), but the streaming writer is 7c-3's, so i-4 is expected to miss here and is recorded as a miss. i-1 and i-2 are budgets for meshing on the GPU (D13, the lane 7c-1g); here they measure Metal evaluation with the CPU mesher, and i-2 is expected to miss. See the risks and U5 and U6 in the header.
- i-1 is judged on a fresh evaluator with the shaders already compiled; the very first pass of the process (which compiles them) is printed too. i-2 edits the gap between the two levels on a warm evaluator.
- After the steps below, run the benchmarks once for real: `scripts/bench.sh ImplicitBench` (a release build of everything, several minutes; then four `BENCH i-…` lines and the peak memory). It also compiles the whole test target in release, which has crashed the Swift 6.4 optimizer on one shape of async test before (see the risks): if it does, restructure that test. Keep its output for Task 19.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBench.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import CreatorOCCT
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp

/// Implicit spec §6, rows i-1 to i-4 (i-5 and i-6 belong to 7c-3). Release, idle Mac, `scripts/bench.sh ImplicitBench`; each prints
/// `BENCH i-…` lines, with a row's budget judged on the median:
/// - i-1: the first preview pass of the sheet (a noise sheet through a 100 mm cube; see `ImplicitBenchFixtures`) on a fresh
///   evaluator, the GPU's pipelines already compiled: under 1 s. The very first pass of the process, which compiles them, is printed
///   too and not judged.
/// - i-2: a thickness edit (the gap between the two levels) on a warm evaluator, so the noise and the cube are cached and no shader
///   is recompiled: one coarse pass under 100 ms.
/// - i-3: To Implicit of the slice's bracket on OCCT at the default tolerance, the bake cache emptied each time: under 500 ms.
/// - i-4: the export of i-1 at 0.1 mm to an STL, the whole pipeline: under 60 s and a peak memory under 4 GB.
@MainActor
@Suite(.serialized, .enabled(if: Bench.isRequested, "set METALCREATOR_BENCH=1 (scripts/bench.sh)"))
struct ImplicitBench {
    static let firstPassBudget = 1000.0
    static let editBudget = 100.0
    static let bakeBudget = 500.0
    static let exportBudget = 60_000.0
    static let memoryBudget = 4_000_000_000

    @Test func theFirstPreviewPassOfASheetFillingACube() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let sheet = ImplicitBenchFixtures.sheet()
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var cold = BenchSamples("i-1 first preview pass of a 100 mm sheet, cold (shaders compiled)")
        var warm = BenchSamples("i-1 first preview pass of a 100 mm sheet")
        for round in 0..<8 {
            let evaluator = Evaluator(registry: BuiltInNodes.registry, kernel: FakeKernel(), implicitEngine: .automatic)
            let start = clock.now
            let report = try await evaluator.evaluate(sheet.graph, demand: [sheet.output.id])
            if round == 0 { cold.append(from: start, to: clock.now) } else if round >= 2 { warm.append(from: start, to: clock.now) }
            #expect(report.results[sheet.output.id]?.state.isSuccess == true)
        }
        print(cold.line())
        print(warm.line(budget: Self.firstPassBudget))
    }

    @Test func aThicknessEditIsOneCoarsePassWithNoRecompile() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let sheet = ImplicitBenchFixtures.sheet()
        let evaluator = Evaluator(registry: BuiltInNodes.registry, kernel: FakeKernel(), implicitEngine: .automatic)
        _ = try await evaluator.evaluate(sheet.graph, demand: [sheet.output.id])
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var samples = BenchSamples("i-2 thickness edit of a 100 mm sheet (one coarse pass, no recompile)")
        var graph = sheet.graph
        for round in 0..<25 {
            graph.nodes[sheet.levels.id]?.inputValues["step"] = .number(-0.1 - 0.001 * Double(round + 1))
            let start = clock.now
            let report = try await evaluator.evaluate(graph, demand: [sheet.output.id])
            guard round >= 3 else { continue }
            samples.append(from: start, to: clock.now)
            #expect(report.evaluatedNodes.count == 5)
        }
        print(samples.line(budget: Self.editBudget))
    }

    @Test func toImplicitOfTheBracket() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let (app, bracket) = try await makeBracketBenchApp()
        let solid = try #require(solids(app, bracket.output).first)
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        var samples = BenchSamples("i-3 To Implicit of the bracket (bake, OCCT)")
        for round in 0..<13 {
            let cache = ImplicitBakeCache()  // empty each time, so every round bakes
            let start = clock.now
            let baked = try await cache.baked(for: solid, tolerance: 0.01, kernel: app.kernel)
            guard round >= 3 else { continue }
            samples.append(from: start, to: clock.now)
            #expect(baked.blocks.storedBlockCount > 0)
        }
        print(samples.line(budget: Self.bakeBudget))
    }

    @Test func exportingTheSheetAtATenthOfAMillimetre() async throws {
        guard Bench.requireRelease() else { return }
        let sheet = ImplicitBenchFixtures.sheet(exportResolution: 0.1)
        let app = AppModel(kernel: FakeKernel(), file: GraphFile(graph: sheet.graph), implicitEngine: .automatic)
        await app.settle()
        let url = URL.temporaryDirectory.appending(path: "\(UUID().uuidString).stl")
        defer { try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: url) }
        let clock = ContinuousClock()
        let start = clock.now
        try await app.export(.stl, to: url)
        var samples = BenchSamples("i-4 export of the 100 mm sheet at 0.1 mm (STL, whole pipeline)")
        samples.append(from: start, to: clock.now)
        let size = (try? FileManager.default.attributesOfItem(atPath: url.path)[.size] as? Int) ?? 0
        #expect(size > 84, "the file has triangles")
        print(samples.line(budget: Self.exportBudget))
        let peak = ImplicitBenchFixtures.peakMemory
        let gigabytes = (Double(peak) / 1_000_000_000).formatted(.number.precision(.fractionLength(2)))
        let verdict = peak <= Self.memoryBudget ? "met" : "missed"
        print("BENCH i-4 peak memory \(gigabytes) GB (file \(size / 1_000_000) MB); budget 4.00 GB: \(verdict)")
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBenchFixtures.swift`**

````swift
// Test fixture file: the graphs and numbers of the implicit benchmarks (implicit spec §6), checked in the default run.
import CreatorGeometry
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp

/// What `ImplicitBench` measures. Row i-1's "gyroid sheet" is a sheet of the same cost class until the lattice nodes exist (7c-2): the
/// band of a noise field between two levels (one isosurface cut from another), held to a cube: a thin curved sheet through a 100 mm cube,
/// where each point costs two noise evaluations and a gyroid's costs one trigonometric sum. 7c-2 swaps this for the TPMS node and keeps
/// the rows' ids and targets.
@MainActor
enum ImplicitBenchFixtures {
    struct Sheet {
        var graph: Graph
        /// The Series node that makes the two levels, outer then inner: its `step` is the sheet's thickness in noise units.
        var levels: Node
        var output: Node
    }

    /// The noise the sheet follows: features 50 mm across, one octave, so a 100 mm cube holds a sheet or two. (A gyroid of 8 mm cells
    /// has about 150 million triangles at 0.1 mm, more than any in-memory mesh holds in 4 GB: row i-4's target needs a sparse sheet, and
    /// 7c-2 must choose its cell size knowing that.)
    static let noiseScale = 50.0
    static let noiseOctaves = 1
    static let outerLevel = 0.55
    static let thickness = 0.1

    /// A sheet through a cube of `side` millimetres, its noise features `scale` across, meshed at `exportResolution` for export.
    static func sheet(side: Double = 100, scale: Double = noiseScale, exportResolution: Double = 0.1) -> Sheet {
        var builder = GraphBuilder()
        let noise = builder.add(NoiseFieldNode.self, ["seed": .integer(1), "scale": .number(scale), "octaves": .integer(noiseOctaves)])
        let cube = builder.add(ImplicitBoxNode.self, ["size": .vector(Vector3(side, side, side))])
        let levels = builder.add(SeriesNode.self, ["start": .number(outerLevel), "step": .number(-thickness), "count": .integer(2)])
        // The slope the noise declares, held a little above so each isosurface's lipschitz bound is 1.
        let slope = (FieldNoise.slopeBound(scale: scale, octaves: noiseOctaves) * 100).rounded(.up) / 100
        let iso = builder.add(IsosurfaceNode.self, ["max slope": .number(slope)])
        let band = builder.add(ImplicitSubtractNode.self)
        let mesh = builder.add(MeshNode.self, ["export resolution": .number(exportResolution)])
        let output = builder.add(OutputNode.self)
        builder.wire(noise, "field", to: iso, "field")
        builder.wire(cube, "implicit", to: iso, "bounds")
        // Two levels make a list of two isosurfaces; Subtract cuts the second (the lower level) from the first.
        builder.wire(levels, "values", to: iso, "level")
        builder.wire(iso, "implicit", to: band, "shapes")
        builder.wire(band, "implicit", to: mesh, "implicit")
        builder.wire(mesh, "mesh", to: output, "mesh")
        return Sheet(graph: builder.graph, levels: levels, output: output)
    }

    /// The process's peak resident memory so far, in bytes.
    static var peakMemory: Int {
        var usage = rusage()
        getrusage(RUSAGE_SELF, &usage)
        return Int(usage.ru_maxrss)
    }
}
````

**Create `Tests/CreatorAppTests/Bench/ImplicitBenchFixturesTests.swift`**

````swift
import CreatorGraph
import CreatorImplicit
import CreatorKernel
import CreatorNodes
import Foundation
import Testing
@testable import CreatorApp

/// The implicit benchmarks' fixtures, checked in the default run (the benchmarks themselves are skipped): the sheet builds, meshes to a
/// closed surface at a small size, and a thickness edit changes it.
@MainActor
struct ImplicitBenchFixturesTests {
    @Test func theSheetMeshesToAClosedSurface() async throws {
        let sheet = ImplicitBenchFixtures.sheet(side: 20, scale: 8, exportResolution: 0.5)
        let report = try await Evaluator(registry: BuiltInNodes.registry, kernel: FakeKernel())
            .evaluate(sheet.graph, demand: [sheet.output.id], meshQuality: .export)
        guard case .mesh(let payload)? = report.results[sheet.output.id]?.outputs?["mesh"]?.items.first,
              let mesh = payload as? TriangleMesh else {
            Issue.record("no mesh: \(String(describing: report.results[sheet.output.id]?.state))")
            return
        }
        #expect(MeshTopology(mesh).isClosedManifold && mesh.signedVolume > 0)
        // The band between two levels of the noise holds a fraction of the cube's volume, never all of it.
        #expect(mesh.signedVolume > 100 && mesh.signedVolume < 8_000 * 0.5)
    }

    @Test func aThicknessEditMakesAThickerSheetWithoutEvaluatingTheNoiseAgain() async throws {
        let sheet = ImplicitBenchFixtures.sheet(side: 20, scale: 8, exportResolution: 0.5)
        let evaluator = Evaluator(registry: BuiltInNodes.registry, kernel: FakeKernel())
        let first = try await evaluator.evaluate(sheet.graph, demand: [sheet.output.id], meshQuality: .export)
        var edited = sheet.graph
        edited.nodes[sheet.levels.id]?.inputValues["step"] = .number(-0.2)
        let second = try await evaluator.evaluate(edited, demand: [sheet.output.id], meshQuality: .export)
        func volume(_ report: EvaluationReport) -> Double {
            if case .mesh(let payload)? = report.results[sheet.output.id]?.outputs?["mesh"]?.items.first,
               let mesh = payload as? TriangleMesh { mesh.signedVolume } else { 0 }
        }
        #expect(volume(second) > volume(first) * 1.2)
        // The noise and the cube are cached; the levels, the isosurfaces, the cut, the Mesh and the Output ran again.
        #expect(second.evaluatedNodes.count == 5)
    }

    @Test func peakMemoryIsReadable() {
````

@@ -346,6 +346,46 @@ If a later run puts `field-3` over 50 ms, the levers, in the order to pull them
 which loosens the packing a little and changes every scatter, so `FieldScatterNodeTests` and `FieldsAcceptanceTests` re-pin their
 counts), (3) stop and ask. The budget is not edited and a miss is not called met.
 
+## Implicit (implicit spec §6, sub-project 7c-1)
+
+`scripts/bench.sh ImplicitBench` runs rows i-1 to i-4 (i-5 and i-6, the decimation and Mesh to Solid, belong to 7c-3). Release, an idle Mac
+with a GPU. The fixture is a sheet through a 100 mm cube: the band of a 50 mm noise field between two levels (one isosurface cut
+from another), because the lattice nodes (7c-2) do not exist yet; 7c-2 swaps in the gyroid and keeps the rows. A gyroid of 8 mm cells
+has about 150 million triangles at 0.1 mm, which no in-memory mesh holds in 4 GB, so row i-4 is only reachable for a sparse sheet.
+Per the spec's Errata, the budgets of i-1 and i-2 are for the Metal path measured with Metal meshing (D13, plan 7c-1g), and the streamed
+export that i-4 needs (D12) belongs to 7c-3. **Until then these rows measure Metal evaluation with the CPU mesher, and i-2 and i-4 are
+expected to miss;** the CPU engine records its numbers without a budget (D11).
+
+| Row | Case | Target |
+|---|---|---|
+| i-1 | the sheet's first preview pass, on a fresh evaluator with the shaders compiled | under 1 s |
+| i-2 | a thickness edit (the gap between the two levels), warm, no recompile: one coarse pass | under 100 ms |
+| i-3 | To Implicit of the slice's filleted bracket (OCCT), bake cache emptied each time | under 500 ms |
+| i-4 | the sheet exported at 0.1 mm to STL, the whole pipeline | under 60 s and a peak memory under 4 GB |
+
+The first run, taken while other sessions kept the Mac at a load average of about 100 (so none of these numbers is a measurement of the
+code; they show the benchmarks run, and the order of magnitude). **Re-run on an idle Mac and replace them, keeping the load lines.**
+
+```text
+BENCH machine: MacBookPro18,2, 10 cores, macOS 27.0.1
+BENCH load before: 95.05 92.87 104.38
+BENCH i-1 first preview pass of a 100 mm sheet, cold (shaders compiled): median 557.48 ms, p95 557.48 ms, max 557.48 ms (n 1)
+BENCH i-1 first preview pass of a 100 mm sheet: median 366.42 ms, p95 424.50 ms, max 424.50 ms (n 6); budget 1000.00 ms: met
+BENCH i-2 thickness edit of a 100 mm sheet (one coarse pass, no recompile): median 395.50 ms, p95 531.97 ms, max 610.21 ms (n 22); budget 100.00 ms: missed
+BENCH i-3 To Implicit of the bracket (bake, OCCT): median 777.48 ms, p95 906.27 ms, max 906.27 ms (n 10); budget 500.00 ms: missed
+BENCH i-4 export of the 100 mm sheet at 0.1 mm (STL, whole pipeline): median 87940.63 ms, p95 87940.63 ms, max 87940.63 ms (n 1); budget 60000.00 ms: missed
+BENCH i-4 peak memory 4.96 GB (file 1599 MB); budget 4.00 GB: missed
+BENCH load after: 117.08 110.72 110.68
+```
+
+Where the misses are likely to stay on an idle Mac, and who owns them: **i-2** is the first coarse pass again (128 cells across, marching
+cubes and the Newton snap on the CPU), so it is about as long as i-1 and cannot be under 100 ms without a coarser first pass or marching
+cubes on the GPU (D13, plan 7c-1g, which re-measures it); the spec's targets for i-1 and i-2 are not consistent with each other while
+the first pass is the same pass. **i-3** is the tessellation, the BVH and 600 to
+2,000 blocks of samples in parallel on the CPU. **i-4** holds the whole mesh and its vertex map in memory (about 150 bytes a triangle);
+streaming bricks to the STL, which the user decided on (D12) and 7c-3 builds, cuts the memory; the time is the mesher's, so GPU
+meshing (7c-1g) is what moves it.
+
